# Changelog

## 0.41.0 (2026-10-29)

- Adopter gaps, round 41: approval policies by effect, catch-all and platform rows, payload conditions, tenantless requests, every holder of a permission, escalation and service-role deciders; quote working copies, version lines, fixed and pay-first deposits, subscription periods, withdrawals and app portal answers; and actors on block-written status changes.

  - Approvals: floors take `effects` and an `"*"` action; tenant policies match `subjectType: "*"`, `action: "*"` and an `effect`. `options.platformPolicies` keeps runtime-edited platform policies (no organization) that apply everywhere and stand in for a missing floor. `options.conditions: "payload"` matches conditions against the request payload without automations.
  - Approvals: `options.tenantless` lists subject types whose requests may have no organization (`request(..., { tenantless: true })`); `organizationId` is `null` on them.
  - Approvals: `required: "all"` with only `approvers.permission` asks every holder; `escalateAfter` and `escalateTo` with `escalate_approvals()` let fallback approvers decide a step that waited; `approve` and `reject` take `{ decidedBy }` for the service role.
  - Leave and expenses: `decide` and `decideExpense` take `decidedBy`, which the service role passes so the decider is kept.
  - Quotes: `options.workingCopy` edits sent quotes while the customer sees the last published version (`publish` on the same link); `options.versionLines` keeps published lines as rows with ids in `quote_version_lines`, and `selected` takes those ids. `depositAmount`, `depositRequired`, line `periods`, `withdraw`, `decide` with an `actor`, and `portalView` for the service role.
  - State machine: `actor.id` names the user the service role acts for; quotes (`send`, `publish`, `revise`, `withdraw`, `decide`, customer answers) and invoices (`issue`, `recordPayment`) pass their actor to the history.
  - Invoices: `fromQuote` copies a subscription line as quantity times periods.

  Configs that set none of the new options behave as before. `ApprovalRequest.organizationId` and `ApprovalTenantPolicy.organizationId` are typed `string | null`.

## 0.40.0 (2026-10-29)

- Ledger and invoice gaps: organization purges with a ledger, the tenant column of block subjects, payments after a write-off, and actors on ledger and invoice payment reads.

  - Data lifecycle: `purge_organization` now deletes a tenant's ledger entries and transactions. It sets the transaction-local `better_supabase.purge_tenant` setting, and the ledger's append-only guard lets a delete through only for the service role, only for that tenant and only while its deletion is due. Updates, and deletes by anyone else, still fail with `LEDGER_APPEND_ONLY`. Before, purging any organization with a ledger transaction failed.
  - Subjects: the subjects blocks register (`ledger_account`, `work_item`, `product` and the others) name the tenant column `database.sql.tenantColumn` gives, as the block's tables do. Before, they kept `tenant_id`, so comments and other subject checks failed once such a block was installed.
  - Invoices: `options.paymentsAfterWriteOff: true` lets a written-off invoice take payments, such as a provider payment that arrives after the write-off. The invoice stays `uncollectible` while a balance is left and moves to `paid` once nothing is owed. The default still refuses them with `INVOICE_NOT_PAYABLE`.
  - Invoices: `payments(invoiceId)` (`list_invoice_payments`) reads an invoice's payments with `recordedBy`, and needs the read key.
  - Ledger: `entries(accountId)` returns the transaction's `actorId` on each entry.
- The `better-supabase` skill covers 0.36 to 0.39: `codemod 0.37` after upgrading from 0.35, doctor's BS607 version check, `bsb.contextClient(ctx.auth)` for a supabase-js client on the server, writing block tables through the block client with your own columns in `fields` (BS351), and encrypting secret columns (BS352). BS352 now names the `tables.<name>.columns` config key instead of `appTables`, and the migration guides say that 0.38 and 0.39 only add options.

## 0.39.0 (2026-10-29)

- Adopter gaps, round 40: column defaults and unique errors in the catalog, integer rank helpers, team feeds by an employees column, dated leave adjustments, withdrawing approved expenses, and per-year invoice numbering.

  - Catalog: a new product, variant or category takes the column default for a value the call leaves out, instead of an explicit `null`, so `set_product_options` and `createVariant` work over a `title text not null default ''` column.
  - Catalog: `CATALOG_SLUG_TAKEN` and `CATALOG_SKU_TAKEN` are raised only for an index on the slug or SKU column. Any other unique violation, such as an app index on a barcode, is raised unchanged with its constraint name.
  - Catalog: with `options.rankType: "integer"` (now passed to the client through `blockConfig`), `reorderVariant`, `moveCategory` and `addToCollection` take `{ position }` or whole-number neighbors.
  - Leave: `options.feed.teamColumn` shares a team feed by an employees column instead of team members, `feed.teamName` and `feed.organizationName` add the names, and `feed.confidential: "busy"` lists hidden confidential leave under `busy` without the type.
  - Leave: `adjust` and `accrue` take `effectiveOn`.
  - Expenses: `options.withdrawApproved` lets the owner, or `permissions.withdraw`, take an approved expense back to draft, and `permissions.deleteSubmitted` lets its holders delete a submitted or approved expense.
  - Invoices: `options.uniqueNumber` (`"number"`, `"identifier"` or `false`) chooses the unique number index, so numbering that restarts each year works, and `options.linePositionKey: false` leaves out the unique line position index on your own line table.

  Configs that set none of the new options or keys behave as before.

## 0.38.0 (2026-10-29)

- Adopter gaps, round 39: variant status, category keys, expense categories without a key or currency, reapproval of changed leave, and draft invoice edits and deletes.

  - Catalog: `setVariantStatus(variantId, status)` (`set_variant_status`) moves a variant to `active`, `archived` or a label `values.variants.status` names, with the update key. It needs a mapped `columns.variants.status` and refuses without one (`CATALOG_VARIANT_STATUS_UNSUPPORTED`). A variant that goes live again needs a free combination of option values.
  - Catalog: optional `permissions.categoryCreate`, `categoryUpdate` and `categoryDelete` for `save_category`, `move_category` and `delete_category`, each falling back to `manage`.
  - Expenses: the categories table's `key` and `currency` columns may map to `null`. Without a key, a category's key is its id, and the ledger names its account by the id. `options.categoryExtraColumns` adds columns that `saveCategory({ fields })` writes and category reads return under `fields`.
  - Expenses without reports no longer register the ledger's `ledger_account` subject, so comments, messaging, hiring and search render without the ledger tables.
  - Leave: `options.reapprove: true` lets the requester change their own approved leave, which goes back to approval: the taken leave returns to the balance and a new approval opens. `permissions.reapprove` names a key whose holders may change anyone's leave the same way.
  - Invoices: `update(invoiceId, patch)` (`update_invoice`) changes a draft's header and extra columns in one statement with the `update` key, and `delete(invoiceId)` (`delete_invoice`) deletes a draft with its lines and charges, with the new optional `delete` key (falling back to `update`, then `write`). Both refuse an issued invoice and record `invoice.updated` and `invoice.deleted`.

  Configs that set none of the new keys, columns or options behave as before.

## 0.37.0 (2026-10-28)

- Adopter gaps, round 38: one-statement updates, a whole option matrix, finer keys for invoices and expenses, and confidential leave.

  - Updates write the extra columns in the same `update` as the block's own columns: `save_product` (with `kind` and the price settings), `save_variant`, `save_category`, `update_expense`, `update_leave_request` and `save_calendar_event`. A table check across a block column and an extra column, such as `(kind = 'digital') = (delivery is not null)`, now holds when a product changes kind.
  - Catalog: `setOptions(productId, options, { titleSeparator? })` (`set_product_options`) replaces a product's options by name and makes its variants the full matrix in one transaction. Variants of a surviving combination keep their id, duplicates and variants of removed values are deleted or archived, missing combinations get a new variant, and titles become the values joined by `" / "`. `options.maxVariants` (default 100) caps the matrix.
  - Invoices: optional `permissions.create`, `update`, `issue`, `payment`, `credit` and `charge` (falling back to `write`) and `void`, `writeOff` and `reverse` (falling back to `manage`).
  - Expenses without reports: optional `permissions.create`, `update`, `delete` (for someone else's expenses), `reimburse` and `categories`, each falling back to `manage`. `reimburse` also covers `reimburse_expense_report`, and `categories` the category functions with reports.
  - Leave: `columns.types.confidential` maps a boolean column of your own types table. Leave of a confidential type is left out of `list`, `balances`, `ledger`, the feeds and the read policy for anyone but the employee, their manager and holders of `permissions.readConfidential` (`manage` when unset).
  - Leave feed: `options.feed.names` reads first and last name when the employees block uses `name: "split"`, and `options.feed.typeKey` names the types column `hideTypes` matches, such as `category`.

  Configs that set none of the new keys or columns behave as before.
- Inventory counts fractional units with lots, serials, expiry, bins and average cost; orders come from lines or a quote; and shipments carry stock (ADR 0127).

  - `setStockItem(variantId, { unit, scale, tracking, tracksExpiry, costing, currency })` gives a variant a unit with up to 6 decimals, lot or serial tracking and weighted average cost. The ledger posts base units (the quantity times ten to the scale).
  - Every movement takes a `detail` with `binId`, `lotId` or `lot` and `serials`. `saveBin` (with `hold` bins), `saveLot`, `stockDetail`, `expiringLots`, `expireLots` and `stockValuation` read and age the stock. Stock leaves first-expiry-first-out, and sales skip hold bins and expired lots.
  - `allocate`, `fulfillAllocation`, `restoreAllocation`, `releaseAllocations` and `listAllocations` hold stock for a document line in `stock_allocations`, with backorders that fill as stock arrives. Events: `allocation.created`, `allocation.filled`, `allocation.released`.
  - `orders.fromLines(organizationId, input)` and `orders.fromQuote(quoteId)` make a `confirmed` order, once per origin or quote, and allocate its lines. `orderFromQuoteAction` calls `orders.fromQuote` when you pass no `create`.
  - `shipments.setLines(id, lines)` and `shipments.receiveReturn(id, lines)` with the new `shipment_lines` table: the label takes the lines out of inventory, cancelling puts them back, and returns are restocked, held or scrapped (`shipment.return_received`).

  Breaking changes:

  | Before                                                    | After                                                            |
  | --------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | integer stock columns and functions                       | `numeric(20, 6)`; `available` is on hand minus reserved and held |
  | `before_stock_adjust(uuid, uuid, integer, text)`          | `before_stock_adjust(uuid, uuid, numeric, text)`                 |
  | `before_stock_reserve(uuid, uuid, integer, text, text)`   | `before_stock_reserve(uuid, uuid, numeric, text, text)`          |
  | `move_stock(..., quantity integer, ...)`                  | `move_stock(..., quantity numeric, ..., detail jsonb)`           |
  | `OrderStatus` without `confirmed`                         | `confirmed`, the first status of an order from a document        |
  | `orderFromQuoteAction({ create })` with `create` required | `create` optional                                                |
  | `Shipment` without `lines`                                | `Shipment.lines`                                                 |

  Raw SQL reads of the stock columns now return decimal strings such as `"7.000000"`, and stock event payloads carry them the same way (`available`, `reorderPoint`).

  `better-supabase codemod 0.37` lists the stock hooks declared with an `integer` quantity, the order status switches and `orderFromQuoteAction` calls for review.
- Templates take brand starters: with `blocks.templates.options.brand` set to the table and column that hold a tenant's brand, a starter `sync` saves with a `brand` reaches every organization of that brand. `templates.recurring` applies a template at each occurrence of a recurrence rule kept in reminders: `upsert`, `list` and `remove` manage schedules, `recurring.schedule()` (a manifest schedule every five minutes) enqueues one `templates.recur` job per occurrence on the `jobs` client, and `recurring.job` applies it once. A state-machine machine with `workflow: "<slug>"` reads its stages and edges from the published workflow-editor version and pins that version per record. Timelines add `transition` entries from the status history and `message` entries from the record's conversation.

  Breaking: `TemplateScope` adds `"brand"`, `ActivitySource` adds `"transition"` and `"message"`, and `StatusState` adds `workflow` and `workflowVersionId`, so exhaustive switches over them need the new cases. Nothing is renamed. Run `better-supabase upgrade` and `db diff` to add the `brand` column, the schedule tables (with `reminders`) and the lifecycle's `workflow_version_id`.
- `better-supabase codemod 0.37` makes every rewrite from 0.35 to 0.37 and lists the rest for review: the session, cache and block changes of 0.36.0 and the stock and order changes of 0.37.0. The new [From 0.35 to 0.37](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/migration/0.35-to-0.37) guide covers upgrading from 0.35 to 0.36 or 0.37 and says which release brought each change. The 0.34 to 0.35 guide now lists only what 0.35.0 shipped. `codemod 0.35`, which 0.36.0 shipped, keeps its rewrites and review lines.

## 0.36.0 (2026-10-28)

- Leave, expenses, the drive, the catalog, invoices and calendar feeds run over more of an app's own tables.

  - Leave: `options.owner: "employee"` keys requests and balances by an employee of the employees block, so an employee without an account has leave too. The client takes `employeeId` on `request`, `list`, `balances`, `ledger` and `adjust`, and `employeeIds` on `accrue`. Reads return `employeeId`, and `userId` is now optional on requests, balances, ledger entries and feed entries.
  - Leave: mapped `columns.requests.startPart` and `endPart` book half a day at either end of a request (`startPart` and `endPart` on `request`), combined with hours for an hours type, and overlaps are checked by half day.
  - Leave: `tables.balances` maps the app's table of signed amounts (with an optional `expiresOn`) instead of the ledger block. The block sums it, adds taken and adjustment rows, and deletes the taken row when approved leave is cancelled. `adjust` takes `expiresOn`.
  - Expenses: mapping `tables.reports` and `tables.lines` to `null` keeps each expense as one row of `tables.expenses`, without the approvals block or the ledger. The client adds `createExpense`, `updateExpense`, `submitExpense`, `decideExpense`, `reimburseExpense`, `getExpense` and `listExpenses`, and `options.owner: "employee"` keys expenses by employee. Report mode is unchanged.
  - Drive: node kind and source, node and drive grant roles, drive kind, and connection provider and status may be enums in the app's tables.
  - `add --adopt` and `apply --adopt` render the proposal with the authorization provider, so calendar `accessFunctions` in the `{ rows, key }` form and drive `roleFunctions` in the `{ rows, roles }` form no longer fail with the `rowsWith` error.
  - Catalog: `updateProduct` with `price: null` clears the price (`save_product` takes `clear`). `setProductStatus` writes the canonical app value of a label even when another mapped value reads as it, accepts an app value itself, and moves an archived product back to `active` when there is no draft status. A product with variants can gain an option, each variant taking its first value. `updateVariant` keeps what it isn't given, and `sku` is optional. `permissions.create`, `update` and `delete` name separate keys for products, options and variants.
  - Invoices: `issue(id, issuedOn, { dueOn })` sets the due date, and `termsDays: null` leaves none. `removeCharge(chargeId)` removes a charge. `writeOff(id, reason)` keeps the reason in a mapped `writeOffReason` column. `reversePayment(paymentId, reason)` posts the opposite ledger transaction for a payment recorded by hand and refuses one whose method is in `options.providerPaymentMethods` (default `stripe` and `order`). A credit note is now capped at the invoice's total and charges less earlier credits instead of the open balance, so a paid invoice can be credited and refunded.
  - Calendar: `calendar_feed` returns the link's creator as `createdBy`, and the feed writes the creator's reminders as VALARMs.
- Calendar feed data, extra columns in inserts, catalog patches and option removal, payment notes, leave edits and team feeds, and more of standalone expenses.

  - Calendar: `feedData(token)` and `userFeedData(token)` return the parsed feed (the calendar with its colour, the organization with the organizations block, the link's creator, and the events with reminders, attendees and extra columns) for an app that writes the iCalendar itself. `feed` and `userFeed` serialize the same data with `toIcs`. `calendar_feed` and `calendar_user_feed` return `color` and `organization`.
  - Extra columns are written in the insert itself instead of an update after it, so a `not null` column without a default or a check across columns holds from the first write: catalog products, standalone expenses, expense reports, leave requests, calendar events, time entries, invoices, quotes and flags.
  - Catalog: `updateProduct` and `updateVariant` patch. A key left out keeps its value; `null` clears a product's `description`, derives its `slug` from the title again, and clears a variant's `sku`, `title`, `barcode` or `weightGrams`. `save_product` always takes `clear`, and `save_variant` takes `clear` before `fields`, so a positional `fields` argument moves one place. `reorderOptions(productId, optionIds)` orders a product's options. A value `saveOption` leaves out is removed with the variants that use it; a variant still referenced (a foreign key, stock movements or `options.variantInUse`) is archived through a mapped `columns.variants.status` instead. Removing them needs the update permission.
  - Invoices: `recordPayment` takes a `note`, and each payment returns `note` and `recordedBy`. `record_invoice_payment` takes `note` as its last argument.
  - Leave: `update(requestId, change)` changes a pending request (which goes back to approval) or, for a manager, approved leave, which is booked again. `split(requestId, { gapStarts, gapEnds })` takes a gap out of approved leave. A type with `autoApprove` (the block's table, or a mapped `columns.types.autoApprove`) is approved when it is requested; `saveType` takes `autoApprove`. `options.feed` takes `teams` (a `leave_team_feed` link per team), `names`, `hideTypes` and `fields`, and feed entries carry `startPart` and `endPart`. New events `leave.updated` and `leave.split`.
  - Expenses without reports: `updateExpense` takes `fields` and clears `categoryId`, `description` or `merchant` with `null`. `withdrawExpense` moves a submitted expense back to draft and `deleteExpense` deletes a draft. `options.companyExpenses` lets `createExpense({ company: true })` record an expense without a member or employee. New events `expense.withdrawn` and `expense.deleted`.
- The `ai-chat` block runs without a tenant, shares chats with participants, can store a linear list of native AI SDK parts keyed by uuid, and takes extra columns on every table (ADR 0122). `better-supabase upgrade` moves the module to version 5.

  - `blocks["ai-chat"].options.tenant: false` lets chats and projects live outside a tenant: `chats.create(null, input)` and `projects.create(null, input)`. Such a chat stays private to its owner and its participants, and the AI SDK adapter skips the quota and the `org:` gateway tag for it.
  - `participants.add(chatId, userId, { role })`, `remove` and `list` share a chat with viewers and editors, stored in the new `ai_chat_participants` table. In a tenant chat a participant must be able to read the tenant's chats and counts only while a member. `chats.list` returns the chats the caller owns or takes part in, and user messages record their writer (`authorId`).
  - `blocks["ai-chat"].options.messages: { key: "uuid", tree: false, parts: "native" }` keys messages by uuid, keeps one list per chat instead of a tree, and stores the SDK's own parts. Choose it before installing; a uuid key needs uuid message ids (`AI_MESSAGE_INVALID` otherwise), and `switchBranch` on a linear chat fails with `AI_CHAT_LINEAR`.
  - Every ai-chat table takes extra columns: `projectExtraColumns`, `participantExtraColumns`, `messageExtraColumns`, `moderationExtraColumns` and one key per other table. `appendUser`, `saveAssistant`, `projects.create`, `projects.update`, `participants.add` and `moderation.record` take `fields` for them.

  Breaking changes:

  | 0.34                                                              | 0.35                                                |
  | ----------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
  | `organizationId: string` on chats, projects and moderation events | `organizationId: string \| undefined`               |
  | `AssistantContext.organizationId: string`                         | `AssistantContext.organizationId: string \| null`   |
  | `chats.create(organizationId: string, ...)`                       | `chats.create(organizationId: string \| null, ...)` |
  | `projects.list(organizationId: string)`                           | `projects.list(organizationId: string \| null)`     |
  | private chats: readable by the owner only                         | readable by the owner and the chat's participants   |
- Approvals: tenant policies, confirm or authorize modes, manager approvers and single-use approvals.

  - `blocks.approvals.options.tenantPolicies: true` adds an `approval_policies` table per organization. A tenant policy tightens the configured policy for the same subject type and action: its steps run after the configured ones, the stricter mode wins, `selfApproval` holds only when every policy allows it and the shortest expiry wins. `conditions` is a rules filter tree over the subject type's rule fields, matched against the request payload (needs the automations block). The client adds `listPolicies`, `savePolicy` and `removePolicy`, and the module records `approval_policy.saved` and `approval_policy.removed`.
  - A configured policy with `steps: []` asks for nothing until a tenant policy adds a step or a mode; it needs `tenantPolicies`.
  - A policy's `mode` is `"authorize"` (the default) or `"confirm"`, where the requester may approve their own request and that approval finishes it. Requests return `mode`.
  - `steps[].approvers.manager: true` lets the requester's manager from the employees block decide the step.
  - `requirement(subjectType, action, payload)` returns what a request would need, or `null` when nothing is required; `request` then fails with `APPROVAL_NOT_REQUIRED`.
  - `request(subject, { fingerprint })` binds the approval to an operation, and `consume(id, fingerprint)` uses an approved request once (`APPROVAL_CONSUMED`, `APPROVAL_NOT_APPROVED`, `APPROVAL_FINGERPRINT_MISMATCH`), recording `approval.consumed`. `approvalFingerprint(value)` builds the fingerprint as the SHA-256 of sorted-key JSON. Requests return `fingerprint` and `consumedAt`.
  - The SQL `request_approval` takes a trailing `fingerprint` argument, so calls with positional arguments keep working. In managed mode, run `better-supabase upgrade` and generate a migration to add the `fingerprint` and `consumed_at` columns; over your own table, map `fingerprint` and `consumedAt` to use fingerprints and `consume`.
- The proxy and every adapter's middleware refresh sessions through `createServerClient` from `@supabase/ssr`, on every request they match, prefetches included. Parallel requests with the same refresh token share one call to Auth. Run `better-supabase codemod 0.35` to rewrite the options and list the rest for review.

  Breaking changes:

  - `refresh: "navigation"` is gone. Refresh is `true` by default, or a function of the request. `verifySession: "navigation"` is now `verifySession: "document"`, and checks document loads only (`isDocumentLoad` from `better-supabase/server`).
  - `onExpiredPrefetch` is removed from `bsb.proxy()`: a prefetch now refreshes, so it never reaches `protect` with an expired session.
  - `shouldRefresh` is removed from `better-supabase/next` and `better-supabase/server`. `shouldCheckSession` stays in `better-supabase/next` only.
  - `clearSessionCookies` returns a promise. Await it before returning the response.
  - The cookie writers are removed from `better-supabase/ssr` and `better-supabase/server`: `writeSession`, `refreshSession`, `pruneSessionChunks`, `sessionEncoding`, `clearSessionAtScopes`, `AUTH_CACHE_HEADERS` and their types. `@supabase/ssr` writes the cookies and their cache headers; `readSession` and `parseCookies` stay.

  Other changes:

  - A token whose `iss` names another project is rejected with `INVALID_ISSUER`. Set `auth.issuer` to enforce an exact issuer, or `false` to skip the check.
  - The JWKS is fetched with `auth.fetch` and cached once per process, so every module graph (proxy, render, route handlers) shares one copy.
  - When Auth can't be reached, a refresh fails within `timeoutMs` without auth-js's retries and keeps the cookies as they are.
  - A response that `after` returns in `bsb.proxy()` keeps the refreshed session cookies and cache headers.
- Open pages pick up a role or grant change without waiting for the token to rotate. Run `better-supabase codemod 0.35` to rename the `SessionProvider` prop and list the lines to review.

  - `blocks.sessions.options.authzVersions` adds `better_supabase.user_authz_versions`, the `bump_authz_version()` trigger function (it reads the user column from its argument, default `user_id`), `bump_authz_versions(uuid[])`, `authz_version(uid)` for the access token hook, and a broadcast on `AUTHZ_TOPIC` for servers and on the private `authz:<uid>` topic, which only that user may read.
  - `SessionProvider` holds the session in a store. With `client` (a browser Supabase client) it listens on `authz:<uid>`: the visible tab refreshes the token and calls `onAuthzChange`, then tells the other tabs over the `better-supabase:session` `BroadcastChannel`, and they call `onAuthzChange` without a second refresh.
  - `bsb.refreshSessionCache()` is a Server Action that drops the caller's own `bsb.cached()` entries; re-export it from a `"use server"` file and call it from `onAuthzChange`.
  - `AuthorizationHooks.sessionTags(userId)` adds a provider's own cache tags to `bsb.cached()` entries, and `evictSession`, sign-out and `refreshSessionCache` clear them. The hook is optional, so `apiVersion: 1` stays.
  - `subscribeAuthzVersions` defaults `topic`, `event` and `userIdKey` to what `authzVersions` sends, and a per-user `authz:<uid>` topic evicts that user. `AUTHZ_TOPIC`, `AUTHZ_EVENT` and `authzUserTopic` are exported from `better-supabase/server`.

  Breaking changes:

  - `SessionProvider` takes `session` instead of `sessionPromise`. It accepts a resolved `AuthSession` or a promise.
  - `sessionStatusRoute` defaults to `verifySession: false`: it reads the verified claims only. Pass `verifySession: true` to ask Auth on every check.
- Status history records who made a change, memory scopes to records and expires, knowledge gets collections and system documents, the inbox keeps reply drafts, and workflows take the app's own node kinds and a default lifecycle workflow (ADRs 0123 to 0126).

  - State machine: `transition(subject, to, { reason, actor })` records the actor's `name`, `email`, `ip` and `userAgent` in `status_history` (`actor_name`, `actor_email`, `ip`, `user_agent`), and `history()` returns them. An app history table maps them with `history.actorName`, `actorEmail`, `ip` and `userAgent`. `history: "app"` leaves the history to the app: the block checks edges and records `status.changed` but writes no history, and `history()` fails with `STATE_HISTORY_APP`.
  - Memory: `{ scope: "record", subject }` keeps facts about one record that every member reads and admins change. `archival.upsert` and `saveExtracted` take `category`, `confidence` (weighs search scores) and `expiresAt`. Searches count `useCount` and `lastUsedAt`. `archival.restore(id)` undoes a forget, and `archival.purge({ retentionDays, batch })` (service role) removes deleted and expired facts.
  - Knowledge: `knowledge.collections` creates collections with a visibility (`automatic`, `restricted`, `disabled`) and a priority, and grants restricted ones to users or teams. Documents take `collectionId`; `documents.list` and `search` filter by collection, and search ranks higher priority collections first. `documents.create(null, ...)` and `ingest.text(null, ...)` (service role) create system documents every organization reads.
  - Inbox: `inbox.drafts` keeps a staff member's reply draft per conversation (`upsert`, `list`, `send`, `discard`), and `suggest` (service role) files an AI draft every staff member who can reply sees.
  - Workflow editor: `defineNodeKind` defines the app's own node kinds (`apiVersion: 1`), `createWorkflowEditor({ nodes })` and `nodeKinds.sync()` register them in `workflow_node_kinds`, and `nodeKinds.list()` returns them. `executeGraph` runs custom nodes through `runtime.node`, and `graphStarter({ nodeKinds, nodes })` binds one step per kind. Definitions take a `purpose` (`automation` or `lifecycle`) and `isDefault`; `definitions.setPurpose` sets them and `definitions.resolve(tenant, { subjectType })` returns the default lifecycle workflow's published version.

  Run `better-supabase upgrade` and generate a migration: state-machine moves to version 3, memory to 3, knowledge to 3, inbox to 6 and workflow-editor to 4. `better-supabase codemod 0.35` rewrites the call sites marked below.

  Breaking changes:

  - `transition(subject, to, reason)` takes `{ reason, actor }` as its third argument; the codemod rewrites a string literal reason and lists the rest. The SQL `transition` takes a trailing `actor jsonb`.
  - `memory.archival.forget` soft deletes: the fact leaves lists and searches but stays until `purge` removes it. Schedule `purge`.
  - `KnowledgeDocument.organizationId` and `ownerId` are `string | undefined`, undefined for a system document.
  - `validateGraph(graph, steps)` is `validateGraph(graph, { steps, nodes })`; the codemod rewrites it. `WorkflowNodeKind` accepts any kind name, so a map over every kind keys by the new `WorkflowBuiltinNodeKind` and falls back for custom kinds. A graph with a kind that is neither built in nor registered fails `validate_workflow_graph`.
  - `WorkflowDefinition` has `purpose` and `isDefault`.
- Calendar events keep their alarms, and reminders work on any subject.

  - Calendar: a new `calendar_event_alarms` table holds each event's `VALARM`s in the feed's reminder shape (a `method`, an `offsetMinutes` before the event or an absolute `at`, a repeat, and an optional `user` for a member's own alarm). `upsert` takes `alarms`, reads return them, `importIcs` reads them and `exportIcs` and the feeds write them. Map `tables.alarms` to your own table or set it to `null`.
  - Calendar: `options.feed.reminders` is replaced by `tables.alarms`. Map your alarm table there, with its columns under `columns.alarms`; the table needs a tenant column. The feed's `reminders` now carry the event's alarms and the link creator's reminders together; `CalendarAlarm` extends `CalendarReminder`.
  - Reminders: `upsert(subject, { at })` sets a one-time reminder (a new `remind_at` column), and `on.<type>.at` names a subject's time column, such as a task's `due_at`, for subjects without a recurrence rule.
  - Reminders: `schedule()` enqueues one `reminders.deliver` job per occurrence due in the next day on the new `jobs` option, once per reminder and occurrence. The block declares it as the `reminders.schedule` timer, and `reminderJobSchema` types the delivery queue.
  - `save_reminder` now refuses a subject of a registered type that doesn't exist in the organization or that the caller can't read (`REMINDER_SUBJECT_NOT_FOUND`), and with `on` set, a type `on` doesn't list (`REMINDER_SUBJECT_UNSUPPORTED`). Run `better-supabase upgrade` and generate a migration.
- The server builds its Supabase clients with `createContextClient` and `createAdminClient` from `@supabase/server`, and keeps one memo of verified tokens. A user's client sends the token to PostgREST, Storage and Realtime (through `realtime.accessToken`). The secret-key client is created on first use and shared. Run `better-supabase codemod 0.35` to rename the calls and list the rest for review.

  Breaking changes:

  | 0.34                                     | 0.35                                                   |
  | ---------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------ |
  | `bsb.supabaseFor(auth, headers?)`        | `bsb.contextClient(auth, headers?)`                    |
  | `sessions: false` skips the session memo | `sessions: false` verifies every token on every call   |
  | `accessTokenResolver` remembers tokens   | it verifies each request; put it behind `createServer` |

  - The 256-entry verified-token memos inside `resolveAuth` and `accessTokenResolver` are removed. `createServer` passes its session memo to the resolver, so the proxy, `bsb.context()`, `getClaims()` and `bsb.snapshot()` verify a token once while it is valid, and `bsb.sessions.evict(userId)` reaches every path.

  Other changes:

  - The memo is keyed by the token instead of its SHA-256, which keeps a hit at about 14 us under load.
  - Loaders of one request share one `bsb.snapshot(request)` resolution.
- Doctor adds three checks.

  - BS351 warns when app code writes a block's table directly with `.from("x").insert()`, `.update()`, `.upsert()` or `.delete()`, which skips the block's functions. Write through the API `createBlocks` returns.
  - BS352 warns when one of your tables keeps a plain `credentials`, `token` or `secret` column while `field-encryption` or the `credentials` module is installed. Mark the column `encrypted: true`, or store it in Vault and keep a `credential_ref`.
  - BS607 warns when the installed `better-supabase` is not the version your lockfile resolves, or when a block file or a `better_supabase.blocks` row records a block version newer than the installed release ships.
- Enrollment program steps take an `assignee` (`{ role }` or `{ userId }`) and a `due` date (`{ days, from: "start" | "end" }`). Enrollments gain `startsAt` and `endsAt`, set through `enroll(program, subject, dates)` or the new `setDates`, and each scheduled step gets an open `enrollment_steps` row with its rendered `due_at`. `openSteps()` lists the open steps by due date, an assigned user can complete their step, and an enrollment's `schedule` lists its scheduled steps. Run `better-supabase upgrade` and `db diff` to add the columns; rows in `enrollment_steps` with a null `completed_at` are open steps, so filter on `completed_at` in your own queries over the table.
- `toNextRoute(entries, handler)` and `toNextAction(entries, action)` from `better-supabase/next` run `@supabase/middleware` entries (`withRequiredClaims`, `withSupabaseClient`, `withCors`) around a Next.js route handler or Server Action. A route handler returns a `Response` or a value and gets the route `params`. An action reads the request from `headers()`, writes the entries' cookies through `cookies().set`, and throws `NextActionRejected` when an entry answers first.
- Pages can read the session, the authorization version and their data in one database call, and keep it in the App Shell. Run `better-supabase codemod 0.35` to list the lines to review.

  - `bsb.cached()` sets `stale` to 300 seconds, so private entries ride in the App Shell. The token's lifetime no longer caps it, since the proxy refreshes every request; `life: { capToToken: true }` brings the cap back. In development a `stale` below 300 seconds warns once.
  - `bsb.remote(fn, { share, tenant })` and `bsb.cached({ scope: "remote", key })` read user-scoped data in `"use cache: remote"`. The key carries the user, Auth session, tenant and authorization version, never the token. Support sessions get no remote token. Remote entries default to `revalidate` 300 and `expire` 3600.
  - `bsb.bootstrap({ rpc, args, tenant, redirect })` calls the `better_supabase.bootstrap_<rpc>` wrapper that `blocks.sessions.options.bootstrap` generates. It checks `session_active()`, returns the token's `authz_ver` and the app function's data, seeds the authorization version cache, and calls once per request for the same arguments. An ended session is not cached, its tags are evicted, and with `redirect` the caller goes to `?reason=session_not_found`.
  - `bsb.bootstrap()` calls the wrappers where the sessions block puts them: `gen` records its API schema (`blocks.sessions.api`, then `sql.api`) or its own `schema` in the schema metadata, and `bootstrapSchema` or the call's `schema` still override it.
  - `proxy({ verifySession: "bootstrap" })` skips the proxy's Auth call for apps whose pages check the session in `bsb.bootstrap()`.
  - `bsb.getAccess(source?)` returns the caller's context, session, tenant, authorization version and `can(permission)` from the request, a `SessionSnapshot` or a bootstrap result.
  - `createNext({ rootParams: { locale, brand, tenant } })` adds `bsb.locale()`, `bsb.brand()` and `bsb.tenant()`, and contexts fall back to the `tenant` root param.
  - `postAuthPath(source, fallback)` reads a safe `?next=` path from a request, search params or page props.
  - `AuthzVersionCache` gains an optional `seed(userId, version)`.
  - Doctor BS608 warns when a remote cache scope runs outside Vercel without `cacheHandlers.remote`.

  Breaking changes:

  - `sessionStale` and `SessionStaleOptions` are removed from `better-supabase/next`. Call `bsb.cached()` in the scope instead of `cacheLife({ stale: sessionStale(session) })`.
  - `life.min` and `life.max` are removed from `bsb.cached()`. Use `life.stale` and `capToToken`.
  - `bsb.contextFromSession(session, { token })` is removed. Pass the `SessionSnapshot` from `bsb.getClaims()`; the context is built without verifying the token again.
- Quotes publish versions and price like European offers: VAT-inclusive prices, document discounts, deposits, sections and optional lines, and tax per rate (ADR 0120).

  - Each `send` publishes the quote to `quote_versions`, a snapshot that never changes. `versions(quoteId)` lists them and `version(quoteId, revision)` returns the quote as it was sent.
  - `pricesIncludeTax`, `discountBps`, `discountAmount` and `depositBps` on `create` and `update`. The database spreads the document discount over the tax rates and writes `quote.taxLines` (net, tax, gross and discount per rate), which add up exactly to the totals. `quote.pricing.depositDue` is the deposit asked.
  - Lines take a `section` and can be `optional`. The customer picks optional lines when they accept (`selected` on `respond`, `decide` and `portalRespond`).
  - `portalView` and `portalRespond` let a signed-in guest read and answer a quote shared with them, with no token. Answers record the user id, IP address and user agent (`decidedUserId`, `decidedIp`, `decidedUserAgent`).
  - Invoices take `pricesIncludeTax`, and `fromQuote` invoices the lines the customer accepted with one `Discount` line per tax rate, so the invoice totals match the quote.

  Breaking changes:

  - `revise` keeps the same quote at the next revision instead of creating a new one, and refuses an accepted quote (`QUOTE_ACCEPTED`). `Quote.supersedesId`, the `supersedes_id` column and `QUOTE_ALREADY_REVISED` are removed; the migration drops the column.
  - The `quote.revised` event carries `revision` instead of `revisionId`; `quote.sent` and `quote.accepted` carry `revision` too.
  - `PublicQuote` returns `totals`, `pricing` and `taxLines` instead of top-level `subtotal`, `discountTotal`, `taxTotal` and `total`. `respond` resolves to `{ status, identifier, revision }`.
  - `decide_quote` and `respond_to_quote` take a trailing `selected jsonb` argument; SQL callers that named the old signatures must use the new ones.
- The `better-supabase-auth` skill covers the 0.35 session APIs: `bsb.bootstrap()`, `bsb.getAccess()`, the remote scope, the 300-second stale time of `bsb.cached()`, the `SessionProvider` `session` prop and `authzVersions` for open pages.
- `dev stack prune` passes `--yes` to `supabase stack destroy` once it has the user's consent, so the destroy no longer fails with `ExperimentalStackDestroyError`. A `--confirm` token now ignores the presentation flags `--yes`, `--json`, `--color`, `--debug` and `--agent`, so adding one of them to the run the user agreed to keeps the token valid.

## 0.35.0 (2026-10-28)

- Blocks run over more of an app's own tables.

  - `upgrade`, `sync` and doctor render adopted tables with the authorization provider, so calendar `accessFunctions` in the `{ rows, key }` form and drive `roleFunctions` in the `{ rows, roles }` form no longer fail with "needs an authorization provider with functions.rowsWith".
  - Drive: a node under a held folder counts as held, so it can't be trashed, deleted or pruned by a sync. With `roleFunctions.node`, a new top-level node no longer gets an owner grant for its creator.
  - Time tracking: a submitted or approved week refuses only changes to logged time (start, end or duration, subject, owner and rate). The billable flag, the note and app columns stay writable, and `options.lockedColumns` lists other columns.
  - Invoices: `options.chargeExtraColumns` adds app columns to charges, set through `charge(id, { fields })`. `options.lineRates: "percent"` keeps line discount and tax rates as percentages in the app's line table (invoices, quotes and deals).
  - Catalog: `save_product` writes the product's own `price`, `currency` and `trackInventory` without a prices table. `options.variantExtraColumns` and `options.categoryExtraColumns` let `saveVariant` and `saveCategory` write app columns from `fields`.
  - Leave: `columns.types.key` and `columns.requests.partial` can map to `null`.

## 0.34.0 (2026-10-28)

- Drive access runs over an app's own tables and authorization rules.

  - `options.roleFunctions.node` and `options.roleFunctions.drive` name schema-qualified functions that return the caller's role on a node or a drive, replacing the block's own grants at that level. `options.readPolicy: false` leaves the read policies to the app.
  - Drive grants accept `user` and `team` columns as well as `principalType` and `principalId`, and node grants accept user and team columns too. Managed node and drive grant tables can be created with user and team columns.
  - A mapped node drive column stays in use when `options.drives` is off, so an adopted table keeps its drive relationship while the block's drive features stay off.
- Calendar and drive access can come from the authorization provider's per-row grants, without a hand-written function.

  - `blocks.calendar.options.accessFunctions` entries of the form `{ rows, key }` check the signed-in caller through the provider's `rowsWith` template, with their own claims, and anyone else, such as the creator of a share link fetched with nobody signed in, through `rowsWithFor`.
  - `blocks.drive.options.roleFunctions.node` and `.drive` take `{ rows, roles }`: the provider's resource and the permission key for each role. The caller gets the highest role whose key they hold.
  - `calendar_access` and `calendar_event_access` answer for the caller only and return false for another user unless the service role asks. The calendar feeds check the link's creator through the new `calendar_access_for` and `calendar_event_access_for`, which no client role can execute.
- Update Ultracite to 8.1.0 and extend its Playwright and React Native Oxlint presets in the Next.js and Expo examples. Repo tooling only, so no package release.

## 0.33.0 (2026-10-27)

- Resume an `approval-required` decision. `approval: { header: "x-approval-id" }` on the server options names the header the client sends the approval id in; it reaches `ctx.approval` and the authorizer's `context.approval`, never the subject. MCP reads it from the header or `params._meta.approval`, returns the id in `structuredContent.approval`, and keeps tools that need an approval in `tools/list`.
- Add `blockTables(config)` to `better-supabase/sql`. It returns the memberships, membership roles and organizations tables with their configured schema, table and column names, plus every installed block's tables, so an authorization library's SQL follows the app's renames.
- Add `better-supabase/claims`. It exports the claim names (`PERMISSIONS_CLAIM`, `MEMBERSHIPS_CLAIM`, `FEATURES_CLAIM`, `AUTHZ_VERSION_CLAIM`, `PERMISSION_CAP_CLAIM`, `PERMISSIONS_TRUNCATED_CLAIM`), `permissionsOf`, `isTruncated`, `tenantOf`, `decodeEntitlementClaim`, `permissionCapOf` and the `isUserSession`, `isApiKeySession` and `isSessionSnapshot` guards, so client code and authorization adapters read the token without importing the server.
- Each codemod publishes its renames as JSON: `better-supabase/codemods/0.32.json` lists the 0.32 imports, members, config keys and option keys, each mapped from the old name to the new one, for tools that check or apply them.
- Add `better-supabase/contract`, a types-only entry that re-exports every type an authorization library implements or reads (the provider, token hook, authorizer, request, hooks, bucket and topic policies, credential provider, session snapshot and block tables) with `ContractApiVersion`.
- Doctor BS407 reports a custom hook that calls `better_supabase.permission_claims` when the authorization provider owns the `permissions` claim. The new BS419 warns when app code calls `permissionsAuthorizer` while some provider keys lack `sqlComplete`, since the token leaves those keys out.
- The `permissions` claim carries only the keys the authorization provider marks `sqlComplete: true`: `better_supabase.member_permissions` and `permission_claims` drop the others, because a key in the token is a grant in the whole tenant. A provider whose `tokenHook.ownedClaims` lists `permissions` gets no permissions lines in the generated hook template, and `tokenHook.truncatedClaim` names the hook's truncated marker for `permissionsOf` and `permissionsAuthorizer`. Run `better-supabase gen` to regenerate the functions.
- Authorizers get more context on each request. `context` gains `intent` (`read` or `write`), `operation` (`get`, `list`, `create`, `update`, `delete`, `action`, `route` or `tool`), `requestId` and `approval`, typed as `AuthorizationContext`. `resource.source` says whether the properties were `loaded` from the database or are caller `input`, and writes carry `resource.change` with the stored row (`current`) and the new values (`next`). `resource.properties` is unchanged. Resources pass the request scope's memo as `runtime.memo`.
- `better-supabase/testing` adds `authorizationRequest` and `sessionSnapshot`, which build the request and snapshot shapes the server builds. `testAuthorizer` also checks write requests with `source: "input"` and a `change`. `testAuthorizationProvider` checks that the token hook names one truncated marker and that a hook writing `permissions` has `sqlComplete` keys.
- `versions` is typed through `createServer`, `createNext`, `createHono`, `createEdge` and `createMcp`, so `AuthorizerRuntime<V>` and `bsb.versions` are the authorization library's own cache type instead of `AuthzVersionCache`. Edge and MCP decisions now get the server's `versions` too.

## 0.32.0 (2026-10-27)

- Invoices, catalog, leave, expenses and time tracking run over more of an app's own tables, and `createNext().signUp` tells the app about an existing account.

  - Invoices: `options.totals: "app"` leaves the totals to the app: no totals trigger and no recount, and the block reads `subtotal`, `taxTotal` and `total` as the app keeps them (`listTotal` and `discountTotal` can map to `null`). A company, contact or origin can be one foreign key column: map its type column to `null`, and the id takes the fixed type `options.company`, `options.contact` or `quote`, cast to the column's type. A new `invoice_charges` table (`tables.charges`, which can be the app's table or `null`) holds charges on top of the total, such as collection costs; `charge(invoiceId, { amount, description })` adds one, the balance and aging include them, and `invoice.charged` is recorded. An invoice's number takes the period of its issue date, so a back-dated invoice is numbered in its own year's sequence.
  - Catalog: `options.rankType: "integer"` orders options, option values, variants, categories and collection products by whole-number positions. With `tables.prices: null`, a variant's price is `columns.variants.price`, falling back to `columns.products.price`, in `columns.products.currency`. `columns.variants.trackInventory` can be `null` with the flag on the product, which inventory and orders then read.
  - Leave: `options.approvals: false` (or no approval column) runs leave without the approvals block; `decide(requestId, { approved, note })` approves or rejects a request, and a rejection records `leave.rejected`. With share-links, a `leave_feed` link to the organization serves its approved leave through `feed(token)` while the link's creator can still read everyone's leave.
  - Expenses: `options.approvals: false` (or no approval column) runs expenses without the approvals block; `decide(reportId, { approved, note })` approves or rejects a submitted report, and a rejection records `expense_report.rejected`. `ExpenseReportSubmittedEventData.approvalId` is `string | null`.
  - Next.js: `signUp` returns `{ existingAccount: true }` (type `ExistingAccount`) when Auth answers with the stand-in user it gives for an email that already has an account, so the app can send its own email. Nothing about it reaches an error message.
  - Time tracking: `options.overlap: "allow"` lets a member's entries overlap, and `options.timers: "many"` runs several timers at once (`stop_timer` takes an entry, `running_timers` and the client's `runningAll()` list them). Over a duration column, stopping adds the running segment and `resume_timer` (`resume(entryId)`) runs a stopped entry again. A rate needs a currency, but a currency without a rate is allowed (`currency` on the client); the managed table's `rate_pair_check` becomes `rate_needs_currency_check`. `stop_timer` takes an optional entry id.
  - Calendar: an organization purge deletes calendar events itself, so an app's restricting foreign key from events to calendars no longer blocks it.
  - Drive: `options.drives: true` adds several drives per organization, drive-level grants, connected drives and mirror nodes. A drive has a kind and an owner, `drives()` lists them with the caller's role and read-only state, `createDrive`, `renameDrive`, `deleteDrive`, `shareDrive` and `unshareDrive` manage them, and `connections()` lists the connected drives. `drive_sync_batch` mirrors a provider tree into a connected drive as service role: it upserts by external id, links parents, and prunes nodes missing from the run. The tree functions take an optional drive, and roots and sibling names are scoped per drive. `drive.connection_created`, `drive.connection_removed`, `drive.created`, `drive.renamed`, `drive.deleted`, `drive.shared` and `drive.unshared` are recorded.
  - Drive legal holds: a `held` subject column, or `options.drives` with `legalHoldUntil`, marks a node as held, and `drive_node_held` reports a node when it or a descendant is held. `holdNode` and `releaseHold` set and clear a hold, a held subtree refuses `drive_delete` and `drive_trash` with `DRIVE_HELD`, and mirror nodes are read-only. `drive.node_hold_placed` and `drive.node_hold_released` are recorded.
  - Trash: version 3 refuses to purge a held batch with `TRASH_HELD`, and `purge_expired_trash` skips held batches instead of failing the whole run.
  - Tasks: `set_work_item_assignees` declares its notification variables only when it notifies, which clears the plpgsql_check warnings with `options.notify: false`.
- The AI SDK integration follows `ai` 7. `createAssistant` reads `stream`, `usage` and `finalStep` from what `run` returns and builds the UI message stream with the standalone `toUIMessageStream`; a result with only the 0.31 members (`toUIMessageStream()`, `totalUsage`, `providerMetadata`) still works until 0.33, and a result without a stream answers `500 AI_CHAT_RUN_FAILED`. `usageOf` takes `usage` and `finalStep` (the old names still work), and the agent runtime stops on `isStepCount`. `validUIMessages` checks a stored history against the app's tools and schemas through `safeValidateUIMessages`. `connectTools` takes `redirect` and `protocolVersionDiscovery`, and `connectAll` sets them per server through `serverOptions`. `aiBatches` gains `list()` over the provider's batches.

  | 0.31                                        | 0.32                            |
  | ------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------- |
  | `usageOf({ totalUsage, providerMetadata })` | `usageOf({ usage, finalStep })` |
  | `AssistantRunResult.toUIMessageStream()`    | `AssistantRunResult.stream`     |
- The AI SDK integration covers more of the AI SDK UI.

  - `createAssistant` takes `stream` (the `toUIMessageStream` options, such as
    `assistantMetadata`, `sendReasoning` and `sendSources`) and `validate`, which
    checks the user's message with `safeValidateUIMessages` and answers
    `422 AI_MESSAGE_INVALID`.
  - `aiErrorToDbError` maps AI SDK and AI Gateway errors to `DbError`s with a fixed
    message per kind. A provider error when the answer starts now answers with its
    own status (a provider 429 answers 429); `aiErrorMessage` is the default
    `onError` for stream errors, so the provider's message no longer reaches the client.
  - `createCompletionRoute` and `createObjectRoute` serve `useCompletion` and
    `useObject` with the quota check, gateway tags and usage in `onFinish`;
    `useAssistantCompletion` and `useAssistantObject` are the client hooks.
  - `useAssistant` takes a message type and passes `onToolCall`, `onData`,
    `messageMetadataSchema` and `dataPartSchemas` to `useChat`; `assistantTransport`
    is exported, and the fields of `AssistantTransportConfig` other than `api` are optional.
- Chat SDK: `stateScheduler` and `scheduledAdapter` give any adapter `scheduleMessage`, holding the
  messages in a Chat SDK state adapter until `publishDue` (or `maintain({ scheduled })`) posts them.
  `inboxAdapter` gains `openDM` (with the new `dmInbox` option), `reply`, `fetchChannelInfo` and
  `listThreads`, and keeps a posted card in `metadata.card`. `better-supabase/testing` adds
  `testChatAdapter`, a conformance kit for Chat SDK adapters.
- The Chat SDK integration follows `chat` 4.42. Inbox thread ids name the inbox (`inbox:<inboxId>:<conversationId>`), so `channelIdFromThreadId` returns `inbox:<inboxId>`; build and read them with `inboxThreadId` and `decodeInboxThreadId`. The inbox module's upgrade (version 4 to 5) rewrites stored thread ids, subscriptions and thread state. `inboxAdapter` is an `Adapter<InboxThread, InboxMessage>`, forwards `WebhookOptions` (`waitUntil`, `propagateHandlerErrors`) from `dispatch` and `handleWebhook`, takes `concurrencyScope`, implements `markAsRead` and `fetchMessages` with `direction: "forward"` (through the new `after` argument of `list_inbox_messages`), and resolves reaction names to unicode. Adapter calls throw `ChatError` with the Chat SDK code (`RateLimitError` for `rate_limited` and `quota_exceeded`, `NotImplementedError` for `unsupported`) and the `DbError` as `cause`; `chatErrorOf` does the mapping. `inboundHandler` takes `propagateHandlerErrors`, `handler.mirror` records messages from other bots and skips only the bot's own, and `deliver` retries a platform `RATE_LIMITED` reply after `retryAfterMs` (`rateLimitRetries`, `maxRetryWaitMs`).

  | 0.31                            | 0.32                                      |
  | ------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
  | `inbox:<conversationId>`        | `inbox:<inboxId>:<conversationId>`        |
  | `Adapter<string, InboxMessage>` | `Adapter<InboxThread, InboxMessage>`      |
  | adapter errors are `DbError`s   | `ChatError` with the `DbError` as `cause` |
- Vercel Connect support covers the rest of `@vercel/connect`.

  - `vercelConnectCredentials` passes a ref's `audience`, `resources`, `authorizationDetails` and
    `validityBufferMs` through to the token request, takes a `region`, and maps Connect's error
    codes to hinted `DbError`s (`CREDENTIAL_CONNECTOR_NOT_FOUND`, `CREDENTIAL_CONNECTOR_NOT_LINKED`,
    `CREDENTIAL_CONNECTOR_NOT_ENABLED`, `CREDENTIAL_RATE_LIMITED`).
  - `getToken` takes `fresh: true` to skip the token cache.
  - `startAuthorization` returns the request, verifier, expiry and device code next to the URL, and
    takes `deviceCode` and `prompt`.
  - `CredentialSubject` adds `jwt-bearer` and `token` subjects for token exchange. Vercel Connect
    accepts them and Vault refuses them.
  - A ref with `inbound: true` verifies webhooks Connect forwards, through `@vercel/connect/chat`.
  - MCP servers whose grant is a Vercel Connect ref connect through `connectAuthProvider` from
    `@vercel/connect/mcp`; a consent request fails with `CONNECTOR_AUTHORIZATION_REQUIRED` and the
    URL in `details`.
  - eve's `credentialAuth` runs the interactive flow for providers without `completeAuthorization`
    and shows a device code.
  - `channelAdapter` passes a `resolve` function to `create`, so adapters read a rotated token per
    call.
  - Mail sync accounts and the sync report set `reconnect` when the member's grant is gone.

  The Next.js example adds an Integrations settings tab that connects Gmail and Outlook through
  Vercel Connect.
- `vercelConnectCredentials()` now returns `VercelConnectCredentials`, a credential provider with the calls only Vercel Connect has: `tokenDetails` (the whole token response), `invalidateToken` (drops a cached token the provider rejected), `connectorMetadata`, `startInstallation` (a connector installation for a tenant) and `channelConfig` (a Chat SDK adapter config from `@vercel/connect/chat`).

  `installationRef(ref, installationId)` lets the app subject read a tenant's own installation; the adapter checks the token's `tenantId` against the ref's tenant. `connectSubject(user, { supabaseUrl })` builds the Connect subject for a Supabase Auth user with the project's Auth issuer, and `withConnectorToken` runs a function with a user's token or returns the consent URL when the user hasn't authorized the connector.
- `better-supabase/eve` runs eve tools as the session's user. `supabaseTool` wraps a tool for eve's `defineTool` and gives its `execute` the repositories from `server.forContext()`, with the agent's `act` claim and the permission keys named in `scopes`. `eveContext` builds that context, and `tenantToolApproval` applies the tenants' `auto`, `ask` and `deny` tool policies.

  `approvalPolicy` is a tool `approval` backed by the approvals block: each call files one request under `eve_tool_call`, with the steps from `blocks.approvals.options.policies.eve_tool_call.<tool>`, and an answer in eve approves or rejects as the person who gave it.

  `queueSchedules` runs the queue block's schedules from one every-minute eve schedule and starts a session for each `eve` job as its user. `scheduleAgent`, `agentJobHandler`, `jobAuth` and the `eveAgentJob` schema go with it. `openApiAuth` gives `defineOpenAPIConnection` its token from a credential provider, and `tenantArgument` fills an argument with the session's organization.
- Feature flags keep a history and roll out on a schedule (breaking). Every `save_flag` records a revision with an optional message, and `createFlags()` gains `history(key)` and `restore(key, revision)`. `rolloutVariant` becomes `rolloutVariants`, a weighted split drawn from a second bucket so growing a rollout never moves a target between variants; `better-supabase upgrade` moves a stored variant over with weight 100. `rolloutSchedule` raises the percentage in steps, and rules can name shared segments (`saveSegment`, `segments`, `removeSegment`). The provider takes `onEvaluate`.

  `better-supabase/flags-sdk` adds `flagsDiscoveryHandler` for discovery routes outside Next.js, `explorerOverrides` and `withExplorerOverrides` for toolbar overrides on the OpenFeature path, `toFlagsSdkDefinition`, `flagDrift` and `vercelEnvironmentContext`. `better-supabase/testing` adds `flagOverrideCookie`.

  | 0.31                         | 0.32                                                |
  | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
  | `rolloutVariant: "on"`       | `rolloutVariants: [{ variant: "on", weight: 100 }]` |
  | `rollout_variant` column     | `rollout_variants`                                  |
  | `save_flag(key, definition)` | `save_flag(key, definition, message)`               |
- `better-supabase/flags-sdk` follows the Flags SDK's adapter conventions (breaking). `betterSupabaseAdapter` is now a ready adapter that reads `SUPABASE_URL` and `SUPABASE_SECRET_KEY` on first use; pass it to `flag()` as `adapter: betterSupabaseAdapter`. The old factory is `createBetterSupabaseAdapter`, which also takes `url` and `secretKey` and an `identify` every flag on it shares. `createIdentify({ server })` reads the session from the request without refreshing it, and `visitor` gives signed-out requests a stable rollout bucket. `getProviderData(adapter, { flags })` joins the declared flags for Flags Explorer. `pnpm better-supabase codemod 0.32` renames the calls.

  | 0.31                             | 0.32                                   |
  | -------------------------------- | -------------------------------------- |
  | `betterSupabaseAdapter(options)` | `createBetterSupabaseAdapter(options)` |
  | `adapter: bsFlags()`             | `adapter: bsFlags`                     |
  | `createIdentify({ claims })`     | `createIdentify({ server: bsb })`      |
- The access token can carry the caller's permission keys. The permissions block adds `better_supabase.permission_claims(user_id, tenant)` for the access token hook, which writes them under the `permissions` claim (`claims.permissions` renames it) for the active tenant, with `permissions_truncated` when they don't fit. `permissionsOf(session, tenant)` reads them back, and `permissionsAuthorizer({ permissions, load })` from `better-supabase/blocks/permissions` decides route, action and resource checks from the claim, calling `load` once per request only when the token can't answer.
- Run jobs that Vercel Queues pushes to a route. `vercelQueueRoute(jobs, { client, handlers })` from `better-supabase/blocks/queue` wraps the `@vercel/queue` client's `handleCallback`: it checks each payload against the queue's schema, runs the handler with the same tenant context as `drain`, retries a failed delivery after `retryAfter` and acknowledges the one that reaches `maxAttempts`. `vercelQueueNodeRoute` does the same for `(req, res)` frameworks over `handleNodeCallback`. `jobs.runJob(queue, delivery, handler)` runs one pushed job for other push backends.

  `vercelQueueProvider` now accepts a `QueueClient`, which only sends (its `read` rejects), and sets `retentionSeconds` on a delayed message so a delay longer than a day is kept until it runs. Delays past 7 days throw, since Vercel Queues keeps a message at most that long. The `onReceive` option reports each `receive` result, with the reason a poll found nothing.
- Vercel Queues: `vercelQueueProvider` takes `retention`, `headers` and a `consumerGroup` function per queue, hashes idempotency keys longer than 256 characters, and its `stats` points to the Vercel dashboard. New `vercelQueueTriggers` writes the `vercel.json` triggers for a push route, `vercelSendOptionsOf`, `vercelIdempotencyKey` and `vercelTopicOf` expose the send rules, and `withDeadLetters` hands jobs past their last attempt to a sink.
- Breaking: the public auth names follow supabase-js and Next.js, and tenant is the public word again. Run `npx better-supabase@latest codemod 0.32`, then read the [0.31 to 0.32 guide](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/migration/0.31-to-0.32).

  | 0.31                                                  | 0.32                                                                    |
  | ----------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | `auth.organization`, `requireOrganization`            | `auth.tenant`, `requireTenant`                                          |
  | `OrganizationConfig`                                  | `TenantConfig`                                                          |
  | `bsb.session()`, `bsb.getRequestSession()`            | `bsb.claims()`, `bsb.getClaims()`                                       |
  | `bsb.contextForSession()`                             | `bsb.contextFromSession()` (a snapshot, or a session with `{ token }`)  |
  | `AuthState.profile`, `AuthSession.profile`            | `userMetadata`                                                          |
  | caller kinds and `allow` values `anon`, `service`     | `none`, `secret`                                                        |
  | `bsb.signIn()`                                        | removed: `bsb.signInWithPassword()`                                     |
  | `bsb.authorizeUrl()`, `OAuthStart`                    | `bsb.signInWithOAuth()`, `SignInWithOAuthParams`                        |
  | `bsb.exchangeCode()`, `CodeExchange`                  | `bsb.exchangeCodeForSession()`, `ExchangeCodeParams`                    |
  | `endedSession`, `checkSession`, `EndedSessionOptions` | `verifySession`, `VerifySessionOptions`                                 |
  | `sessionEnded`, `sessionEndedReason`                  | `sessionVerified: false`, `reason` (`session_not_found`, `user_banned`) |
  | `refreshSkewSeconds`                                  | `leeway`                                                                |
  | `expiredPrefetch: "protect"`                          | `onExpiredPrefetch: "redirect"`                                         |
  | `bsb.invalidateSession()`, `bsb.forgetSession()`      | `bsb.evictSession()`, which returns a promise                           |
  | `bsb.cacheTag()`, `bsb.cacheTags()`                   | `bsb.tag()`, `bsb.tags()`                                               |

  The actor kinds (`ctx.actor.kind`), event subscribers and the Postgres roles keep `anon` and `service`.
- Add `better-supabase/vercel-sandbox`. `vercelSandboxes({ sandboxes })` gives each chat its own Vercel sandbox: `forChat` registers it in the AI chat block's `ai_sandboxes`, creates or resumes it by name, and returns an AI SDK sandbox session that marks the row used. `stop` is the stopper for the block's idle-stop job. The `untrusted`, `gatewayOnly` and `brokered` presets set the sandbox's network policy; `gatewayOnly` and `brokered` add tokens at the firewall, so code in the sandbox never holds them. `@vercel/sandbox` (`>=3 <4`) and `@ai-sdk/sandbox-vercel` (`>=1 <2`) are new optional peers.

  `better-supabase/ai-sdk` adds `codeTool()`, a tool that runs a Python, Node or Bash snippet in the call's `experimental_sandbox` with a timeout and an output cap. Hold each run for approval with `toolApproval` on the model call.
- Workflow SDK runs start once per idempotency key, even when two starts race. The Supabase World claims the key in `workflow.workflow_run_keys` before it starts the run, and `startOnce(ctx, workflow, args, { key })` is `startFor` with a required key. `better-supabase/workflow-sdk` adds `cancelRuns` with a reason, `resumeStreamResponse` for streams a client reconnects to by chunk index, and `resumeHookFromWebhook`, which verifies a Stripe or Standard Webhooks callback and resumes the hook. The World gains `openHooks` (the hooks open runs wait on, without tokens) and `purgeRuns`.

  Ended runs no longer have to stay forever: the `workflow-sdk` block's `retention` option (`"30 days"`) deletes them every hour with pg_cron, and `better-supabase db prune-workflows --older-than 30d` does it on demand. Run `better-supabase upgrade` and generate a migration for the new table and functions.
- The Workflow SDK integration follows `workflow` 5.2 (breaking for graph approvals). An approval node always files an approvals request through the runtime's `requestApproval`, which is now required, and `graphStarter` refuses a live run of a graph with an approval node unless you pass `approval`. `approvalToken` is removed, because a token derived from the run key could be guessed; resume the node with `wakeOnApproval`. The Supabase World speaks protocol 5.0.4, reads `WORKFLOW_POSTGRES_MAX_POOL_SIZE` (with `WORKFLOW_POSTGRES_MAX_CONNECTIONS` as an alias), and adds `registerRoute` and `registerHandler` so a queue prefix can go to its own route or run in-process. `approvalWaiter` and the durable chat resume use the SDK's `HookNotFoundError`, and `durableTurn` takes an optional `setAttributes` that records the turn's `bsb.phase`.

  | 0.31                          | 0.32                                                  |
  | ----------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------- |
  | `approvalToken(runKey, node)` | the approvals request id `requestApproval` returns    |
  | `graphStarter({ ... })`       | `graphStarter({ ..., approval })` for approval graphs |
- `gen` counts a row-level `before insert` trigger as filling a column a CHECK makes not null only when its PL/pgSQL body assigns that column (`new.col := …`, `new.col = …` or `select … into new.col`). A trigger that only validates no longer makes the column optional on insert. Snapshots record the assigned columns as `fills` on the trigger.

## 0.31.0 (2026-10-27)

- Security: block functions no longer let a caller through when a guard compares a null user column with `auth.uid()`. In the employees block anyone in the tenant could read an unlinked employee's contracts and private details and save their private details; the same pattern is fixed in every block that checked an owner, assignee, creator or holder this way.

  - Calendar: `permissions.create`, `permissions.update` and `permissions.delete` gate event writes on top of `calendar.write`, and `options.readOnly: { column, values }` locks events (such as synced ones) against changes, deletes, cancels and splits (`CALENDAR_EVENT_READ_ONLY`). Event `kind` and `transparency` and attendee `role` and `response` can be enum columns of the app's tables. `cancelOccurrence` also takes `{ seriesId, originalStart }`.
  - Employees: `create_employee` reads only the extra column keys from `attrs`, so a block key such as `status` no longer fails on an adopted enum column, and an omitted `not null default` extra column gets its default. `updateContract({ salary: null })` keeps an app table's currency.
  - Tasks: `options.notify: false` leaves assignment notifications to the app, and the `work_item.assigned` notification carries the item's title as its subject label and an action path from `options.actionPath` (`{id}`, `{identifier}`).
  - Share links: `blocks["share-links"].on` limits sharing to the listed subject types, including the types other blocks share.
  - Data lifecycle: exports leave out `share_links.token_hash`.
  - Custom fields: in the keyed layout with an integer rank, a field without a rank goes after the record's last one instead of writing `NULL`.
- The permissions block's catalog model gains `list_roles(tenant)` and `set_permission_override(tenant, role, permission, granted)`, and the permissions client gains `roles()` and `setOverride()`. They list the tenant roles with their default and effective keys, and grant, deny or clear one key for a role in one tenant. Listing needs `members.read`, changing needs the new `roles.manage` key; a member changes only roles they could assign and keys they hold.
- `better-supabase codemod 0.29` renames the `records` config key back to `subjects`, which reverses the `subjects` to `records` rename that `codemod 0.15` made. It renames the key inside `$env.*` and `$<environment>` blocks too, and merges the entries into an existing `subjects` key (those entries win) with a line to review. It also renames the `@bsb record` tag in the table comments of `supabase/schemas/*.sql` to `@bsb subject`, and leaves migrations alone.
- Doctor's BS239 warns when a Supabase project runs the pg-delta workflow but `@supabase/pg-delta` or `@supabase/pg-topo` is not installed, since `db diff` stops without them. The finding names the missing packages and the `pnpm add -D @supabase/pg-delta @supabase/pg-topo` command.

  Doctor's peer checks (BS239 and BS601) now also find a peer installed next to the better-supabase package, where the CLI loads it from, so a pnpm workspace that installs it there no longer reports it missing.
- `createNext` starts the flows that email a link: `signInWithOtp`, `resetPasswordForEmail`, `signUp` and `resend` call Auth with a PKCE challenge and write the same verifier cookie as `authorizeUrl`, so `exchangeCode` signs the user in from the link's `?code=` with the default email templates. A flow started with supabase-js stores its verifier in another format that `exchangeCode` can't read. `signInWithOtp`, `resetPasswordForEmail` and `resend` return an `ActionResult<null>`; `signUp` returns `null` until the user confirms, or commits the session like `signIn` when email confirmation is off. `SignedIn` now carries `user` (the verified claims' `UserClaims`, `null` after sign-out), so an action needs no `getUser()` call after signing in.
- `better-supabase powersync streams` accepts the `memberships`, `teams` and `profiles` instances. Memberships syncs the user's own memberships and the organizations they belong to, teams syncs the user's teams and their members, and profiles syncs the user's own profile. `better-supabase/powersync` adds `powersyncMembershipsTransport`, `powersyncTeamsTransport` and `powersyncProfilesTransport`, which answer the list and update functions from SQLite, and `membershipsRoute`, `teamsRoute`, `teamMembersRoute` and `profilesRoute` for `createUploadConnector({ tables })`, which replay local writes through the block functions.
- `devices.checkReceipts()` emits the `notification.delivered` and `notification.bounced` block events for the receipts it records, with the same data `recordDeliveryEvent` emits (`provider: "expo"`, `providerEventId: "expo-receipt:<ticket id>"`). Before, a push receipt reached the delivery history but no `betterSupabase.on` handler. `createPush` takes `events`, `actorId` and `context` for it. The push module moves to version 3: `complete_push_receipts` returns `{ updated, events }` instead of the ticket count, so run `better-supabase upgrade` and generate a migration.
- `teams.list()` returns each team's `description`, `status` (`active` or `archived`, typed `TeamStatus`) and `memberIds`, sorted by name, so an app can tell archived teams apart and show who is on a team without reading `team_members` itself. `teams.update()` returns the stored row, including a description it kept. Run `better-supabase upgrade` and generate a migration to pick up the new `list_teams` and `update_team`.
- `InviteRequest`, `Organizations`, `OrganizationsOptions` and `createOrganizations` take the role union as a type parameter (`RoleName` from the generated blocks file, or `RoleNames<typeof config>`), so `invite({ role })` and `onAccept.roles` reject an unknown role at compile time. `InvitationDraft` and `createInvitationsController` take it too. The default stays `string`, and `onAccept.teams` stays `string[]`.
- Fixes three block SQL bugs found by running the Next.js example on the `catalog` permissions model.

  - Approval steps that name an approver role (`approvers: { role: "admin" }`) now work under `permissions.model: "catalog"`. The approvals block compared the role key to the membership's role id, which raised `operator does not exist: uuid = text` when it notified approvers or checked who may decide.
  - Removing a member from an organization now also removes them from its teams. The teams block's `team_members` foreign key to the memberships table had no `on delete`, so deleting a membership of someone on a team failed. Run `better-supabase upgrade` and generate a migration to pick up `on delete cascade`.
  - Turning the teams `patch` option off again drops the `create_team` overload that takes a `slug`, as turning it on already dropped the one without. Before, a database could end up with both, and calls to `create_team` failed as ambiguous.
- Ignore Next.js segment and middleware prefetches in `expectDbBudget`, and count only visible matches for `expectInstant`'s `absent` locators.
- `better-supabase/testing` no longer pulls `@supabase/lite` or `@next/playwright` into a Turbopack bundle. Both loaded through a specifier held in a `const`, which Turbopack folds and resolves, so an app that imported `seedTenantOnCreate` from `instrumentation.ts` failed to compile on `@supabase/pg-delta`. They now load through the same bundler-ignored import as the other optional peers.

## 0.30.0 (2026-10-26)

- Blocks laid over your own tables now behave as they do on their own tables.

  - Numbering: a block that numbers its own row (tasks, deals, quotes, invoices, donations) marks the number it took from `next_number` for the transaction, so a `numbering` entity with a `table` accepts it instead of refusing it with `NUMBER_MANUAL`, and the number is assigned once.
  - Rows: read functions of tasks, employees, hiring, pipelines, quotes and invoices return a row of your table keyed as on the block's table. A renamed column also comes back under the block's name (`tenant_id` when the tenant column is `organization_id`), and a mapped value as its label, so the TypeScript clients parse it. Bug fix: `organizationId` and renamed fields used to read as missing.
  - Extra columns: `insertExtraColumns` writes every extra column in the insert, with the type's `default` for a key the call leaves out, so a check across extra columns holds when the row is created.
