Tasks, quote, invoice and deal lines, employee contracts and private details, teams, tags, saved views, hiring jobs and calendars use it.
  - `add_employee_contract` keeps your contract table's currency default when `attrs` has no `currency`.
  - Value maps: `blocks.<name>.values` now applies in the catalog, quotes, invoices, inventory, orders and shipments modules: status and kind literals in functions, policies and reads, with list values and `null` for a label your app lacks. `products.kind` and the status of stock reservations and transfers are typed columns. A quote whose map sets `viewed: null` keeps its status when the link is opened.
  - Calendar: `columns.rules.id` may be `null` for a rules table keyed by its event, and `options.ruleDialect` (`weekdays: "lower"`, `monthDay: "scalar"`, `ordinal: { weekday, position }`) reads and writes structured rules in your table's shape.

  Generated SQL changes only where these apply: blocks over your tables, a renamed tenant column, extra columns and numbered entities with a table.
- `createNext` signs users in and out on the server for every flow that starts or ends a session. `verifyOtp` verifies an email link's `token_hash` or an emailed code, `authorizeUrl` and `exchangeCode` run OAuth with a PKCE verifier cookie (httpOnly, `SameSite=Lax`, 10 minutes, deleted on exchange), `verifyMfa` commits the `aal2` session, and `signOut` ends the session in Auth before it clears the cookies, forgets the verified session and expires the user's cache tags. `signInWithPassword` is the same as `signIn`. Each returns `redirectTo` (a same-origin `next` or the fallback) and `mfa`, plain data a Server Action can return to the client; the committed session is on `getRequestSession()` and `context()` for the rest of the request.

  After a sign-in or sign-out, `getRequestSession()`, `session()`, `context()` and `cached()` return the new session for the rest of the request. Reads rebuild the request from the cookie jar, so a Server Action sees its own writes, and a render that never commits still verifies its token once. Auth errors keep their `error_code` as `code` and no longer carry Auth's message, so a form can't reveal whether an account exists. `cookieScopes` on `createNext` also expires the session cookie at old scopes on sign-out.

  Doctor's BS506 warns when an env file sets `SUPABASE_URL` and a browser spelling such as `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` to different URLs, since the session cookie name comes from the URL and the server and the browser would then read different cookies. Set `auth.cookie.name` on both clients when the split is intended.

## 0.29.0 (2026-10-26)

- `migrate schema-first`, `init --preset` and `add --preset` type a format column (`email`, `url`, `rating`, `percent`) as a domain in the `bsb` schema, such as `bsb.email`, and write the domains as files under `supabase/schemas/bsb`. A column with its own `min`, `max` or `options` keeps its inline check. Rollups and lookups are written as `security_invoker` views in `public/views`. `gen` types a domain column as its base type.
- Breaking: the config has no `tables` key, and `records` is now `subjects`. Tables are SQL files you own under `supabase/schemas`, with features as `@bsb` tags in the table comment. Run `better-supabase migrate schema-first` once to write the files from your old `tables`, then delete `tables` and rename `records` to `subjects`; every other command refuses the old keys and says so. `init --preset` and `add --preset` write a design's tables as SQL files and keep a file that exists, so the breaking-change report, rename statements and `--allow-breaking` flag (and the `allow_breaking` MCP argument) of `add --preset` are gone (`db diff` plans them). `AppTableNames`, `TypedReference` and `hasFlatTables` are removed from `better-supabase/config`, and `presetConfigSource` returns a config without the design's tables. Create's design format and `better-supabase/create/lite` keep `appTables`. See the 0.28 to 0.29 migration guide.
- The App tables docs page describes tables as SQL in `supabase/schemas` with `@bsb` tags, and the Record grid recipe sets a field through the typed repository instead of the `appTables.set` bulk action. The CentraKit validation project declares its record types as `subject` tags.
- Repo tooling: an oxlint rule replaces the raw-policy test. No published change.

## 0.28.0 (2026-10-26)

- `better-supabase add <block> --over <schema.table>` installs one block over a table you already have. It proposes the `tables` and `columns` mapping, reports what cannot map and writes `blocks.<block>` to the config, like `add --adopt <block> --tables`, and refuses to combine with those two flags.
- `better-supabase db diff` plans with pg-delta's library on a local pg-delta project. A rename becomes a rename only when a migration records `-- bsb: rename old -> new`, and a diff that drops tables or columns stops with a `check_failed` error until you pass `--allow-data-loss`. The shadow database comes from `provisionCoLocatedShadow`, so `pg_dump`, `psql` and `BSB_PG_BIN` are no longer used. Install `@supabase/pg-delta` and `@supabase/pg-topo` (optional peers) to use it. The Supabase CLI still runs for the migra workflow and remote databases.
- `better-supabase init` recognizes a project that already has a `supabase` folder: it prints the migrations, schema files and snapshot tables it found, says that the database is unchanged, and points at `doctor` and `table` as next steps. The new `--from` flag starts a project from a `postgres://` URL, which writes the database's schema to `supabase/snapshot.json`, or from a preset, design code, URL or file like `--preset`.
- Tenant topics of `realtime-tables` (`bsb:t:<table>:<tenant>`) are now received by members of that tenant at the time of the message, checked with `member_organization_ids()`, instead of by whoever carries the tenant id in their token. A removed member stops receiving at once. Without the `memberships` module tenant topics stay closed; unscoped and own-user topics are unchanged.
  The preset catalog is regenerated with it.
- `migrate schema-first` keeps the grants, comment and policies of a view in the view's own file, and leaves out a `drop view` for a view it writes. `gen` names the events of a table with an `events` tag after its `subject` tag type, as the table's trigger does.

## 0.27.1 (2026-10-26)

- Block modules now emit every row level security policy as a typed `Policy` value, so the installed SQL is laid out one clause per line. The policies, their names and their drops are unchanged, and a module that writes `create policy` by hand now fails the test suite.

## 0.27.0 (2026-10-26)

- Blocks over the app's own tables take more of their shapes, all opt-in:

  - `pipelines`: with `status.follow`, an insert that sets the status and no stage lands on the first active stage whose category maps to that status, instead of the first open stage overwriting the status.
  - A `mappable` column with a `values` entry, even `{}`, no longer counts as a text column the module writes, so an enum there passes the adoption check. `employees` maps `employmentType` this way.
  - `hiring` `saveJob` writes `not null` extra columns in the insert that creates the job.
  - Per-action keys with fallbacks: `tasks` `permissions.create` and `permissions.delete`, and `options.authorCanDelete: false`; `employees` `permissions.create`, `contractsRead`, `contractsWrite` and `privateDetailsWrite`; `hiring` `permissions.create`, `score`, `jobsCreate` and `jobsUpdate`.
  - `employees` contracts: `salary` and `currency` can map to `null`, `options.salaryUnits: "major"` reads and writes a major-unit salary column, and `options.contractOverlap: "allow"` adds an editable contract without the overlap check.
  - `employees` `update` accepts the app statuses a value map lists after a label's first value, such as `offboarding`.
  - `hiring` with `options.stages`: a move to an unmarked stage keeps a state that no stage marks, such as a rejection.
  - Entity rename errors name `blocks.<name>.names`, the key the config takes.
- `gen` now also reads the `json`, `encrypted` and `events` table tags, and `migrate schema-first` writes them. `json meta=Type` types a jsonb column (`meta=import:module#Name` imports the type), `encrypted ssn=bigint` marks a sealed column, and `events on="created, updated"` lists the outbox events. A config key still wins over a tag.
- The CLI now reads `@bsb` table comments from `supabase/schemas` for every command, so `upgrade` and doctor see the same `subject`, `realtime`, `vector-search` and `json` entries as `gen`. The `subject` tag takes `id`, `id-type`, `tenant`, `scope`, `cascade`, `deleted-at`, `search`, `search-title`, `search-body`, `search-description`, `search-exclude`, `rank`, `rank-group`, `versions`, `versions-auto` and `can-<action>`, and `migrate schema-first` writes them. `migrate schema-first` also works when `supabase/` sits above the project folder. A config key still wins over a tag.

  A new `server` table tag lists the columns signed-in users can't read (`server notes`), so `gen` types them as not selectable once a table lives in your schema files; `migrate schema-first` writes it for `access: "server"` columns.
- Authorization templates are now checked by a tokenizer instead of regular expressions. A `;` or `--` inside a string or quoted identifier no longer counts, and a tagged dollar quote (`$tag$`), a block comment, an unterminated string or a stray `{` is refused. `gen` refuses an unsafe template before it fills the placeholders.

## 0.26.0 (2026-10-26)

- Add `accessMatrixTests` to `better-supabase/create/lite`. It turns the access matrix into one pgTAP file per persona, which you can write to `supabase/tests/database` and run with `supabase test db`.
- `better-supabase/sql` adds `parseBsbComment`, `formatBsbComment`, `setBsbTag` and `commentOnTableSql`, which read and write the `@bsb` feature tags in a table comment (ADR 0102). Nothing reads them yet; `gen` and the `table` command follow.
- A block over your own table no longer creates a plain index that one of your indexes, primary keys or unique constraints already covers with the same leading columns. `add` and `upgrade` read those indexes from your schema files and the saved snapshot (`ownIndexes` in the module layout), and `better-supabase/sql` exports `declaredIndexes`.
- The custom-fields keyed layout takes `options.rankType: "integer"`, which keeps `rank` in an integer column (or casts it to the type of the app's own rank column), and `options.readAccess: "record"`, which makes `read_field_values` check each record through `can_subject(type, id, 'read')` instead of `permissions.read` in the tenant. The keyed layout no longer reports `permissions.sensitive` to the authorization provider, since it keeps no sensitive values. A module can now name the actions a config never checks with `unusedPermissions`.
- The `none` scope refuses anonymous sign-ins: its read checks and `can` conditions require a user whose `is_anonymous` claim is not true. Run `better-supabase upgrade` and generate a migration (ADR 0104).
- `better-supabase/create` adds `gatePlan` and `parseRenameHints`, which decide renames and block destructive actions in a migration plan shaped like pg-delta's (ADR 0106). The `db diff` wiring follows.
- `blocks.messaging.on` and `blocks.comments.on` keep record conversations and comments to the record types they list. `open_conversation` refuses another type with `MESSAGING_SUBJECT_UNSUPPORTED` and `create_comment` with `COMMENT_SUBJECT_UNSUPPORTED`, so registering a type in `config.records` for another block no longer opens conversations on it. Without `on`, every registered type is allowed as before.
- A `config.records.<type>.permissions.<action>` entry takes `also`, a permission key the caller must hold as well, in the row's tenant or on the record's scope: `{ key, rows, also }` asks for a row grant (or the key) and the tenant key both. `can_subject`, `can_subject_for` and the blocks' record read checks honour it.
- Every table and sequence a block creates now revokes its privileges from `PUBLIC` as well as `anon` and `authenticated`, and a block instance schema revokes default privileges for objects created later. Existing databases need no migration: the revokes are no-ops where `PUBLIC` holds no grant.
- `gen` reads `@bsb` table comment tags (ADR 0102): `subject`, `realtime` and `vector-search` on a table behave like `subjects`, `database.realtime` and `vectorSearch` entries, and a key the config sets itself wins. `better-supabase migrate schema-first` writes the tables that `tables` (the old `appTables`) renders as files you own under `supabase/schemas`, with the config's per-table features as tags. The config keys keep working in this release step; delete `tables` after running the command.
- `config.records.<type>.search.watch[]` takes `through: [{ table, from, to }]`, which follows the watched row's `key` across further tables, such as a junction table, before it reaches the record's id (or the record's `via` column). Renaming a tag can now re-index every record it is linked to.
- `better-supabase table <table> enable|disable <feature>` turns a per-table feature on or off by editing the `@bsb` tag in the table comment, in the schema file that creates the table (ADR 0102). It takes `--dry-run` and `--check`. `better-supabase/sql` exports `TABLE_FEATURES`, `setTableFeature` and the helpers behind it.
- The restrictive tenant scope is now one policy per command (`bsb_tenant_scope_select`, `_insert`, `_update` and `_delete`), each `to public` instead of `to authenticated` (ADR 0104). A permissive policy that reaches `anon` or `public` can no longer read across organizations, and a branch one command grants no longer satisfies another. Policies that grant `anon` or `public` on a tenant table keep working because their conditions stay inside the matching scope policy. `upgrade` drops the old `for all` policy and writes the four new ones; run `supabase db schema declarative sync` for the migration. Breaking for apps that relied on an open `anon` or `public` policy on a tenant table without a condition the block knows about.
- `better-supabase/create/lite` adds `verifyFiles`, `verifyCatalog`, `verifyQuery`, `readSecurityCatalog` and `accessMatrix`. They check row level security, write policies that are only `true`, `anon` and `PUBLIC` grants and `search_path` on security definer functions, and report how far `anon` and `authenticated` reach per table and command (ADR 0105).
- Billable time works with time tracking's per-type record columns (`blocks.time-tracking.on.<type>.column`) and duration entries (`endedAt` mapped to `null`): it reads an entry's record from its column, computes the end from the start and `seconds`, leaves running timers out, and locks the duration and running mark of a billed entry.
- `employee_from_application` works over a hiring jobs table of your own: it compares an enum employment type by its text, maps it back through `blocks.hiring.values`, and leaves the team or employment type empty when the jobs table has no column for it instead of failing to render.
- `better-supabase/create/lite` now exports `catalogQuery` and `readCatalog`, which the 0.22.0 notes for `importProject` already named. Run the query yourself, pass its rows to `readCatalog`, then pass the result to `designFromCatalog`.
- A block's `on.<type>` entry for a type `config.records` registers inherits `table` and `idType` everywhere: `add`, `upgrade`, `gen`, `sql --adopt` and doctor now read `config.records` too, so they no longer fail with "needs a table" where rendering already merged the entry.
- Blocks that check a record's read permission (tags, attachments, share links, versions, relations and the other modules that take `on`) now honour a `config.records.<type>.permissions.read` with `rows` or `owner`: the key, the provider's row grants and the owner column each grant the read, as `can_subject` does. Before, only the key counted as a tenant key, so `{ key, rows }` refused callers with a row grant (`SHARE_SUBJECT_NOT_FOUND` and similar) and `{ owner }` checked nothing.
- The memberships module lets `anon` execute `member_organization_ids` and `has_organization_role`. The restrictive tenant scope applies to every role, so an anonymous reader of a public table (a published storefront) hit "permission denied for function" before it reached the block's public policy. For `anon` both functions return no organizations and false.
- A record type whose search reads `from` a view now re-indexes on any update of the record's row, so renaming a record or setting its archived flag updates its search document. Before, only the id, tenant, `deletedAt` and `url` columns were watched.
- `blocks.share-links.tables.views` and `tables.state` can be `null` when the block manages its own tables too; before, only a block over the app's tables could leave them out.

## 0.25.0 (2026-10-26)

- `better-supabase init` adds the `rls-guard` block to the config it writes, so row level security stays on for every table the project creates later. Remove `'rls-guard'` from `blocks` to opt out (ADR 0104).

## 0.24.0 (2026-10-26)

- The calendar block runs over an app's own calendar tables, with five opt-in options. A config without them renders the same SQL as before.

  - `options.access`: per-calendar access. Calendars get a `visibility` (`shared` or `private`) and an owner, events a `visibility` and an owner, and `tables.grants` gives a member `viewer` or `editor` access. Every write function checks the calendar or event it changes, the read policies hide private calendars and events from other members, and feeds and free/busy never show a private event's details. `options.accessFunctions` names app functions that decide instead. Client: `setGrant`, `removeGrant`, `grants`.
  - `options.userFeed`: a share link whose subject is a user (`calendar_user`) serves every calendar that user can read as one feed (`calendar_user_feed`, client `userFeed`).
  - `options.rdates` adds an `rdates` column of extra occurrence starts, and `options.recurrence: "table"` keeps rules and dates in `tables.rules` (an `rrule` text column or structured columns) and `tables.recurrenceDates`. Saving, cancelling, splitting, importing and every read go through them. `expandOccurrences`, `parseIcs` and `toIcs` read and write `RDATE`.
  - `options.attendeeKey: "any"`: an attendee is a user, an email or a subject record, with `organizer` and `optional` flags; `set_calendar_attendee(event_id, attendee)` updates or inserts without a unique constraint and `remove_calendar_attendee(attendee_id)` removes one (client `removeAttendeeById`). The old signatures keep working.
  - `options.calendarDetails`: calendars get `description`, `rank` and `isDefault`, `createCalendar` takes them, and `update_calendar(calendar_id, attrs)` (client `updateCalendar`) changes them and records `calendar.updated`.

  An owner column of another type, such as a `uuid`, takes `values.calendars.ownerId: {}`, so `create_calendar` casts the owner to it.
- `employees` takes three opt-in options for tables an app already has. `options.name: "split"` writes `firstName` and `lastName` instead of one name: `create` takes them or splits `name`, `update` takes `firstName`, `lastName` and `name`, and the full name is read from a mapped (generated) `name` column, which is never written, or joined from the parts when `name` is mapped to `null`; the record title then falls back to `firstName`. `options.privateDetails: "flat"` keeps the address and one emergency contact in plain columns (`addressLine1`, `addressLine2`, `postalCode`, `city`, `region`, `country`, `emergencyName`, `emergencyPhone`, `emergencyRelation`), written from those keys or the usual `address` object and `emergencyContacts` list, and read back in that shape. `options.contracts: "editable"` adds `update_employee_contract` and `delete_employee_contract` (client `updateContract` and `deleteContract`). The private details' `user` and `iban` columns can be mapped to `null`, and a contract's `salaryPeriod` takes `values` for an app enum. Without these settings the module renders as before.
- The hiring block runs over an applicant tracker's own tables. `columns.applications.candidateType: null` makes every candidate an `options.candidate` record and writes the id as the column's own type, such as a uuid foreign key. `options.stages` takes the app's table of stages per job, with boolean or keyed markers for hired, rejected and withdrawn stages, so the job is the pipeline and `applications.pipeline` can be `null`. `tables.interviewers` keeps interviewers as junction rows, `options.scorecardKey: "application"` keeps one scorecard per reviewer per application, `scorecards.recommendation` can be `null`, and `tables.scorecardScores` stores criterion scores that `score({ scores })` writes and `detail` returns. `options.hiringManager: "employee"` takes an employee id, and `employmentType`, `source` and interview `kind` accept value maps. A config without the new keys renders the same SQL.
- `time-tracking` takes `on.<type>.column` over your own entries table: a record type keeps its id in its own foreign key column (cast to the record's `idType`), and `subject_type` and `subject_id` can be mapped to `null` once every type has one. `options.owner: "employee"` (with `approvals: false` and the employees block) keeps entries and timesheets per employee, so an employee without an account has timesheets: the timesheet key is the employee and the week, `columns.timesheets.user` can be `null`, `log_time`, `list_time_entries`, `get_timesheet`, `submit_timesheet` and `list_timesheets` take an `employee_id`, own entries and weeks are those of the caller's employee record, and rows and events carry `employeeId`. The client takes `employeeId` on `log`, `list`, `timesheet`, `submit` and `timesheets`. Without these settings the module renders as before.
- Rendering blocks and running `doctor` or `upgrade` are faster again. The SQL scanner that finds policies and grants now copies untouched text in whole slices, so the actor guard's policy read no longer slows every render.
- `rls-guard` also fires after `alter table`, so a statement that disables row level security on a table outside the Supabase-managed schemas is undone straight away. Run `better-supabase upgrade` to refresh the module's data file (ADR 0104).

## 0.23.0 (2026-10-26)

- Blocks take `blocks.<name>.values.<table>.<column>`, which maps each label a block writes or compares to the value your own column stores: `{ cancelled: "canceled" }`, several values for one label (`{ started: ["in_progress", "on_hold"] }`, the first is written), or `null` for a label the app does not support, which fails with `VALUE_UNSUPPORTED`. The map applies to writes, comparisons in functions, triggers and pipeline bindings, and to the typed fields read functions return. Tasks (`state`, `closeReason`, and `priority` once mapped), calendar (`status`, and `kind`, `transparency`, attendee `role` and `response` once mapped), employees (`status`) and hiring (`state`, job `status`) apply it. A config without `values` renders the same SQL as before.
- `comments` and `messaging`: a record type that declares a `comment` action in `config.records` keeps its comment threads on the record's own checks. Reading follows `can_subject(type, id, 'read')` without the tenant messaging read key, opening a thread and sending or editing a comment need `can_subject(..., 'comment')`, and deleting others' comments takes the record's `moderate` action (when declared) or the tenant moderate key. Record types without a `comment` action keep today's checks, so existing configs render as before.
- `custom-fields` takes `options.layout: "keyed"`: each value row carries its field's `key`, `label` and `type`, and its value in the column of that type (`valueUrl` and `valueEmail` are new optional columns). `set_field_value(tenant, subject_type, subject_id, key, label, type, value jsonb, rank, if_absent)` upserts by record and key and clears the other value columns, `read_field_values` returns typed rows, and the new `delete_field_value(value_id)` records `field_value.deleted`; the client gains `setField`, `readFields` and `deleteValue`. A record type can keep its id in its own column of the values table (`on.<type>.column`), so `subject_type` and `subject_id` can be mapped to `null`. Every layout's `set_field_value` checks the record's `fields` action when `config.records` declares one. The normalized and inline layouts render as before.
- More tables take the app's own columns: `blocks.calendar.options.calendarExtraColumns` (written by `create_calendar`, returned by `list_calendars`), and `blocks.employees.options.privateDetailsExtraColumns` and `contractExtraColumns` (written from `attrs.fields` by `save_employee_private_details` and `add_employee_contract`). `saved-views` writes an extra column that is `not null` without a `default` inside the insert, as tags and teams do. Without the options the modules render as before.
- `pipelines`: a binding's `status.follow: true` moves a record to the first active stage whose category maps to a status written directly to its status column, so stage, history and time in stage stay right when app code writes the status (`PIPELINE_STAGE_INVALID` when no active stage matches a mapped status). `tasks`: `tables.checklist` can be `null` for an app with its own subtasks, which drops the checklist table and functions and returns `checklist: null`; `priority` takes `values` (the block's 0 to 4) for an app enum. Both render as before without the settings.
- `better-supabase/sql` exports a typed policy builder and a policy rule DSL:
  `policy`, `renderPolicy`, `readPolicies`, `readGrants`, `maskSql`, `rulePolicy`,
  `renderRulePolicy`, `readRulePolicies`, `renderRule`, `isRule`,
  `disallowedPolicyCalls`, `policyFunctionsSql` and `POLICY_FUNCTIONS`, with the
  `Policy` and `Rule` types. A rule renders to a policy that calls only
  `bsb.is_member`, `bsb.can`, `bsb.owns` and `bsb.uid`, and reads back from the
  `-- @bsb rule` comment above it.
- `search`: a `config.records.<type>.search` entry takes `description` (returned on each hit as `SearchHit.description`), `exclude` (a column that keeps a row out of the index while it is set, separate from `deletedAt`), `from` (a view or table keyed by the record's id that the title, body, description and exclude columns read) and `watch` (related tables whose changes re-index the record, directly or through a `via` column). The index gains a `description` column only once a subject sets one, so existing configs render as before.
- `share-links` checks a record's `share` action from `config.records` when its type declares one: `create_share_link` needs it on the record (as the caller, so per-row grants and owner columns count), and `revoke_share_link` and `list_share_links` need it on top of the organization key unless the caller created the link. `options.tokenHash` adds a caller-made `token_hash` to `create_share_link` and a non-consuming `find_share_link(token_hash)`; `options.fullRows` makes `list_share_links` return the tenant, subject, creator and creation time and take `include_revoked`. The client gains `createHashed`, `find` and a `list` filter. Without a `share` action and the options the module renders as before.
- `config.records.<type>.permissions.<action>` takes `{ key, rows, owner }` besides a key: `rows` grants the action on the rows of a provider resource the caller holds `key` on, through the new `rowsWith` provider template (`rowsWithFor` for `can_subject_for`), and `owner` grants it to the user a uuid column names. `can_subject` and `can_subject_for` check every form. Blocks that act on a record ask the record's own action when its type declares one: `tags` checks a `tag` action in `tag` and `untag`, and `custom-fields` a `fields` action when it writes a value; a config that declares neither keeps its checks.
- `tags`: `blocks.tags.permissions` takes `create`, `update` and `delete`, each falling back to `manage`. With `options.patch: true`, `create_tag` takes an optional `slug` (checked against `^[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, `TAG_SLUG_INVALID`), and `update_tag` an optional `patch jsonb` that sets `name`, `slug`, `color`, `description` and `kind`, where `null` clears `color` or `description`; the client's `create` and `update` take them. An extra column that is `not null` without a `default` is written inside the insert, so `create` can set it. Without the option the functions keep their signatures.
- `teams`: `blocks.teams.permissions` takes `create`, `update`, `delete` and `members`, each falling back to `manage`. With `options.patch: true`, `create_team` takes an optional `slug` and `update_team` an optional `patch jsonb` that sets `name`, `slug` and `description` (where `null` clears the description); the client's `create` and `update` take a trailing options object for them. With `options.restore: true`, the new `restore_team` makes an archived team active again and records `team.restored`, and the client's `restore(id)` calls it. An extra column that is `not null` without a `default` is written inside the insert. Without the options the functions keep their signatures.
- `time-tracking`: `options.approvals: false` drops the approvals dependency; timesheets then keep a `status` column (open, submitted, approved, rejected) with optional `decidedBy`, `decidedAt` and `decisionNote`, and members with `time_tracking.approve` decide through `approve_timesheet` and the new `reject_timesheet` (client `approve` and `reject`, event `timesheet.rejected`). `options.userTimeZone` starts each member's week in the zone a column of yours holds, through a new `time_week(at, user_id)`. Over your own entries table, an entry can be a start and a duration (`endedAt: null`, `seconds` as the writable duration column, `runningSince` as the running mark). Without these settings the module renders as before.

## 0.22.0 (2026-10-26)

- `better-supabase/create/lite` adds `openSandbox` and `designDataApi`. `openSandbox(project)` applies a project's files to an in-memory PGlite database and offers `as({ sub, role, claims })` personas that run with row level security, plus a `rest(request)` handler for a subset of PostgREST (table reads and writes, filters, order, limit, offset, `rpc`). `designDataApi(preset, design)` returns the OpenAPI 3.1 document of the Data API a design produces. `validateDesign` now shares its file runner with the sandbox.
- `importProject` in `better-supabase/create/lite` reads an existing project into a `Design` and a report. It takes a declarative schema or migrations folder (replayed in PGlite), any queryable database, or a Supabase project ref and access token (through the Management API read-only endpoint). The report lists what it modeled, what it kept as it is, the tables that belong to blocks, each table's adoption rung and the security findings. `catalogQuery` and `designFromCatalog` are exported for callers that run the query themselves.
- One project pipeline for Create, the CLI and the cloud (ADR 0100). `better-supabase/create` adds `loadProject`, `checkProject` and a single `Issue` type with a `severity` and a `source`; `checkDesign`, `designIssues` and `validateDesign` now report `Issue` values, and the two `DesignIssue` types are gone (breaking: a lite issue has `table` and `file` where it had `module` and `path`). `previewFiles` takes `ownTables` and `ownColumns` to render over tables the project already has. `better-supabase/preset` exports the instance rules the cloud copied: `canAddInstance`, `blockRequires`, `instanceNameProblem`, `withInstance`, `withoutInstance`, `withEntityName`, `installedBlocks`, `presetKey`, `createQuery` and `presetArgumentOf`. Rename `DesignIssue` to `Issue` and read `issue.file` and `issue.table` for lite findings.
- App table write policies now refuse a read-only support or agent session. The insert, update and delete policies that `appTables` renders carry the same `actor_may_write()` check as every block policy, and the SQL defines the function when the file needs it. Run `upgrade` and generate a migration to pick it up.
- Doctor's BS345 check treats the `actor_may_write()` support-session guard as tenant membership, so app tables that carry the guard no longer raise a false warning for attachment subjects.

## 0.21.0 (2026-10-25)

- Blocks run over the app's own tables everywhere, block calls return a `Result`, and Temporal values replace date strings. The upgrade guide is at [From 0.20 to 0.21](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/migration/0.20-to-0.21).

  **Breaking:**

  - `db.$api(fn, args, options)` returns an `AsyncResult` instead of throwing. Call `.orThrow()` for the old behavior, or read `.ok`. It takes `$rpc` options (the new `ApiOptions` type), and a `returns` schema validates and types the value. `db.$apiResult` from 0.19 is now a deprecated alias of `$api`.
  - `DbBlockSource.call` is replaced by `api`, the name of the API schema; the core calls it through `$rpc`. `BlockSet.call` returns an `AsyncResult`, and `BlockSet.api` names the API schema.
  - These fields are `Temporal.Instant` instead of strings: the argument of `reminders.markSent`, `DeliveryPayload.occurrenceAt`, `PlannedDelivery.occurrenceAt`, `Relation.createdAt`, `BulkRun.createdAt` and `finishedAt`, and `IncomingWebhook.createdAt` and `lastReceivedAt`. A bookings service's duration is a `Temporal.Duration`.
  - An app table a block runs over must have the columns that block calls need. Calls that need a missing column fail with a `*_UNSUPPORTED` error, and a review reply without a reply column fails with `REVIEW_INVALID`.
  - The default SQL of several blocks changed: identities, queue and collections upsert `on conflict` by columns, status columns are written explicitly, and the activities `externalRef` index exists only when the column is mapped. The line tables of quotes, invoices and deals get a `bsb_<lines>_position_key` unique index and upsert by position. Run `upgrade` and generate a migration.

  **Added and fixed:**

  - Temporal values are encoded at any depth in function arguments, `queryRaw` parameters and block fields. A `ZonedDateTime` is sent as its instant, and `Duration`, `PlainYearMonth` and `PlainMonthDay` have a wire form.
  - Every block table can be the app's own table, except the `workflow-sdk` world. Line tables take optional columns and an enum `productType`. Idempotency, rate-limit, reserved-slugs, queue and `audit.registry` have `names.tables`, and their default SQL quotes identifiers.
  - Tenant columns follow the config: state machine and numbering entities default to the `config.records` tenant, else `organization_id` (or its rename); `history.tenant` defaults to the machine's tenant column; the tasks pipeline, doctor's subject check and the legacy upgrades follow a renamed tenant; `gen` writes renamed tenant columns and the middleware reads them back as `tenant_id`.
  - `orderMetrics`, `timeTrackingMetrics` and `driveSubject` take `table` and `tenant`; the workflow editor takes `tenantClaim`; the SCIM sync log maps through `blocks.sso.tables` and `columns`.
  - Notifications deliver to adopted profiles whose locale column is an enum or a domain.
  - The attachments trigger resets the scan state of client inserts into an app table. Adopted usage history is never partitioned and needs an increasing `bigint` id. Adopted ledger balances keep the app's grants.
  - Render refuses two blocks that would create the same table, such as `blocks.idempotency.tables.leases` and `blocks.leases.tables.leases` both set to `better_supabase.leases`, and names both config keys.
- `blocks.<module>.api` now always writes a wrapper for every function the block's TypeScript client calls over a transport, also when `api.functions` lists fewer. With `"rate-limit": { api: { schema: "api", functions: ["check_rate_limit"] } }`, `checkAll` over `rpcTransport(admin, { schema: "api" })` failed with PGRST202 because `api.check_rate_limits` was missing; the module now writes it and `api.purge_rate_limits`, with the same `security invoker` body and `service_role` grant as `api.check_rate_limit`. Run `better-supabase add` or `upgrade` to rewrite the module files.

## 0.20.0 (2026-10-25)

- Block clients built from a `Db` get its Storage:

  - `db.<block>` and `db.$blocks.<block>` clients, `blockSet.client(db, key)` and `createBlockClient(db, block)` pass `db.$storage` to the block as `storage` when the `Db` has Storage, so `db.$blocks.attachments`, `db.$blocks.dataLifecycle`, ai-files and drive read, sign and remove files as the `Db`'s caller instead of failing without one. A `storage` you pass through `options` or `.with({ storage })` still wins.
  - `BlockContext`, `BlockClientOptions` and `CreateBlocksOptions` take `storage`, and the options `.using(create)` passes its creator hold it.

## 0.19.0 (2026-10-25)

- Adopting existing app tables with blocks:

  - `database.sql.tenantColumn` names the tenant column of every block table, such as `organization_id`, instead of `tenant_id`. Explicit `blocks.<name>.columns.<table>.tenant` mappings still win, a name another column of a block table has is refused, and `gen` writes the name to the block's `names` so its TypeScript client reads rows back with `tenant_id`.
  - The line tables of `quotes`, `invoices` and `deals` (`quote_lines`, `invoice_lines`, `deal_lines`) can be the app's own tables, mapped through `tables.lines` and `columns.lines`. The new `options.lineExtraColumns` adds columns to the line table that each line's `fields` writes and that line reads return under `fields`.
  - `defineSupabase(schema, { temporal }).blocks(...)` passes `temporal` to every block instance it builds.
  - `db.$apiResult(fn, args)` calls an API schema function like `db.$api` and returns a `Result` with a `DbError`, like a repository call. `db.$api` is unchanged.
  - Record types (`config.records` keys, `blocks.<name>.on` keys) may contain hyphens, such as `purchase-order`, in every block. Subject type columns and checks accept them, and triggers named after a type use underscores (`bsb_field_history_purchase_order`), so two types that differ only by `-` and `_` are refused. Doctor BS346 no longer reports a hyphenated type that `config.records` registers.

## 0.18.3 (2026-10-25)

- With an authorization provider that sets `authorization.functions.actorMayWrite`, the blocks replace calls to `better_supabase.actor_may_write()` with the provider's check but keep the function's own `create function`, `grant` and `revoke` statements. The created-by block file no longer renders `create or replace function (provider_check())`, which failed with a syntax error.

## 0.18.2 (2026-10-25)

- The profiles block works over an app profiles table whose `locale` column is an
  enum or a domain, such as `public.app_locale`. `update_my_profile` writes the
  locale through `better_supabase.as_column_type`, and a sign-up that maps a
  metadata key to `locale` writes it through the new
  `better_supabase.try_column_type`, which stores null for a value the type
  rejects instead of failing the sign-up. `upgrade` and doctor (BS342) no longer
  refuse an enum `locale`, and `add --adopt` no longer leaves it unmapped; they
  still refuse an enum in a column the block writes as text.

## 0.18.1 (2026-10-25)

- `relayRealtimeClient` retries a first join that failed instead of leaving the
  channel dead until a reload, for example while a local Realtime service starts.
  Until the channel joins, each failed attempt reports `CHANNEL_ERROR` and is
  retried with the exponential backoff up to `maxBackoffMs`; an `end` event before
  the join (`upstream_error`) goes through the same backoff instead of the fixed
  `retry:` loop. `timeoutMs`, now counted from `subscribe()`, caps this phase:
  the channel then reports `TIMED_OUT` with the status and problem detail of one
  final request, and stops.

  `realtimeRelay` no longer reports a check that can't run now as a denial. A
  throw from `authorize`, a policy check that can't reach Postgres and a session
  with a `network` or `unexpected` error (such as `JWKS_FETCH_FAILED`) end the
  stream with the new `unavailable` reason, which the client retries; a send
  answers 503. Refusals keep their 400, 401, 403 or 404.

  `better-supabase upgrade` also renames `bs_` event triggers, such as the audit
  block's `bs_audit_forget_dropped`, so a full declarative diff no longer drops
  and recreates them.

## 0.18.0 (2026-10-25)

- Close the gaps a production Expo, oRPC and MCP app hit on the blocks. See the
  0.17 to 0.18 migration guide.

  - Security: `team_ids_of(member)` refuses a signed-in caller that names another
    user, and `list_members` checks a key `blocks.organizations.permissions.listMembers`
    can remap. The profiles email mirror is stored lowercase, with a backfill.
  - The audit block takes `ipHeader`, `staffClaim` and `subjectColumns`.
  - The oRPC, Hono, `handle()` and MCP middleware take `cookies: false` for bearer
    tokens and API keys only. oRPC contexts accept `headers` and `url`.
  - `blocks.memberships.options.roles.many` lets a member hold several roles
    (memberships version 3), with `addMemberRole` and `removeMemberRole`, and
    invitations take `defaultRole` and `onAccept`.
  - Notifications (version 9) render per delivery and per locale, and take
    `actions` and `resolve({ resolution })`. Push (version 2) retries Expo sends,
    stores tickets and records receipts with `devices.checkReceipts()`.
    Profiles gain a `locale` column; `add --adopt` leaves it unmapped when the
    app's `locale` column is an enum.
  - Attachments take `offline`: client ids, an upload trigger, a cleanup queue
    with `createAttachmentSweeper`, and an `{ext}` path placeholder.
  - PowerSync: `better-supabase powersync streams` and `gen.powersync` write the
    Sync Streams and the publication migration for notifications and attachments.
    `better-supabase/powersync` adds `powersyncNotificationsTransport`,
    `notificationRecipientsRoute`, `powersyncTopicPort` and
    `attachmentQueueRoute`, and `gen` marks the other block instances as needing
    the network on native.
  - Every block types `extraColumns`, and memberships, organizations and teams type
    their nested options. Column types accept quoted defaults and casts.
  - `acceptInvitationById` and `declineInvitationById` emit `invitation.accepted`
    and `invitation.declined`. The access-requests approve key is in the typed
    permission registry, and doctor BS409 compares
    `tokenHook.activeOrganizationFrom` with `blocks.permissions.activeTenant`.

## 0.17.0 (2026-10-25)

- Add `ownTablePolicies` to keep blocks' policies off your own tables. Set `database.sql.ownTablePolicies: "app"` for every block, or `blocks.<name>.ownTablePolicies` for one, which wins. With `app`, a block that runs over a table your schema creates adds none of its `bsb_<table>_<command>` policies there, even where none of your policies repeats its condition, and no restrictive `bsb_tenant_scope` either, so your policies alone decide who reaches the rows (a platform permission that reads across organizations keeps working). It still writes its functions, triggers, indexes and grants. Its file keeps the `drop policy if exists` and a comment that your policies govern the table, so `upgrade` removes the copies an earlier install left. The default, `block`, keeps the behaviour since 0.15, and `add --adopt` suggests `app` when an adopted table already has permissive policies of its own. Doctor skips such tables in BS347, and the new BS350 (info) reports one with row level security on but no permissive policy for a command the block's policies would have granted.

  The `organizations` block adds its `stripe_customer_id text unique` column only when the entitlements block reads the Stripe customer from it: not with a `plans` or `custom` source, a configured `auth.entitlements.customer` on another column, or the managed `billing` block's customers table. Otherwise the column leaves the block's file on `upgrade`: on a managed organizations table the next declarative schema diff drops the column and its data, and `stripeCustomerId` leaves the generated models. If your app reads or writes that column, declare it in your own schema file, or set `auth.entitlements.customer` to it (`better_supabase.organizations.stripe_customer_id` for the managed table), which keeps the block adding it, before you run `upgrade` and the diff.

  When tenant and platform invitations share one table (`blocks.invitations.tables.platformInvitations` names the tenant table), the platform invitations' open-email unique index is limited to rows without a tenant, so a person can hold open invitations to several organizations at once. The block's file drops the old `platform_invitations_open_email_idx` and creates `invitations_platform_open_email_idx` in its place.

  Add `options.stamp` to the `updated-at` and `created-by` blocks: `client` (the default) keeps stamping `createdAt`, `updatedAt`, `createdBy` and `updatedBy` in the repository, and `trigger` leaves them to the `track_updated_at` and `track_actor` triggers, so tables with column-level grants that leave those columns out accept the app's writes. `gen` passes it to `updatedAtExtension({ stamp })` and `createdByExtension({ stamp })`, which still mark the columns read-only and refuse caller-supplied values without `{ override: true }`.

  The filter form `update({ where }, patch, args)` accepts `override` in its types, as the runtime already did.

## 0.16.0 (2026-10-25)

- Realtime, session checks and uploads for apps that keep the session out of the browser, and atomic multi-limit rate limiting.

  - `realtimeRelay(server, { topics, postgres })` from `better-supabase/server` serves Realtime to callers without a browser token of their own, such as a support session ("view as user") or an app with httpOnly session cookies. It checks each topic's `realtime.messages` policies as the caller (the viewed user in a support session), joins upstream with the secret key and streams broadcasts and presence as server-sent events, with heartbeats, reconnects, session expiry and an overflow limit. `POST` broadcasts for the caller and is refused in a read-only support session. `liveRelayTopic` serves the `liveQuery` channels, and `realtimeTopicAccess` runs the policy check on its own.
  - `relayRealtimeClient(url)` and `relayTopicPort(url)` from `better-supabase/realtime` are the client: `defineTopic(...).subscribe`, `send`, `liveQuery`, `watchTopic` and the hooks take it unchanged. `<BetterSupabaseProvider realtime={...}>` makes `useBroadcast`, `usePresence`, `useLiveQuery` and `useLiveCount` join through it, and `useRealtimeRelay(url)` returns one while `useSupportSession()` reports a support session. The hooks' `client` options now take any `RealtimeClient`.
  - `sessionStatusRoute(server)` from `better-supabase/server` and `useSessionWatch()` (plus the framework-free `watchSession()`) from `better-supabase/next/client` watch an open page without a browser Supabase client: on focus, visibility and an interval they report an ended, banned or signed-out session and a changed claims fingerprint (support target included), and tell the other tabs through a `BroadcastChannel`. `claimsFingerprint()` computes the fingerprint on the server.
  - `bucket.reserveResumable(target, { upsert, direct })` signs a resumable (TUS) upload and returns `tusEndpoint`, `headers` (`x-signature`, `x-upsert`), `metadata`, `bucket` and `expiresAt`. The endpoint is `<storage>/upload/resumable/sign`, on the direct `<project-ref>.storage.supabase.co` host for hosted projects; `resumableEndpoint(url)` builds it on its own. `claimReserved` and support-session checks work as for `reserve`.
  - `createRateLimit().checkAll([{ scope, key, rule }, ...])` counts one hit against several limits in one round trip through the new `check_rate_limits(hits)` function of the `rate-limit` module. The hit counts only when every limit allows it, and the decision names the limit that blocked and the longest `retryAfter`. Run `better-supabase upgrade` to add the function.

## 0.15.1 (2026-10-25)

- A block runs over a view of yours again, as `ownership: "adopt"` did in 0.14. When your schema files or the snapshot create a view or materialized view at a block table's name (its default name, or the one `blocks.<name>.tables` maps), the block writes no table DDL for it: no `create table`, added columns, constraints, indexes, row level security, grants or policies. It still writes the functions that read the view. An optional column the view lacks counts as mapped to `null`, and a required one stops `upgrade` with an error that names the view and the column. A view at the name of a table only the block keeps is still an error. Doctor reports a block whose API writes through a view Postgres can't update (BS349). `organization_member_role` now leaves out disabled memberships, like the other membership checks. `better-supabase/sql` exports `declaredViews` and `adoptedViews`.
- `codemod 0.15` no longer leaves a script running the same command twice. It rewrites both `sql sync` and `sql upgrade` to `upgrade`, so a `package.json` script or a workflow `run:` step that called both now calls `upgrade` once. Only identical commands next to each other and joined by `&&` collapse.
- `codemod 0.15` lists the lines of your own SQL that name a `bs_` object the upgrade migration renames to `bsb_`: your pgTAP tests (`sql.testsDir`), the schema files no block wrote, and your newest migration. Each line shows under "Check these by hand" with its file and line. The codemod never rewrites SQL, since a migration you pushed must not change.
- `better-supabase/server` exports `PERMISSION_CAP_CLAIM` (`bsb_permission_cap`), the claim an `authorization.automation.runAs` hook puts the permission cap under. Through 0.15, `cap_allows` and `permissionCapOf` also read the 0.14 name, `bs_permission_cap`, when the new claim is missing, so a hook or a queued job that still carries the old name keeps its cap instead of running uncapped. `runAs` warns once when it gets only the old name.

## 0.15.0 (2026-10-24)

- Framework adapters now come in two tiers. Next.js, Hono, Expo Router, SvelteKit, TanStack Start and React Router are supported adapters: they get the full test matrix, an example app and every new guide. Astro, Edge Functions, Elysia, H3, Nuxt and Nitro 2 (`better-supabase/h3/v1`), NestJS, Node and SolidStart are community adapters: they keep shipping in every release with a smoke test that serves a request, and their docs pages carry a community note. No adapter is removed and no import changes. Read more under [adapter tiers](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/frameworks#adapter-tiers) and in ADR 0094.
- Blocks declare what they give `approvals`, `bulk`, `pipelines`,
  `share-links` and `subjects` in a new `contributes` field, and those generic
  blocks no longer name their consumers. A second instance contributes to the
  generic instance its `uses` names. Two contributions with the same subject or
  action name fail at render. Run `better-supabase upgrade` and `db diff`; the
  rendered functions change only in the order of their branches.
- Block clients are keyed by instance. `gen` writes a `blocks.generated.ts`
  entry for every instance, with the block it installs, its `uses` and the
  function names its SQL writes, plus a renamed client such as `ticketsBlock`.
  `createBlocks` reads the entry under the instance key, binds a port that two
  blocks provide through the consumer's `uses` or the new `ports` option, and
  `createBlockClient` takes `instance`. Block queries use the table key space
  `["bsb", instance, method, ...args]`, so `invalidateTables` makes table and
  instance queries stale, optionally narrowed to methods.
- Blocks that attach to records take their record types from `on` on the block
  entry instead of `options.subjects`, and `field-history` takes
  `on: { <type>: { fields: [...] } }`. `better-supabase upgrade` moves the
  value. A config that still sets `options.subjects` fails with the key to move,
  and the new doctor check BS346 reports an `on` entry that `config.records`
  doesn't register.
- Breaking: 26 blocks take plain names. The block name changes in the config, the CLI, the subpath (`better-supabase/blocks/<name>`), the exports, the operation names (`<block>.<action>`) and the CloudEvent source (`better-supabase/<block>`). Table names, permission keys, event types, hint codes and queue names don't change. `better-supabase codemod 0.15` renames the config keys, the imports and the subpaths, and lists event sources for review. `better-supabase upgrade` moves the installed blocks to their new names and renames two tables that clashed: hiring's `jobs` becomes `job_openings` and catalog's `collections` becomes `product_collections`. A 0.14 name in the config stops `add` and `gen` with a message that names the codemod.

  | 0.14                 | 0.15               |
  | -------------------- | ------------------ |
  | `work-items`         | `tasks`            |
  | `parties`            | `contacts`         |
  | `engagements`        | `activities`       |
  | `targets`            | `goals`            |
  | `number-sequences`   | `numbering`        |
  | `actor`              | `created-by`       |
  | `ensure-rls`         | `rls-guard`        |
  | `shared-signals`     | `security-events`  |
  | `service-principals` | `service-accounts` |
  | `support-sessions`   | `impersonation`    |
  | `controls`           | `compliance`       |
  | `ai-tasks`           | `ai-schedules`     |
  | `ai-providers`       | `ai-keys`          |
  | `workflow-builder`   | `workflow-editor`  |
  | `jobs`               | `queue`            |
  | `access`             | `permissions`      |
  | `rules`              | `automations`      |
  | `tenant`             | `memberships`      |
  | `activity`           | `timeline`         |
  | `workflow-sdk-world` | `workflow-sdk`     |

  The docs pages move with their blocks, and `/docs/blocks/<old>` redirects to the new page.
- Breaking: the SQL engine says block. `SqlModule` is `BlockSql`, `ModuleDefinition` is `BlockSqlDefinition`, and the other `Module*` types from `better-supabase/sql` are `BlockSql*` (`BlockSqlContext`, `BlockSqlLayout`, `BlockSqlNames`). `renderModules`, `resolveModules`, `resolveModule`, `isModuleIdType`, `sameModuleFile`, `InstalledModule` and `SkippedModule` are `renderBlocks`, `resolveBlocks`, `resolveBlock`, `isBlockSqlIdType`, `sameBlockFile`, `InstalledBlock` and `SkippedBlock`.

  Four exports take their glossary names: `SubjectRef` is `RecordRef`, `entityForm` is `recordForm` (with `useRecordForm` and the `RecordForm*` types), `ShipmentCarrier` is `ShippingProvider` (`testShippingProvider`), and `QueueBackend` is `QueueProvider` (`sqlQueueProvider`, `transportQueueProvider`, `vercelQueueProvider`, `testQueueProvider`). `better-supabase codemod 0.15` renames these imports.

  Installed versions live in `better_supabase.blocks` instead of `better_supabase.modules`. `sql upgrade` creates the new table and copies the rows before the schema diff drops the old one, and doctor reads the old table while the new one is missing. Block files carry `-- @bsb-block`, `-- @bsb-block-data`, `-- @bsb-block-test`, `-- @bsb-names` and `-- @bsb-tables` lines; `sql upgrade`, `sql sync` and doctor read the `@bs-*` lines of files written by 0.14 and older.
- Breaking: the runtime instance is `bsb`, matching the CLI's short name, so `const db = await bsb.db()` replaces `bs.db()` in the docs, skills, examples, generated files and the `ctx.bsb` key on request contexts (`c.var.bsb`, `locals.bsb`, `context.bsb`). Run `better-supabase codemod 0.15` to rename the instance where a file creates, imports or destructures it; it lists cache tags and query keys that start with `bs` for review.

  Strings on the wire move with it, so deploy the new package and run `better-supabase sql upgrade` together: realtime topics start with `bsb:t:`, the request and job headers are `x-bsb-actor`, `x-bsb-job` and `x-bsb-signature`, workflow run attributes are `bsb.*`, the debug header is `x-bsb-db-calls`, the stats route is `/api/bsb-stats`, and Server-Timing names are `bsb-proxy` and `bsb-verify`. Workflow runs started before the upgrade keep their `bs.*` attributes, so failure alerts don't count them. The support session cookie is `bsb-support` and the replica cookie is `bsb-primary-until`, so staff in a support session start it again after the deploy.

  The triggers, policies, constraints, indexes and functions the blocks install are named `bsb_` instead of `bs_` (`bsb_audit`, `bsb_session_active`, `bsb_json_<table>_<column>`). `sql upgrade` renames the installed `bs_` objects in place before it applies the new block files, so nothing is created twice; an object on a table the migration role doesn't own (`auth.users`, `storage.objects`, `realtime.messages`) gets a warning naming the rename to do by hand. Doctor and the error mapper read both prefixes.

  Stored names keep `bs`, because renaming them would orphan existing data: Vault secret names (`bs:field-key:`, `bs:cred:`, `bs:signing:`, `bs:marketplace:`), the queue envelope key `$bs` and the API key prefix `bs`, which existing keys are verified against.
- Breaking: the CLI has one command per job: `init`, `add`, `remove`, `upgrade`, `blocks`, `gen`, `dev`, `db`, `doctor`, `config`, `spec`, `scaffold`, `mcp`, `codemod`, `skills` and `completion`. Running a moved command exits 2 and names the new one, and `better-supabase codemod 0.15` rewrites the calls in `package.json` scripts and GitHub workflows.

  | 0.14                          | 0.15                                          |
  | ----------------------------- | --------------------------------------------- |
  | `apply --preset <p>`          | `add --preset <p>`                            |
  | `adopt <block>`               | `add --adopt <block>`                         |
  | `sql add <block>`             | `add <block>`                                 |
  | `sql sync`, `sql upgrade`     | `upgrade`                                     |
  | `sql data`                    | `add --adopt`                                 |
  | `sql list`                    | `blocks list --installed`                     |
  | `sql print <block>`           | `blocks view <block> --sql`                   |
  | `preset decode <code>`        | `blocks list --preset <code>`                 |
  | `preset info`                 | `config --preset`                             |
  | `introspect`                  | `gen --save-snapshot`                         |
  | `seed`                        | `db seed`                                     |
  | `env`, `run`, `keys`, `stack` | `dev env`, `dev run`, `dev keys`, `dev stack` |

  `remove <block...>` is new: it takes blocks out of the config and `lib/blocks.ts`, deletes their module files and points to `db diff` for the migration that drops their tables. `add --adopt --check` exits 1 when the data migration is out of date, and a comment-only change to a data file no longer needs a new data migration. The MCP tools follow the commands: `config_preset`, `blocks_installed`, `add_preset_dry_run`, `upgrade`, `add_preset` and `remove`. The CLI docs move to `/docs/cli/dev` (env, run, keys and stack), `/docs/cli/db#seed` and `/docs/cli/gen#saving-a-snapshot`; the old pages redirect.
- Block instances live on `db`. `.blocks(blockSet)` on the definition puts each instance on the repository object (`db.comments`), running through the same executor as the tables and acting in the active organization. An instance whose name a table already uses stays at `db.$blocks.<instance>`, and `db.$api(fn, args)` calls a function in the API schema. `blocks.generated.ts` exports `blocks`, the block for each instance, so `defineBlocks(blockConfig, blocks)` lists them all.

  `gen.hooks` writes `hooks.ts` with a list and a record hook per table (`useCustomers`, `useCustomer`) for React, Vue, Svelte or Solid, built on `defineDataHooks`. The hooks share reads, update cached rows at once on `create`, `update` and `delete`, and roll back on an error. They keep reads in memory by default; `createClient(definition, { engine: queryEngine(queryClient) })` from `better-supabase/query` keeps them in TanStack Query.

  The `requireTenant` option and helper are now `requireOrganization`. Run `better-supabase codemod 0.15` to rename them; doctor reports a `requireTenant` that is left.
- Blocks stop copying tables other blocks own. Leases drops its maintenance
  requests (install a `tasks` instance named `maintenance_request` instead; the
  upgrade renames the old table to `maintenance_requests_legacy`), admission
  pass credits post on the ledger, inventory can keep stock at the assets
  locations with `tables: { locations: null }`, and donations requires
  `numbering` and contributes a `donation_receipt` sequence (`R-{n}`) that an
  app entry replaces. Blocks contribute numbering defaults through
  `contributes.numbering`. A renamed instance now reaches notifications and
  the queue under their own names, and `gen` names its events type after the
  instance (`MaintenanceEvents`) and adds it to `BlockEvents`.
- Breaking: `better-supabase.config.ts` groups its keys into `database` (`source`, `schemas`, `sql`, `seed`, `expose`, `buckets`, `realtime`, `topics`, `vectorSearch`), `auth` (`organization`, `claims`, `authorization`, `entitlements`), `gen` (`output`, `casing`, the generator `tables` overrides and the other type options), `api` (`openapi`, `specs`) and `tooling` (`doctor`, `env`, `keys`, `skills`, `scaffold`). `preset`, `blocks`, `plugins`, `records`, `tables` and `adapters` stay at the top level.

  | 0.14                     | 0.15                       |
  | ------------------------ | -------------------------- |
  | `subjects`               | `records`                  |
  | `appTables`              | `tables`                   |
  | `appTables.<name>.scope` | `tables.<name>.owner`      |
  | `integrations`           | `adapters`                 |
  | `sql.scope`              | `database.sql.owner`       |
  | `claims.tenant`          | `auth.organization.claim`  |
  | `plugins.tenant.column`  | `auth.organization.column` |
  | `blocks.<name>.mode`     | `blocks.<name>.ownership`  |
  | `blocks.<name>.scope`    | `blocks.<name>.owner`      |
  | `blocks.<name>.entities` | `blocks.<name>.names`      |
  | `blocks.<name>.hooks`    | `blocks.<name>.callbacks`  |

  `better-supabase codemod 0.15` regroups a config file, its environment blocks and a `better-supabase.config.json`. Loading a config with a 0.14 key fails with a message that names the key's new place. `resolveConfig` returns the same resolved config as before, and `flattenConfig`, `groupConfig` and `blockEntrySource` from `better-supabase/config` convert between the two shapes. `init` and `add` write the grouped keys, and CLI and doctor messages name the grouped paths. Doctor finding targets keep their 0.14 names, so saved baselines still match. Create designs keep `appTables` and `scope` in this release.
- Blocks install as instances. Each key in `blocks` names an instance; a key
  other than a block's name installs a second instance of the block in its
  `block` setting, `on` lists the record types an attachable block attaches
  to, and `uses` picks the instance of a dependency. The options of a named
  instance are typed from its block. `resolveConfig` reports unknown blocks,
  renamed or repeated core blocks, unknown `uses` keys and a missing `uses`
  together, and `ResolvedConfig.instances` lists the resolved instances. The
  config JSON schema accepts instance keys.
- Repositories and block clients share one data contract. Reads are `list`, `page`, `get`, `count` and `aggregate`; writes are `create`, `update`, `upsert` and `delete`; `attach` and `detach` link a row to a record. `findMany`, `findFirst`, `findOnly`, `findUnique`, `findById`, `paginate`, `exists` and the `*Many` methods go: `get` takes a primary key, a unique key or `{ where }` (with `{ optional: true }` for null), `create` and `upsert` take one row or an array, and `update` and `delete` take a key or `{ where }`.

  Block clients move to the same verbs. `tags.tag` and `untag` become `attach` and `detach`, `tags.create`, `update` and `list` take objects, `forSubject(record)` on tags, channels and scores becomes `list({ on: record })`, and `save` on the block clients and `set` on ai-cache and ai-files `providerFiles` become `upsert`. The lint rules `no-unbounded-find-many` and `no-delete-many-without-where` become `no-unbounded-list` and `no-empty-where`, and the guards `noUnboundedFindMany` and `noDeleteManyWithoutWhere` become `noUnboundedList` and `noDeleteWithoutWhere`.

  Run `better-supabase codemod 0.15` to rewrite the repository calls, the guard options and the lint rule names. It lists the block client calls to review.
- `better-supabase db backfill <table.column>` fills the null values of a column that a migration added to a table that already holds rows. `--from projects.organization_id via project_id` copies each value from the table a foreign key points at, and `--value "'EUR'"` writes one literal. It updates `--batch-size` rows per statement (default 1000) and locks only those rows, prints a line per batch, supports `--dry-run` and the `--json` envelope, and touches only null rows, so a second run fills nothing. Read more under [db backfill](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/cli/db#backfill).

  `doctor` adds BS236, which reports a check added `not valid` that no row fails any more, and the new `doctor --fix` flag runs `alter table ... validate constraint` for it. Against a database that is not local, an agent asks for consent first. JSON findings that `--fix` can resolve carry the SQL in a new optional `fix` field.

  The migration lock lints now follow expand and contract. BS230 flags a `references` or `check` written inline in `add column`, BS231 flags `add column ... not null` without a default, and BS233 no longer asks for `lock_timeout` before a statement that only runs `validate constraint`.
- Breaking: designs replace preset codes. The config's `preset` key takes a preset name or an object with the conventions that differ from the defaults and the blocks, a Create URL is `/create?preset=<name>` or `/create?design=<code>` with the preset inside the design (format 7), and a design file is the design JSON with its preset. `init`, `add` and `blocks list` stop on a 0.14 code and name `better-supabase codemod 0.15`, which converts the config's code and `*.design.json` files.

  | 0.14                                         | 0.15                                                    |
  | -------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------- |
  | `encodePreset(preset)`                       | `presetSpec(preset)` or `createUrl(resolved)`           |
  | `decodePreset(code)`                         | `presetAndDesignOf(input)` or `presetOfSpec(value)`     |
  | `defaultPresetCode()`                        | `defaultPreset()`                                       |
  | `withPresetField(preset, key, value)`        | `presetFromSpec({ ...presetSpec(preset), key: value })` |
  | `designConfigSource(resolved, design, code)` | `presetConfigSource(resolved, design)` (async)          |
  | `designInstallCommand(resolved, code)`       | `presetInstallCommand(resolved, design)` (async)        |
  | `designApplyCommand(resolved, code)`         | `presetApplyCommand(resolved, design)` (async)          |

  `isPresetCode`, `PRESET_BLOCKS`, `PRESET_FIELDS`, `PRESET_VERSION` and `DecodedPreset` are removed; `CONVENTION_FIELDS` lists the convention fields. The docs page `/docs/presets/codes` moves to `/docs/presets/designs`.
- A `tables.<name>` entry can now name a table your own schema already creates. Instead of refusing it, better-supabase enriches the table: it skips `create table` and adds only the columns the table lacks (the scope column, the timestamps, the id when it has none, and the columns you list) with `alter table ... add column if not exists`, nullable unless they have a default. Enriching never overwrites what your schema wrote: the scope's policies are named `bsb_<table>_<command>` on your table, so a policy of yours named `<table>_select` stays, and your grants stay too, since the entry only adds the grants its scope needs. The scope column gets a `not valid` not-null check, so new rows must fill it while `better-supabase db backfill` fills the old ones. Row level security, the scope's grants and policies, the restrictive `bsb_tenant_scope` policy, subject permissions, events, the `updated_at` trigger, versions, read audits, rollups and lookups render the same as for a table it creates. Each feature works on its own, so `scope: "none"` with only `subject` or only `events` adds no scope column.

  Doctor adds two checks. BS237 reports permissive policies of your own on a scoped app table (a hint when `bsb_tenant_scope` narrows them, a warning in `user` scope) and the grants on an enriched table beyond its scope (a warning when they reach columns the entry restricts). BS238 counts the rows of an enriched table without an owner and names the `db backfill` command. BS228 no longer reports a required column without a default that an enriched table keeps nullable.

  `gen` filters a table on the tenant column its subject names (`subjects.<type>.tenant`), so a block over your table with its own tenant column gets the memberships filter too.
- Every block that owns tables now declares the nouns it names its tables, indexes, policies, triggers, functions and events after, so `blocks.<name>.entities` renames them. That covers 43 more blocks, among them `quotes`, `invoices`, `orders`, `inventory`, `bookings`, `forms`, `pipelines`, `hiring`, `ai-chat`, `knowledge`, `agents`, `teams`, `tags`, `templates` and `automations`. Quotes, invoices, orders, bookings and forms are also record types, so comments, attachments, tags and activity can point at them.

  Run `better-supabase upgrade` after updating. The new record types widen the subject switches in the blocks that read record titles, and three internal keys move: the orders advisory lock key, the relations lock key and the `better_supabase.restoring_version` setting that `versions` sets while it restores a row.

  A config that declares `records.quote`, `records.invoice`, `records.order`, `records.booking` or `records.form` now fails. Remove the entry when it points at the block's own table, or set `blocks.<name>.names.<noun>.subject` to `false` to keep a table of your own.
- The per-block framework subpaths go: `better-supabase/blocks/<block>/react`, `/vue`, `/svelte` and `/solid` (120 subpaths) and `better-supabase/chat-sdk/react`. A block's React hooks are now a registry item that shadcn copies into the app: `npx shadcn add @better-supabase/use-<block>` writes `hooks/use-<block>.ts`, with the same hook names, options and return values, and the app imports from `@/hooks/use-<block>`. Presets install the hook item with each block.

  Vue, Svelte and Solid call `useBlockController(create<Name>Controller, options)` from `better-supabase/vue`, `better-supabase/svelte` or `better-supabase/solid`, with the controller from the block's main entry. It returns what the per-block hooks returned. `better-supabase/react` now exports `useController`, `useLatest`, `useBlockEnv` and `useBlockTenant` for your own hooks, and `better-supabase/blocks` exports `envBlockOptions`, `envBlockSettings` and the `BlockEnv` type.

  Doctor's BS418 finds imports of the removed subpaths and names the registry item.
- Sessions verify once per request across cache scopes, and the Next.js proxy and sign-in take fewer round trips.

  - `createServer` keeps a session memo keyed by the token's hash. `server.snapshot(request)` returns a verified `SessionSnapshot`, and `server.contextFromSession(snapshot)` builds a context from it with no cookie read, no network call and no second verification. In Next.js, `bsb.getRequestSession()` returns the snapshot, so ten `'use cache: private'` islands verify the token once. `sessionCacheKey`, `sessionIdTag`, `sessionMemo` and `bsb.sessions.stats()` are new; `createNext(..., { sessions })` sizes the memo or turns it off. See the [island pattern](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/frameworks/next-islands) and ADR 0095.
  - `contextFromSession` accepts only a snapshot the server made: a copy (a spread, `structuredClone`, a cached function's arguments) gets the `invalid` context. Pass plain values into cached functions.
  - The proxy refreshes a token only within `refreshSkewSeconds` of its expiry (`auth.leeway`, else 60), stores a refreshed session in the memo, and treats `refresh_token_already_used` as `raced`: it keeps the cookies the other request set instead of signing the user out.
  - `serverTiming: true` now reports `bsb-auth` for resolving the session and `bsb-parse`, `bsb-verify`, `bsb-refresh` and `bsb-ended` per phase. `bsb-verify` used to be the whole resolution; read `bsb-auth` for that number now.
  - `isPrefetch` also recognizes `next-router-segment-prefetch`, `x-middleware-prefetch` and `x-moz: prefetch`. Expo's `bsb.middleware()` and API routes no longer refresh on a prefetch, like the other adapters.
  - `bsb.signIn({ email, password, next })` signs in from a Server Action in one Auth call, writes the cookies, stores the session for the next render and returns a same-origin `redirectTo` (and `mfa`), so the form calls `router.replace` once. `safeRedirectPath` and `passwordGrant` are exported from `better-supabase/server`.
  - `AuthSession` and `SessionSnapshot` gain `issuedAt` (from `iat`) and `authzVersion` (from an `authz_ver` claim, or `null`).
  - `createServer(def, { versions })` takes an authorization library's version cache. Authorizers get `{ versions, memo }` as a runtime, `invalidateSession(userId)` evicts the user from it, and `subscribeAuthzVersions({ client, topic, event, userIdKey, versions })` evicts users when the database broadcasts a change on a private Realtime channel. See [the version cache](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/extending/authorization-providers#version-cache) and ADR 0096.
  - SvelteKit, TanStack Start, React Router, Hono (`c.var.snapshot`) and Expo hand loaders and handlers the snapshot.
- Installed blocks now work together where they overlap. `ai-chat` records each
  run's tokens on the `ai.input_tokens` and `ai.output_tokens` meters when
  `usage` is installed (turn it off with `meterUsage: false`). `knowledge`
  queues ingestion through `queue` (`ingest.queue` and `ingestJob()`). With
  `invoices` installed, a paid order creates, issues and pays an invoice and
  returns its id. Quote lines that name a catalog variant take its price and
  title when they leave them out. Time entries and leave requests carry an
  `employee_id` when `employees` is installed. `attachments` takes a
  `publicBuckets` option and `Attachments.publicUrl()` for files anyone may
  read. The registry adds `@better-supabase/ai-chat-next`, the AI chat route
  with its stream and stop routes.
- PowerSync, SQLite and TanStack DB run as engines behind the generated data hooks. `powersyncEngine(powersync, { connector, local })` from `better-supabase/powersync`, `sqliteEngine(database)` from the new `better-supabase/sqlite` (Expo SQLite through `expoSqliteDatabase`, Supabase Lite through `liteDatabase`) and `tanstackDbEngine({ collections })` from `better-supabase/tanstack-db` go to `createClient` or `createNativeClient`, so native screens read `useCustomers(...)` like web screens. With a device engine, `bsb.db` runs on the local database for the tables in `local` and on PostgREST for the rest. `useSyncStatus()` and `useConflicts()` move to `better-supabase/react` and read the engine.

  `memoryEngine` and `queryEngine` take `live`, a change feed such as `realtimeChanges(betterSupabase, supabase)` from `better-supabase/realtime`, and `tables` to map a hook name to its tables. `where` on every hook and repository takes a filter tree from a list query or saved view, checked against the filterable columns.

  Breaking: `better-supabase/powersync/react` (`useWatch`), `better-supabase/expo-sqlite` (`expoSqliteExecutor`) and `better-supabase/tanstack-db/supabase` (`supabaseCollection`) are removed, along with `watch` and `sqliteTables`, and `@supabase-labs/tanstack-db` is no longer a peer. Doctor reports what is left as BS418. See the 0.14 to 0.15 guide.
- `defineApi` publishes every table and every block instance by default. Without `resources`, each table gets its CRUD operations; a map now publishes only the tables it names, and `resources: false` publishes none. Each table also gets the contract verbs `page`, `count`, `aggregate` and `upsert` as `POST /<table>/<verb>` (operation ids such as `countCustomers`), served by `defineResource`, `bsb.resource` and `bsb.resources` too; `verbs` limits them, and a write hook drops `upsert`. Each block instance gets `POST /<instance>/<method>` per client method, configured with the new `blocks` option (`verbs`, `path`, `tag`, `permissions`).

  `createOrpc(betterSupabase).router(api, { guard, meta })` serves that model as oRPC procedures, with routes that match the document. `bsb.blockRouter` is removed: serve `bsb.router(defineApi(betterSupabase, options))` instead. Doctor reports BS418 for a `blockRouter` call that is left.
- One automation model. Automations, workflow action steps and sequence action steps run actions from
  one registry: `defineAction` declares an action (`apiVersion: 1`, with an optional config schema)
  and `createActionRegistry` collects them; `createRuleDispatcher` takes `{ actions }` instead of
  `handlers`. The action hints are now `ACTION_UNKNOWN` and `ACTION_CONFIG_INVALID`.

  `better-supabase/flows` is removed. Blocks ship default automations instead: the inbox opens a task
  for each new conversation, hiring preboards each hired candidate and quotes orders each accepted
  quote (turned off). Each organization gets its own copy, which it may turn off, edit or reset with
  `resetDefault`. The conversions are actions of the target block (`taskFromConversationAction`,
  `employeeFromApplicationAction`, `orderFromQuoteAction`). `createRuleSink` falls back to
  `eventTarget(event)` when `load` returns nothing.

  Workflow definitions lose their event triggers (`triggers.onEvent`, `builder.sink()`); an
  automation's `workflow` action starts them. Block timers (SLA checks, sequence waits, AI schedules,
  workflow schedules) run on the queue through `blockSchedules` and `blockTickHandler`. Doctor
  BS418 names each removed API and its replacement.
- `better-supabase/spec` is the one entry for API documents. It exports the
  OpenAPI, AsyncAPI, Arazzo and Overlay renderers (`createOpenApi`,
  `createAsyncApi`, `createArazzo`, `defineWorkflow`, `applyOverlays`,
  `defineOverlay` and the rest) next to `defineApi`. The subpaths
  `better-supabase/openapi`, `better-supabase/asyncapi`,
  `better-supabase/arazzo` and `better-supabase/overlay` are removed: import
  the same names from `better-supabase/spec`. `better-supabase codemod 0.15`
  rewrites the imports, and doctor reports BS418 for any that are left.
- Breaking: `ownership: "adopt"` is removed, because blocks now find your tables themselves.
