  | `comments.counts(organizationId, subjectType, subjectIds)`, keyed by id                               | `comments.counts(subjects)`, keyed `type:id`                                                                                    |
  | `comments.copy(organizationId, from, to)`                                                             | `comments.copy(from, to)`                                                                                                       |
  | `comments.history(organizationId, subject?, options?)`                                                | `comments.history(subject?, options?)`                                                                                          |
  | `attachments.upload({ organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, ... })`                                 | `attachments.upload(subject \| null, attachment)`                                                                               |
  | `attachments.put({ organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, ... }, file)`                              | `attachments.put(subject \| null, attachment, file)`                                                                            |
  | `attachments.list(organizationId, subjectType?, subjectId?)`                                          | `attachments.list(subject?)`                                                                                                    |
  | `versions.snapshot({ organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, ... })`                                  | `versions.snapshot(subject, input?)`                                                                                            |
  | `versions.list(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, options?)`                                     | `versions.list(subject, options?)`                                                                                              |
  | `shareLinks.create(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, options?)`                                 | `shareLinks.create(subject, options?)`                                                                                          |
  | `shareLinks.recordView(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, viewerKind, viewer?)`                  | `shareLinks.recordView(subject, viewerKind, viewer?)`                                                                           |
  | `shareLinks.list(subjectType, subjectId)`, `setState(subjectType, subjectId, starred)`                | `shareLinks.list(subject)`, `setState(subject, starred)`                                                                        |
  | `tags.create(organizationId, kind, name, ...)`, `tags.list(organizationId, kind?)`                    | `tags.create(kind, name, ...)`, `tags.list(kind?)`                                                                              |
  | `tags.tag(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, tagId)`, and `untag`                                | `tags.tag(subject, tagId)`, and `untag`                                                                                         |
  | `tags.forSubject(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId)`                                             | `tags.forSubject(subject)`                                                                                                      |
  | `reactions.react(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, emoji, options?)`                            | `reactions.react(subject, emoji, options?)`                                                                                     |
  | `reactions.summary(organizationId, subjectType, subjectIds)`, keyed by id                             | `reactions.summary(subjects)`, keyed `type:id`                                                                                  |
  | `reactions.customEmoji(organizationId)`, `saveCustomEmoji(organizationId, input)`                     | `reactions.customEmoji()`, `saveCustomEmoji(input)`                                                                             |
  | `customFields.listDefinitions(organizationId, entity)`, `saveDefinition(organizationId, entity, ...)` | `customFields.listDefinitions(entity)`, `saveDefinition(entity, ...)`                                                           |
  | `customFields.readValues(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId)`                                     | `customFields.readValues(subject)`                                                                                              |
  | `customFields.setValue(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, definitionId, value)`                  | `customFields.setValue(subject, definitionId, value)`                                                                           |
  | `relations.link({ organizationId, source, target, kind, ... })`, and `unlink`                         | `relations.link(source, target, { kind, data?, fields? })`, and `unlink(source, target, { kind })`                              |
  | `relations.of(organizationId, subject, kind?)`, `edges(organizationId, kind)`                         | `relations.of(subject, kind?)`, `edges(kind)`                                                                                   |
  | `relations.ancestors(organizationId, kind, subject)`, and `descendants`                               | `relations.ancestors(subject, kind)`, and `descendants`                                                                         |
  | `relations.rollup({ organizationId, subject, ... })`                                                  | `relations.rollup(subject, { kind, of, agg, column? })`                                                                         |
  | `relations.convertedInto(organizationId, origin, intoType)`, `lineage(organizationId, subject)`       | `relations.convertedInto(origin, intoType)`, `lineage(subject)`                                                                 |
  | `sla.savePolicy(organizationId, policy)`                                                              | `sla.savePolicy(policy)`                                                                                                        |
  | `approvals.request({ organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, action, ... })`                          | `approvals.request(subject, { action, reason?, payload? })`                                                                     |
  | `approvals.list(organizationId, options?)`                                                            | `approvals.list(options?)`, with an optional `subject` filter                                                                   |
  | `approvals.isApproved(subjectType, subjectId, action)`                                                | `approvals.isApproved(subject, action)`                                                                                         |
  | `stateMachine.transition(subjectType, subjectId, to, reason?)`                                        | `stateMachine.transition(subject, to, reason?)`, and the same for `pause`, `resume`, `setFlag`, `state`, `history` and `stages` |
  | `reminders.save(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, offsetMinutes?, fields?)`                     | `reminders.save(subject, offsetMinutes?, fields?)`                                                                              |
  | `reminders.list(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId)`                                              | `reminders.list(subject)`                                                                                                       |
  | `timeTracking.start(organizationId, fields?)`, `stop(organizationId)`, `running(organizationId)`      | `timeTracking.start(fields?)`, `stop()`, `running()`                                                                            |
  | `timeTracking.log`, `list`, `timesheet`, `submit` and `timesheets` with `organizationId` first        | the same calls without it; entries take `subject` instead of `subjectType` and `subjectId`                                      |
  | `row.subjectType`, `row.subjectId`                                                                    | `row.subject.type`, `row.subject.id`                                                                                            |
  | `useComments({ organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, schema?, topic?, limit? })`                    | `useComments({ subject, topic?, limit? })`                                                                                      |
  | `useReactions({ organizationId, subjectType, subjectIds, schema?, topic? })`                          | `useReactions({ subjects, topic? })`                                                                                            |
  | `useTagInput({ organizationId, subjectType, subjectId, kind, schema? })`                              | `useTagInput({ subject, kind })`                                                                                                |

  To upgrade:

  1. Pass `tenant` to blocks you create yourself (`createComments({ transport, tenant: () => ctx.tenant })`), or build them with `createBlockClient` or `blockSet.client`, which pass the request's tenant.
  2. Replace each `organizationId, subjectType, subjectId` argument list with one `{ type, id }` object, and read `row.subject` instead of `row.subjectType` and `row.subjectId`. Read batch results by `` `${type}:${id}` ``.
  3. Pass `blocks: blockConfig` to the browser `createClient` and drop `schema` from the hooks.
  4. Run `better-supabase sql sync`, then generate a migration, for the comments and attachments author functions.
- Attachments keep versions and show image previews, and work items get checklists, repeat rules, watchers and attachments. See [Attachments](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/blocks/attachments) and [Work items](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/blocks/work-items).

  Run `better-supabase sql upgrade` (or `sql sync` and a new migration): `create_attachment` takes three more arguments, so the upgrade drops and recreates it.

  - `attachments.replace(id, file)` uploads a new file under the same attachment and keeps the current one as an earlier version. `versions(id)` lists them and `restore(id, version)` makes one current again. Both emit `attachment.replaced` and `attachment.restored`.
  - Attachments record `width`, `height` and `pages`, and `attachments.images(options)` signs resized URLs for `useImage`, behind the scan gate.
  - `createAttachmentUploadController` and `useAttachmentUpload` in `better-supabase/blocks/attachments/react`, `/vue`, `/svelte` and `/solid` upload files side by side with progress, wait for the scan, and retry or dismiss a failed upload.
  - Work items take `attachmentIds` on `create` and an `attachments` change on `update`, which link files the caller uploaded without a subject.
  - Work items have checklists: `saveChecklistItem`, `removeChecklistItem` and `checklist`, and each item carries `checklist: { total, done }`. `get(id)` reads one item. The `checklist` section (`workItemChecklistSection`, with the `workItemChecklistOptimistic` reducer) shows the steps on the item's page.
  - With the reminders block installed, `setRecurrence(id, { rrule, tzid })` gives an item a repeat rule, and `workItemRecurrenceSink(workItems)` creates the next item when a repeating one is completed. `repeat(id, dueAt)` runs once per item, so a redelivered event changes nothing.
  - With the notifications block installed, `watch(id, level)` and `watchers(id)` follow an item through notification subscriptions.
- Add AI thread summaries to the messaging block, without tying it to an SDK.

  - `messaging.summarize(target, summarizer, { force, maxMessages })` summarizes a conversation (`{ conversationId }`) or a thread (`{ threadId }`) from the messages the caller can read, leaving out internal notes and deleted messages, and saves it as the service role. `messaging.summary(target)` reads it. A saved summary is `stale` once a message in its scope is sent after it, edited or deleted, and `summarize` returns a current one without calling the model.
  - The summarizer is a versioned `ThreadSummarizer` (`apiVersion: 1`). `summarizeWith(model, { providers, instructions, gateway, maxOutputTokens })` from `better-supabase/ai-sdk` provides one over `generateText` with an AI Gateway model string, tags the spend with the tenant and the `summary` feature, and sends the tenant's own keys as BYOK when you pass the ai-providers block.
  - `createMessaging` takes an optional `service` transport for the summary writes; `createBlocks` passes it.

  Run `better-supabase sql sync` and generate a migration to get the `thread_summaries` table and the `thread_summary` and `save_thread_summary` functions.
- Rename a block's entities once in the config and get typed config keys, renamed clients, automatic subjects, generated name constants, input schemas, block hooks and server helpers. See [Typed blocks](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/blocks/typed-blocks).

  This release breaks the config and a few exports. Update them as follows:

  | Before                                                                                                                  | After                                                                                                                                                |
  | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | `sql: { naming: { tenant: "workspace" } }`                                                                              | `blocks: { tenant: { entities: { organization: "workspace" } } }`                                                                                    |
  | `workItemsSubjects()`, `dealsSubjects()`, `partiesSubjects()`, `catalogSubjects()`, `employeesSubjects()` in `subjects` | nothing: installed blocks register their entities as subjects. Remove the spread.                                                                    |
  | the helpers' `url` option                                                                                               | `blocks.<name>.entities.<entity>.url`, such as `catalog: { entities: { product: { url: "/products/{id}" } } }`                                       |
  | `CatalogSubjectsOptions`, `SqlNaming`                                                                                   | `EntityConfig`, `EntitiesConfig`                                                                                                                     |
  | `blockConfig[name].naming` in `blocks.generated.ts`                                                                     | `blockConfig[name].names` (run `better-supabase gen`)                                                                                                |
  | `work_item` methods, types and events after `entities: { work_item: "task" }`                                           | `saveWorkItem` becomes `saveTask`, `workItemId` becomes `taskId`, `work_item.created` becomes `task.created`, `work_items.read` becomes `tasks.read` |

  A `subjects` entry with the name of a block entity is now an error, and changing `entities` on an installed block makes `sql upgrade` refuse.

  - `blocks.<name>.entities` renames a block's nouns in its tables, columns, functions, events, permission keys, subjects and generated types. Every config key under `blocks` is typed from the block's names, and `defineConfig` keeps the config's literal types.
  - `renameBlock(block, names)` in `better-supabase/blocks` renames a block client's methods, argument keys, result keys and events. `gen` writes `tasksBlock` and type aliases such as `Task` for each renamed block.
  - `blocks.generated.ts` also exports `permissions`, `PermissionKey`, `RoleName`, `blockEvents`, `urls`, `topics` and `paths`.
  - The deals, parties, work-items, employees and catalog blocks export Standard Schema input schemas, such as `workItemInputSchema`, `workItemChangeSchema` and `workItemListFilterSchema`.
  - The `zod`, `valibot`, `standardSchema` and `jsonSchema` generators write schemas for SQL functions (`<function>Args`, `<function>Result`) and for block entities (`<entity>Input`, `<entity>Change`, `<entity>ListFilter`). `fieldMeta: true` adds labels, required flags, enum options and check bounds.
  - `blockQueries(blocks)` and `invalidateOnBlockEvents` in `better-supabase/query` make TanStack Query options for every block method and invalidate reads when a block event arrives.
  - `useBlockQuery`, `useBlockMutation` and `useBlockEvent` in `better-supabase/react`, `/vue`, `/svelte` and `/solid` work with any block client, renamed or not.
  - `bs.blockAction(client, method, options)` in `better-supabase/next` and `blockRouter(client, options)` in `better-supabase/orpc` expose block methods as server actions and oRPC procedures.
  - The `nuqs()` generator writes typed search params for your tables, and the `factories()` generator writes test factories, with `defineFactory` and `factoryTime` in `better-supabase/testing`.
- `better-supabase/preset` adds 17 vertical presets in a new `verticals` group (`PRESET_GROUPS.verticals`), so `/create` and `init` list them under Verticals: `property`, `field-service`, `clinic`, `lms`, `events`, `legal`, `spend`, `contracts`, `customer-success`, `logistics`, `pos`, `fitness`, `nonprofit`, `itsm`, `compliance`, `construction` and `claims`. Each one is `saas` plus the blocks of one kind of business app, such as leases on the ledger for property management, a dispatch board for field service or controls with recurring evidence for compliance, and each has a page under [Presets](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/presets). The `clinic` preset installs `field-encryption`; its page shows the config for encrypted columns and read audits, which name your own tables and so stay in your config. Existing presets and preset codes keep their blocks.
- Security fix: a record's conversation and a channel linked to a record stay in the record's tenant. `open_conversation` with kind `subject` (and so `create_comment`) and `create_channel` with `subjectType` and `subjectId` checked only that the record exists and that the caller holds its read permission in the record's own tenant, and they read the record past its table's policies. A member of two tenants could post a comment in one tenant on a record of the other, and any member could when the record type has no read permission in `config.subjects`. Both now refuse a record whose tenant column in `config.subjects` names another tenant, with `MESSAGING_NOT_FOUND` or `CHANNELS_NOT_FOUND`, for the service role as well. Types with `tenant: false` are not compared.

## 0.13.0 (2026-10-21)

- Structured, non-throwing design APIs, a provider-neutral OAuth subpath, deploy records and a doctor rule for dropped library functions. See [From 0.11 to 0.12](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/migration/0.11-to-0.12).

  - `parseDesign`, `decodeDesign`, `designFileSource` and `readDesignFile` in `better-supabase/preset` throw a `DesignParseError` with a stable `code` and a JSON Pointer `path`. `safeParseDesign` and `safeDesignFileSource` return `{ ok: false, issues }` instead of throwing. `DesignTooLongError` is a `DesignParseError` with the code `too_long`.
  - `DesignIssue` from `checkDesign` gains `code` and `path`, and a successful `DesignCheck` gains `fitsUrl`.
  - `designSchema` is `parseDesign` as a Standard Schema, and `schemas/design-v1.json` publishes the design as JSON Schema.
  - `DEFAULT_PRESET_NAME`, `defaultPreset()` and `defaultPresetCode()` name the preset Create and `init` start from.
  - `better-supabase/create/oauth` holds PKCE, sealed cookies and an `OAuthSession` shape for any OAuth provider. `seal` and `unseal` take an optional HKDF `info`. `better-supabase/create/supabase` re-exports them, and `SupabaseSession` and `isSession` stay as aliases.
  - `newDeployId()`, `deployQuery(files, { id })` and `DeployRecord` in `better-supabase/create` let a host app match a project to the preset it deployed. `DeployResponse` gains `id`, `preset` and `design`.
  - Doctor's BS235 warns when an unapplied migration drops a `better_supabase` function without creating it again with the same number of arguments, as a schema diff did for `audit()` in 0.11.
- `codemod 0.9` now pins `defaultFormat: "standard"` on an adopted
  `webhooks-out` block that maps no `columns.endpoints.format` and sets no
  `defaultFormat`, which keeps the 0.8 body, and lists `sql add` calls in
  scripts and workflows for review (`add` also wires clients). A new
  `codemod 0.11` pins `defaultFormat: "cloudevents"` under the same conditions,
  since adopt mode requires the key from 0.11. Both list the edit for review.
- Add `better-supabase db diff`, which turns the declarative schema into a migration with the Supabase CLI's command for the workflow and, when the shadow database can't start because no System V shared memory segment is left, diffs inside the running stack instead. `--in-stack` skips the shadow database, `-f <name>` writes the migration, and `$BSB_PG_BIN` names the directory with `pg_dump` and `psql` when the CLI can't find them next to the postmaster.
- Add doctor checks for the machine's System V shared memory, which every local stack and shadow database needs.

  - BS700 warns when 80% of the segment limit is in use (32 on macOS) and names the stacks that hold segments, so `supabase db diff` failing with `could not create shared memory segment` points at `stack prune`.
  - BS701 warns when a stack holds more than its one interlock segment, which means its Postgres sets `shared_memory_type` or `dynamic_shared_memory_type` to `sysv`.
- `doctor`, `gen`, `db query` and `seed --apply` no longer guess port 54322 for the local stack. When `supabase/config.toml` sets no `[db] port` and `supabase status` fails, they stop with `not_found` and name both sources, so a per-worktree stack (`supabase start --runtime native`) can't reach another project's database on 54322. `--stack <name>` (or `$BSB_STACK`) reads `supabase status --stack <name>` when `supabase/stacks/<name>/config.toml` sets no port.
- A `permission` on an action, route, loader or oRPC middleware can now be checked against the row it acts on. Pass `resource: (input, ctx) => AuthorizationResource` next to `permission`: it runs after the session and `requireTenant` checks, gets the validated input and the request's context (`db`, `session`, `tenant`), and its return value replaces the default `{ type: "action" }` or `{ type: "route" }` resource. A loader that throws refuses the caller: a `DbException` keeps its error (such as `not_found`), and any other throw refuses with `forbidden` and `code: "RESOURCE_UNAVAILABLE"`. Every adapter supports it, and `bs.middleware()` in `better-supabase/orpc` now passes the procedure's input to the checks. The loader's context type is exported as `ResourceLoaderContext`.
- Add `better-supabase stack ls` and `stack prune` for the Supabase CLI's managed stacks.

  - `stack ls` lists every stack with its branch, project root and running state, marks stacks whose worktree or branch is gone, and counts the System V shared memory segments each one holds against the system limit.
  - `stack prune` destroys the stacks whose worktree or branch is gone, never the one for this checkout. `--stop-idle` also stops the running stacks of other branches, and `--dry-run` prints the plan. Destroying a stack needs consent in agent mode.
  - The command manifest has a new effect kind, `stacks`, with `read` or `write` access, in `schemas/cli-commands-v1.json`.
- Crop, show and download stored images through Storage image transformations. See [Image transformations](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/platform/storage#image-transformations).

  - `defineBucket` takes `variants`, named transforms such as `thumb: { width: 160, height: 160, resize: "cover" }`. `renderUrl`, `signedUrl` and `publicUrl` accept a variant name wherever they took a transform, and the names are typed. `defineBucket` refuses a side outside 1 to 2500 or a quality outside 20 to 100.
  - `avatarBucket()` and `organizationLogoBucket()` come with `sm`, `md` and `lg` variants: center-cropped squares of 48, 96 and 256 for avatars, and squares of 64, 128 and 256 that fit the whole logo. Pass `variants` to replace them.
  - `useImage(bucket, target, variant)` in `better-supabase/react` returns `src`, a `srcSet` at 1x and 2x, `width` and `height`. Public buckets build the URLs while rendering; private buckets sign them and sign again before they expire.
  - `storageImageSrcSet({ url })` in `better-supabase/storage` builds a `srcset` for a public Storage URL.
  - New types: `ImageTransform`, `ImageVariant`, `ImageSource`, `StorageImage` and `UseImageOptions`.
  - Fix: the `better-supabase/next/image` loader and `storageImageUrl` keep a crop's shape. When `src` carries a `width` and `height`, the height scales with each requested width, so a square crop stays square. A size over 2500 now shrinks both sides by the same factor instead of clamping the width alone.
- Add `authorization.functions.teamIdsOf`, a template that returns the team ids
  a user belongs to. Under the `provider` access model, `team_ids_of()` in the
  `teams` block returns its set instead of reading `team_members`, and
  `saved-views` and `collections` share with those teams even without the
  `teams` block. Doctor BS417 warns when the provider declares a `team` scope,
  one of those blocks is installed, and the template is unset.
- Seed each tenant with the same demo rows. `defineTenantSeed(betterSupabase, (tenant) => fixtures)` in `better-supabase/testing` builds typed fixtures from a tenant id, and `better-supabase seed --tenant <slug>` (or `--tenant '*'`) inserts them for organizations from the organizations block, all in one transaction. The file comes from the new `seed.perTenant` config key (`supabase/seed.tenant.ts` by default). `seedTenantOnCreate(betterSupabase, seed, { sql })` applies the seed to each organization created in development; it runs after the create call and never changes its result.
- Add `codecs: { timestamptz: "utc" }`. `timestamptz` columns stay `string`,
  but every value is rewritten in UTC as ISO 8601 with a `Z` and keeps all its
  fraction digits (`2026-10-21 07:10:00.123456+02` reads as
  `2026-10-21T05:10:00.123456Z`). `infinity` passes through. The default stays
  `"string"`.
- The Vercel Marketplace block serves the Partner API. `marketplace.partnerApi({ resources, plans })` returns one handler for the installation, resource, secret rotation and plan routes. It verifies Vercel's OIDC token (signature against `/.well-known/jwks`, issuer, audience and expiry) and requires its `installation_id` to match the path, answering 401 or 403 as Problem Details. The installation's access token is sealed in Vault, so `accessToken` is now optional. Tenants are tracked per installation and resource, and an empty tenant the integration created is removed with it. A rotation revokes the previous secrets only after the new ones are issued.

  `reportUsage` now sends one request per billing period, with the period total as `periodValue` and the part not reported before as `dayValue`, and no longer sends `planValue`. It moves the reported amount only after Vercel answers 2xx. `marketplace.usageRoute({ secret, usage })` runs it from a cron caller. The SSO exchange reads keys from `https://marketplace.vercel.com/.well-known/jwks`, the path Vercel's discovery document names. Run `better-supabase gen` to apply the module's version 2 upgrade.
- Add `withClaims(postgres, claims, fn)` to `better-supabase/testing`. It runs
  raw SQL as the user the claims describe (`role` and `request.jwt.claims` are
  set, so `auth.uid()`, `auth.jwt()` and RLS apply) inside one transaction that
  always rolls back.
- Two SQL fixes. See [From 0.11 to 0.12](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/migration/0.11-to-0.12) for what to check.

  - `better_supabase.acting_member()` is granted to `service_role` only; `anon` and `authenticated` get `permission denied` instead of an RPC that returned null. `better_supabase.set_actor()` is `security definer`, so the trigger still records the actor for every writer. Run `sql sync` to get the new grant in a migration. If your own SQL calls `acting_member()`, run it as `service_role` or from a `security definer` function.
  - Module file headers no longer carry the package version, so a release doesn't rewrite every module file. The `@bs-module` marker keeps the module version, and a header written by an older release still counts as current. `ModuleLayout.version` is deprecated.
- The Record grid recipe, on the UI recipes page and in the skill reference, now notes that a copy of `@reui/data-grid` installed from ReUI 2.8.0 or earlier doesn't repaint after an edit that keeps the row count. Reinstall it with `npx shadcn add @reui/data-grid --overwrite`, or add `table.options.data` to the dependency list of the `value` memo in `DataGridProvider`.
- Security fix for support session upload claims. In 0.12.0, `claimSignedUpload`, `claimReserved` and the blocks' `confirm` and `commit` claimed any object without an owner on a path the target may insert into. That could include a file the service role stored earlier, such as an export or an import, which the claim then handed to the target and stamped with the session. A claim now only takes an object without an owner whose `updated_at` is at or after the session's `startedAt`, and in a versioned bucket only the current version. Older objects are refused with `403` `AccessDenied`. An upsert reservation that replaces such an object during the session can still be claimed.

  Required change for direct callers: `SupportStorageOptions.session` now needs `startedAt`. The server already passes the whole `SupportSession`; pass it too when you call `supportStorage` yourself. `uploadToSignedUrl` on the server removes what it stored when the claim fails or is refused. A refused claim after a browser upload, including `upload` with `onProgress`, removes nothing, because the claim can't tell that upload from another object without an owner; `sweep` removes what is never claimed. A claim of an object the target already owns now succeeds even when it is older than the session. The impersonation page documents the limits of the claim's insert rehearsal and how to sweep uploads that are never claimed.

## 0.12.0 (2026-10-20)

- Check a Create design in one call, and hand designs too large for a URL to `init` as a file.

  - `checkDesign(input, preset)` in `better-supabase/create` checks a design object, design code or
    `/create` URL against a preset with every rule the canvas and `sql sync` apply. It returns
    `{ ok: true, design, code }`, where `code` is `null` for a design too long for a URL, or
    `{ ok: false, issues }`, and never throws for a bad design.
  - `parseDesign(value)` in `better-supabase/preset` checks the shape of a design object.
  - `designFileSource` and `readDesignFile` write and read a design file
    (`better-supabase.design.json`, `DESIGN_FILE`), which holds the preset code and the design as
    JSON with no length limit.
  - `init --preset ./better-supabase.design.json` and `apply --preset ./better-supabase.design.json`
    read a design file like a Create URL. `DesignTooLongError` now points to it.
- Doctor's BS503 warns when the shell exports a `SUPABASE_*` variable (plain or with a browser prefix) that differs from the running local stack, so an app started from that shell would talk to another project. It reads `supabase status` only when such a variable is exported, and shows redacted values.
- `better-supabase env` writes the full stack env: `SUPABASE_URL` and `SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY` under their plain names for server code, the same two with the browser prefix, and the new `SUPABASE_JWKS_URL`. The secret key, database URL and JWT secret never get a browser prefix. `--write <file>` names the file (`--out` still works), and `--prefix` takes a framework name (`next`, `vite`, `expo`, `nuxt`, `astro`, `sveltekit`), `none`, or the prefix itself. Help lists a long alias after the flag it stands for.
- Add `better-supabase run -- <cmd>`. It starts the command with the running stack's URL and keys set, over the shell's exported variables and every `.env` file, passes its output and exit code through, and forwards Ctrl+C and `SIGTERM`. When an exported `SUPABASE_*` variable differs from the stack, `run` warns and names it, showing the host and port of a URL or a `sha256:` fingerprint of a key, never the value.
- Server auth can run hermetic. `auth.jwks: false` (or `null`) verifies against no keys and never fetches one, ignoring `SUPABASE_JWKS` and `SUPABASE_JWKS_URL`; `null` used to fall through to the JWKS URL. `createServer({ env })` also takes the variables as an object (`{ SUPABASE_URL, SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY }`) and loads only those, never filling a missing one from `process.env`. `createTestSigner().jwks` is typed so it passes to `auth.jwks` without a cast.
- Signed uploads work in a writable support session. `supportStorage` used to refuse `createSignedUploadUrl` and `uploadToSignedUrl` with `SUPPORT_STORAGE_WRITES`, so `bucket.connect(storage).reserve(...)` and the blocks' browser uploads failed, and an admin could not upload a file as the user. Now `createSignedUploadUrl` runs the same dry run as `upload` as the target (an insert, or an upsert with `upsert: true`, refused with Storage's `403`), then the service client signs the URL.

  An object uploaded to a signed URL has no owner, so a claim step finishes the upload. It checks again that the target may insert at the path, then makes the target the owner and adds `supportSessionId` and `supportAdminId` to the object's `user_metadata`. It claims only an object without an owner; one another user owns is refused with `403` `AccessDenied`. The `StorageApi` that `supportStorage` returns has `claimSignedUpload(bucket, path)`, and a connected bucket has `claimReserved(reservation)`, which succeeds without a request outside a support session, so call it after a reserved upload and before you record the file. `uploadToSignedUrl` on the server and `upload` with `onProgress` claim on their own.

  The blocks claim in their own finish step: `attachments.confirm`, `drive.commit` and the AI files block's `files.confirm` claim the upload before they record it. A refused claim keeps the record pending and returns the block's `forbidden` (`ATTACHMENT_FORBIDDEN`, `DRIVE_FORBIDDEN`, `AI_FILE_FORBIDDEN`) or `not_found` (`ATTACHMENT_NOT_UPLOADED`, `AI_FILE_NOT_UPLOADED`) error. Outside a support session they make no extra request. Read-only sessions still refuse every write with `SUPPORT_READ_ONLY`.
- Create moved to the cloud app. `createUrl()` and the comment `init` writes above a preset now link to `https://app.bettersupabase.com/create`. Old `bettersupabase.com/create` links redirect there with their query string, and `presetOf` and `designOf` still read both.

## 0.11.0 (2026-10-20)

- `bs.admin()` returns an `AdminDb` with `$authAdmin`, the typed Auth admin API (`auth.admin`) of the secret-key client, so cron jobs and webhook handlers can create, invite, ban and delete users without a second client. Request repositories don't have it. The `AdminDb` type is exported from `better-supabase/server`.
- Fixes and additions from adopter feedback.

  New API:

  - `matchDbError(error, matchers)` maps a `DbError` kind to a value, for `orThrow` factories.
  - `paginate({ withCount: true })` returns `total` on offset and cursor pages and narrows its type to `number`. On a cursor page after the first, it sends one extra request to count the matching rows.
  - `db.$buckets.<name>` is each bucket registered with `betterSupabase.buckets()`, connected to `db.$storage`. The type follows `$with()`, `$withoutPlugins()` and the `db`, `sql` and `admin()` repositories of `createServer(betterSupabase)`, and `db.$storage` on a server `Db` now uses the caller's Storage client. Every framework adapter (`next`, `hono`, `orpc`, `edge`, `mcp`, `astro`, `sveltekit`, `react-router`, `solid-start`, `tanstack-start` and `expo`) carries the registry through a new trailing `B` type parameter, so `ctx.db.$buckets.<name>` is typed in handlers, actions and routes. `ctx.db.$storage` and `ctx.db.$buckets` now work on request contexts.
  - `better-supabase/testing` adds `scopedSeed`, `withRollback` and `TestUser.context`. `asUser` also turns off supabase-js trace propagation.
  - `gen` emits `WritableColumnOf` and `GeneratedColumnOf`, and the schema types export `WritableColumns` and `GeneratedColumns`.

  Fixes:

  - `update` with no columns to write reads the row and returns it, and `updateMany` returns a count of 0, instead of failing with `invalid_request`. A caller that relied on that error needs to check for empty data itself. A plugin that leaves the patch empty still gets `invalid_request`.
  - `createMany`, `upsertMany`, `updateMany` and `deleteMany` type their result by `returning`. `orderBy` accepts `undefined` values and `updateMany` data no longer infers readonly arrays.
  - The client follows auth state only when the supabase client has it.
  - Text filter escaping and case rules are documented, and `$rpc` typing is pinned as stable API.

  The generated column `memberships.tier_role` in `supabase/` is a repo-only test fixture and does not ship in the package.
- Attachments and comments subjects taken from the subject registry now carry the registry's per-action keys: `subjects.<type>.permissions.upload` and `manage` for attachments, `create` and `moderate` for comments. The registry's `read` stays the key for reading the subject itself. `blocks.<block>.options.subjects.<type>.permissions` overrides them key by key.

  Doctor `BS345` warns when an attachments subject has no `read` or `upload` key of its own while its table's policies check more than tenant membership, so any member of the tenant would get the files of rows they can't open.
- Adds `better-supabase db query <file...>`, which runs SQL files as multi-statement scripts against the local stack or the database in the connection string. `--single-transaction` keeps all files or none.
- `seed --apply` and `db query` accept `--stack <name>` (or `$BSB_STACK`) to connect to the stack in `supabase/stacks/<name>/config.toml` instead of the default local stack.
- Closes the gaps found on 0.9.0: `audit()` takes `tenant_path` and `tenant_function`, `admin({}, { as })` names the acting member, optional RPC arguments accept `undefined`, `createHono` and `createNext` share one server, OAuth server resources follow RFC 8707, `jwtType` validates `typ`, billing webhooks cover subscriptions, usage meters count distinct or computed values, and the doctor flags partitions without RLS (BS343).
- `bs.middleware()` from `better-supabase/expo` hands the session it refreshed to the loaders and `bs.request` calls of the same request. They read the new cookies without calling Auth again, so a loader with `refresh: false` renders signed in on the first paint after an expired token, and each request makes at most one Auth call. Loaders that run without middleware still refresh on their own.
- Add the `ieq` filter operator for case-insensitive equality, on text columns and jsonb `path` filters. It sends `ilike` with `%`, `_` and `\` escaped, so `where: { email: { ieq: input } }` matches only that address in any case.

  Add the `require-escape-like` rule to `better-supabase/lint`, on as a warning in `configs.recommended`. It flags a `like` or `ilike` value taken from a variable, a property, or a template or concatenation without `escapeLike(...)`.
- Realtime topic triggers read less and send less.

  - A generated trigger function reads each parent row once per changed row, into a local record, however many values, payload keys and topics use it. A custom payload is built once for all topics. Before, every lookup ran again for the payload, for each topic's values and for each nested level. Run `sql sync` to rewrite the functions; the messages they send don't change.
  - `topic.trigger(table, { when: { changed: ["status"] } })`, and `when` on a `realtime.triggers` entry, send an update only when one of the listed columns changed. On a trigger for updates alone it becomes the trigger's `when` clause, otherwise an early return in the function. Inserts and deletes always send.
  - A delete whose lookup finds no parent row, such as a child removed by `on delete cascade`, raises a warning that starts with `better-supabase: realtime lookup found no row` instead of skipping the topic silently. The Realtime docs have a new "Deletes and cascades" section.
- New block `better-supabase/blocks/vercel-marketplace` (`better-supabase add vercel-marketplace`): installations, resources, plans, invoices and meter reports for an app listed on the Vercel Marketplace. `createVercelMarketplace()` verifies webhooks (`x-vercel-signature`, HMAC-SHA1), applies `integration-configuration.removed` and invoice events (the newest event wins), exchanges SSO codes and verifies the `id_token` against Vercel's JWKS, submits invoices and reports usage with retries. Only the service role calls it.
- `vercelQueueBackend(client)` in `better-supabase/blocks/jobs` runs jobs on Vercel Queues. Pass a `PollingQueueClient` from `@vercel/queue` (new optional peer, `>=0.8 <0.9`). It passes `testQueueBackend`. Vercel Queues keeps no dead letters and has no schedules, so `schedule` still needs the SQL backend.
- An adopted `webhooks-out` endpoints table has to choose its body format.

  Breaking config change: in adopt mode, when `columns.endpoints.format` maps no column, `sql sync` now refuses the config unless `blocks.webhooks-out.options.defaultFormat` is set. Without a format column every endpoint gets `defaultFormat`, whose default changed from the 0.8 body (`standard`) to `cloudevents` in 0.9, so receivers broke without any change to the config. Set `defaultFormat: "standard"` to keep the 0.8 body, `"cloudevents"` if your receivers read CloudEvents, or map a format column.

  Doctor reports the same config as `BS344` (warning), and says whether the table has no `format` column or one the config doesn't map. The webhooks-out page lists `defaultFormat` and the version 3 columns, and the new 0.9 to 0.10 guide covers the change.
- `seal` from `better-supabase/create/supabase` now accepts any object, so other OAuth connections on the Create page can reuse the sealed session cookie.

## 0.10.0 (2026-10-20)

- Adopt mode takes enum columns where a block writes kinds and subject types. In adopt mode the tags block casts the `kind` and `subjectType` values it writes and compares, and `next_number` casts the `entity`, to the adopted column's type through the new `better_supabase.as_column_type(value, target)`, so `kind public.organization_tag_kind` or `entity public.workflow_subject_type` works. An enum on any other text column a block writes a parameter or computed value into is refused instead of failing at runtime (columns a block writes only string literals into, such as the teams `status`, may stay enums): `sql sync` and `better-supabase adopt` report it when the schema files declare the enum, and doctor reports it as BS342. A module table spec lists its castable columns in `typed`, and `ModuleContext.cast(table, column, value)` renders the cast.
- App table columns take `access: "read"` or `access: "server"`. `sql sync` then grants `select`, `insert` and `update` on the columns signed-in users may use, by name, and the `<table>_view` lists only the readable columns. `gen` marks a server column `selectable: false`, so a default select leaves it out and the row type makes it optional; select it by name with the service role client. `appTables` refuses a required restricted column without a default, and a server column used as the subject title, a search column, a rollup or lookup source, or in `events.include`. Tightening a column's access is a breaking change in the design diff, and Create design codes with `access` use format 5.

  `doctor` counts column grants for BS106, so a table whose grants list columns no longer reports "Table not granted to the Data API".
- App tables emit outbox events, and rules and the workflow builder react to them (ADR 0048).

  - `appTables.<name>.events` (`true` or `{ on, include }`) adds a trigger that writes `<type>.created`, `<type>.updated` and `<type>.deleted` to the outbox in the same transaction as the write. The payload is an `AppTableEventData` with the id, the tenant, the subject, the `include` columns and, on update, the changed columns. It needs the outbox block. `gen blocks` writes `appTableEvents`, a typed event map.
  - `createRuleSink` runs the rules for each outbox event and dispatches their actions, and `WorkflowBuilder.sink()` starts the event triggers that match.
  - Rules get the built-in handlers `notifyAction`, `setFieldAction`, `webhookAction` and `workflowAction`.
  - A change to `events` is the safe design change `table.events`, and a design with `events` is format 4.

  `gen` tags `appTableEvents` and `appTableEventSchemas` with `@public`, so Knip doesn't report the one an app leaves unused.
- App tables get record history with per-field restore, upsert imports and a bulk set action.

  - `appTables.<name>.subject.versions` (`true` or `{ columns, auto }`) registers the table with the versions block, so its rows get snapshots without an entry in `subjects`. It needs the versions block, and a design with `versions` is format 4. A change to it is the safe design change `table.versions`.
  - `versions.restoreFields(id, fields)` writes back only the named fields. It fails with `VERSION_FIELDS_EMPTY`, `VERSION_FIELD_UNKNOWN` or `VERSION_RESTORE_UNSUPPORTED`.
  - `appTableImport` builds an import keyed on one of the table's unique sets that updates existing rows (`update: "fill"` or `"overwrite"`), and `previewImport` counts the rows it would create, change and leave unchanged.
  - The bulk action `appTables.set` sets columns on the selected rows through `app_table_set`, as the caller. A row fails with `APP_TABLE_SET_EMPTY`, `APP_TABLE_SET_COLUMN`, `APP_TABLE_SET_SUBJECT` or `APP_TABLE_SET_NOT_FOUND`.
  - New recipes: record history and upsert import.
- Add `better-supabase mcp`, which serves the CLI to a coding agent as an MCP server on stdio. The read-only tools (`doctor`, `blocks_list`, `config`, `gen_check`, `apply_dry_run` and others) run by default, and `--allow-writes` adds `gen`, `sql_sync`, `apply` and `add`, each of which asks for the user's consent with a token before it writes. `better-supabase/mcp` now exports `answerMessage` and `answerLine` for serving a `ProtocolServer` over any transport, and `createMcp` logs one warning naming the tools a missing authorizer hides.
- Add `better-supabase/create` and `better-supabase/create/supabase`. They hold the design model, schema parsing, previews, deploy plans, Supabase OAuth and the Management API client that `/create` used to keep inside the marketing app, so other apps can build on them.
- Add `better-supabase/create/lite`. `validateDesign(files)` applies the files Create generates to an in-memory PGlite database through `@supabase/lite` and reports what Postgres rejects. Modules that need `pgmq` or `btree_gist` are reported as skipped.
- Doctor checks the migrations that haven't run for statements that lock a table with rows: an index built without `concurrently` (BS229), a foreign key or check added without `not valid` (BS230), a column type change or `set not null` without a validated check (BS231), a column added with a volatile default (BS232), a migration that takes an exclusive lock before `set lock_timeout` (BS233), and drops or renames (BS234, info). With `--db-url` it checks the migrations the database hasn't applied; offline, the ones after the new `doctor.migrationSafety.since` setting, or the newest one. `init` and `adopt` set `since` to the newest migration.
- `loadEnv`, `parseEnv` and `envSchema` read `dbUrl` from `POSTGRES_URL` and then `POSTGRES_URL_NON_POOLING` when `SUPABASE_DB_URL` and `DATABASE_URL` are unset, the names the Vercel Supabase integration and Vercel Postgres set. `createPostgres()` falls back to the same names in the same order.
- Add `migrationBetween(code, base, next)` to `better-supabase/create`. It returns the migration that takes a deployed design to the next one for added tables and columns, and refuses breaking or unsupported changes with the changes that caused it.
- `number-sequences` accepts `in` and `not in` lists of string or number literals in `assignOn`, such as `new.status in ('sent', 'overdue')`, which its comma ban refused. A comma anywhere else outside a quoted string is still refused, and so is an unterminated quote, which could only fail at migration time.
- `number-sequences` entities take `permission`: the key `preview_number` checks for that entity, such as `invoices.write` for invoices and `quotes.write` for quotes. An entity without one keeps checking `blocks.number-sequences.permissions.assign`. `modulePermissionKeys` lists the entity keys, so `sql sync` and doctor check them against the authorization provider, and `ModulePermissionKey` gains an optional `setting` with the config path that sets the key.
- `sql sync` now keeps `number-sequences` counters in step when a tenant changes its period. For an entity with `table`, `column` and `periodColumn` that sets `periodFrom`, or `formatFrom` with `period: "format"`, it adds an `after update` trigger, `bs_sync_number_period_<entity>`, to each tenant table those columns name. When an update changes the tenant's effective period, the trigger runs `sync_number_sequences(tenant, entity)` in the same transaction, so no number repeats; other updates leave the counters alone. It compares the old and new rows through the new `number_period_kind(period, format)`, so a generated column works too. Without `periodColumn` no trigger is added, as sync cannot run for such an entity. Drop a hand-written trigger that called sync on a period change.
- `number-sequences` adds `preview_number(tenant, entity, at)` and `createNumberSequences().preview()`: the formatted number the next document would get, in the tenant's format and period, without assigning it. Signed-in users may call it when they hold the block's `assign` permission (`blocks.number-sequences.permissions.assign`, default `number_sequences.assign`), which until now no function checked; others get `NUMBER_FORBIDDEN`. `next_number` stays service-only, and documents keep getting their numbers from the trigger.
- `number-sequences` takes the period of a new number from the row: with `periodColumn` set, the trigger reads the period and the date tokens of the format from that column (a `date`, `timestamp` or `timestamptz`, in UTC), such as an invoice's issue date, and falls back to the time of assignment while it is null. `sync_number_sequences` reads a `date` column in UTC too.
- The number-sequences block adds `sync_number_sequences(p_tenant, p_entity)`, a service-only function that raises each counter to the highest number its table already holds, per tenant and period, and never lowers one. Run it after seeding (`select better_supabase.sync_number_sequences();` at the end of `supabase/seed.sql`), after an import, or once after adopting tables whose documents already have numbers, so the next number does not collide. It reads the entities that set `table` and `column` and parses each number with the entity's `format`; the period comes from `{yyyy}`, `{yy}` and `{mm}` in the format, or from the new `periodColumn` option.
- `number-sequences` numbers each tenant in its own format. `formatFrom: { column: "schema.table.column", key }` reads a tenant's format from a settings column and falls back to the entity's `format`; a tenant format without `{n}` fails with `NUMBER_FORMAT_INVALID`. The new `period: "format"` restarts the counter by the format's tokens: monthly with a year and `{mm}`, yearly with only a year, never without either, so one tenant can number continuously and another per year. `sequenceColumn` also stores the bare number next to the formatted one. `sync_number_sequences` reads per-tenant formats and sequence columns, and the orders, invoices, quotes, deals and work-items modules use the tenant's format when they number through the block.
- `number-sequences` entities take `periodFrom: { column, key? }`, the column that holds each tenant's reset period (`none`, `year`, `month` or `format`), so a tenant can keep a format such as `INV-{yyyy}{mm}-{n}` and still number on through the year. A null or empty value falls back to the entity's `period`, so `period: "format"` keeps deriving the period from the format; any other value fails with `NUMBER_PERIOD_INVALID`. `number_period(tenant, entity)` returns a tenant's choice. `next_number`, `preview_number` and `sync_number_sequences` use the same period, and sync needs `periodColumn` for such an entity.
- `realtime.triggers` entries take everything `topic.trigger()` does: lookups through a parent row (`{ from, via, select, key }`), a payload object whose values are columns, lookups or `{ sql }` expressions, one event name per operation, extra `topics` with their own values, and `functionSchema`. `sql sync` passes each entry to the topic's `trigger()`, so the config and the call write the same SQL. `topic.trigger()` without `betterSupabase` takes lookups by database names too, with `key` defaulting to `id`, and `realtime-tables` is required only for triggers that call `better_supabase.broadcast_topic()`.
- The number-sequences and teams docs name the adopt-mode table keys the blocks read: `tables.sequences` (not `tables.numberSequences`) and `tables.members` (not `tables.teamMembers`). A unit test now checks every `tables.*` and `columns.*` key on the blocks pages against the block's SQL modules.
- Fix block findings from the no-code research review.

  - `create_share_link` checks that the subject exists in the link's tenant and that the caller can read it, and raises `SHARE_SUBJECT_NOT_FOUND` or `SHARE_SUBJECT_UNKNOWN` otherwise. Links take `maxUses`, and `resolve_share_link` counts each use, so a spent link stops resolving. `drive_link` and `get_shared_ai_chat` also check the link's tenant.
  - `runImport` marks an import `failed` with the rows it inserted when a batch throws, instead of leaving it `running`. A new `running` hook (with `mark_import_running` in SQL) marks it running before the first batch.
  - A number sequence refuses a number set by hand unless the service role sets it (`NUMBER_MANUAL`).
  - `save_view` drops `isDefault` when the view moves to another scope, list or team.
  - The app tables pgTAP file inserts a row of another tenant or user before it checks that a signed-in user can't read it, so the check fails when a policy leaks rows.
  - App table and column names are at most 58 characters, and a table is refused when a constraint or index name it derives would pass Postgres's 63-character limit. A column default must fit the column's options and bounds. `better-supabase/preset` exports these checks (`appIdentifierError`, `appNameLengthError`, `appDefaultError`, `isAppDefault`, `appReservedColumns`, `APP_NAME_MAX`) so builders validate with the same rules.
- `toDbError` now classifies PostgREST and Postgres error objects, and a thrown `PostgrestError`, through `mapDbError`, so a unique violation becomes `conflict`, `PGRST116` becomes `not_found`, and permission and check failures get their kinds instead of `unexpected`. Other errors and unknown values map as before.
- `diffAppTables` marks every change that rows already in the table may not pass as breaking, with steps: a new `unique` column or unique set, a removed option, a raised `min` or lowered `max`, a checked format, a new reference target, a new scope, and removing `timestamps`, `rank` or `softDelete`. These get their own kinds (`column.unique`, `column.options`, `column.bounds`, `column.format`, `column.reference`, `table.scope`, `table.unique`, `table.columns`). Indexes, comments, the subject and `onDelete` stay safe.

  `apply` compares the whole design with `appTables`. It reports the tables the design leaves out as kept (`data.kept`), and it refuses a table or column rename without `--allow-breaking`. With the flag it prints the `alter table ... rename` statements to run before `sql sync` (`data.renames` under `--json`).
- `gen` makes the `column` and `sequenceColumn` of each `number-sequences` entity optional on insert, so adopters no longer list them in `tables.<table>.insertOptional`.
- `number-sequences` refuses a client that writes `sequenceColumn` with `NUMBER_MANUAL`, as it already did for `column`. A signed-in user could send the bare number on a draft, which stayed visible until the trigger overwrote it on issue. Only the trigger and the service role write it now.
- `createNumberSequences().next()` sends `p_entity`, the name `next_number` declares, so the call reaches the function instead of failing to find it.

## 0.9.0 (2026-10-19)

- A record's activity timeline now includes its notification deliveries (with the `notifications` module) and its workflow runs (with the `workflows` module), as `delivery.<status>` and `run.<status>` entries with `source` set to `delivery` or `run`. Deliveries need `activity.read` and `notifications.send`, the same as the notifications client's `subjectDeliveries`; runs follow the `workflow_runs` read policy. `createComments().history()` takes `sources` to pick which kinds of entry to return, and `list_activity` gains a matching `sources text[]` argument. The comments module moves to version 6, so re-run `better-supabase sql sync`.
- `better-supabase adopt <block> --tables <schema.table>` maps a block onto tables the app already has. It reads the tables like `introspect`, matches the block's logical tables and columns to them (including likely renames such as `organization_id` for `tenant`, and `null` for optional columns the table lacks), prints the mapping with a doctor-style report of what can't map, and writes `blocks.<block>` in adopt mode when everything maps. `--dry-run` writes nothing and `--json` prints the entry, the mapping and the report.

  `apply --preset <code> --adopt` proposes the same mapping for every block of the preset that has a table under its default names, shows it before the config diff, and writes nothing when one of those tables can't be adopted. `apply` now also takes `--snapshot`, `--db-url-stdin` and `--project-ref`, and `--json` prints the config it writes and the blocks it adopted.
- Agent actors match support sessions. An agent `act` level reads `read_only` (`readOnly` on the actor; a non-boolean value makes the chain invalid), and `agentClaims(claims, { agent, tools, readOnly })` writes it. `tools` is now optional: a level without it delegates every tool and `tools: []` none. `createAgentRuntime({ actor })` drops the tools a delegating agent didn't list and denies a call to them with `AGENT_TOOL_NOT_DELEGATED`, which `assertToolDelegated` throws too, and a read-only agent makes the run read-only.

  `actor_may_write()` decides by `kind` first: support writes only with `read_only: false`, an agent unless `read_only` is `true`, and impersonation or an unknown `kind` never. An impersonation token with `read_only: false` could write before; allow it through the provider's `authorization.functions.actorMayWrite` template instead. `read_only` is compared as a JSON boolean.

  `better-supabase/testing/act-cases.json` (and `actCases` from `better-supabase/testing`) lists `act` chains with the expected verdict, so other parsers can run the same cases.
- An RFC 8693 `act` level may be `{ kind: "agent", sub, tools }`. `agentClaims(claims, { agent, tools })` from `better-supabase/server` builds it on top of the user's claims, and agent runs carry it as `run.act`. An agent writes with the user's row level security unless its level sets `read_only: true`, and a `scope` on its token limits it like an OAuth client. The read-only check in every SQL module knows the new kind, so `sql sync` rewrites each installed module.

  `inboxAdapter({ identities })` resolves each inbox contact before the handlers run and issues a run ticket for a contact linked to a signed-in user, which `ticketOf(message)` returns for `forContext({ ticket })`. `testAuthorizationHooks` also checks `automation.mayPublish` and that a support start cannot approve itself.
- `createAgentRuntime` from `better-supabase/ai-sdk/agents` takes `caller` (the invoking user, always the principal; the agent is only the actor), `authorizeModel` (checked before every model call, refusing with `forbidden` before tokens are spent), `results` (filters knowledge hits before the model sees them), `operations`, `permissions` and `authorizeTool` (each tool is the operation `ai.tool.<name>`, the provider's entry wins, and `high` risk waits for approval), and `gateway` (AI Gateway `user` and tags from the caller). `searchTool` and `recallTool` take `results` too. Breaking: `gatewayOptions` drops a `user` passed in its second argument, so `user` only ever comes from `context.userId`; pass the caller's id there. `GatewayContext` takes `tags`.
- AI chat sharing moves onto the `share-links` block. `share_ai_chat`, `revoke_ai_chat_share`, `list_ai_chat_shares` and `get_shared_ai_chat` stay callable and use `share_links`. Existing `ai_chat_shares` rows are copied, including the leaf. New links are stored only as a hash.
- API keys have a `kind`. List the allowed kinds in `blocks.api-keys.options.kinds` (a check constraint enforces them) and pass `kind` to `create`. `create_api_key` takes `kind` as its last argument; the module's upgrade drops the old signature. `apiKeyResolver`, `apiKeyClaims` and `withApiKey` take a `claims(key)` hook that adds claims but cannot set `sub`, `role`, `aud` or `api_key`, and `serviceRoles` and `claims` receive the key with the `fields` columns.
- A Bearer token that starts with the API key prefix and `_` now counts as an API key even when the rest is malformed. `apiKeyResolver` and `withApiKey` verify it, and `verify` answers `invalid`, so `bs_bad_key` gets `INVALID_API_KEY` like a revoked or unknown key instead of `API_KEY_REQUIRED` or a JWT error. `createApiKeys` returns `isApiKey(token)` for code that routes a Bearer token to either API key or JWT verification. A `keys` object without `isApiKey` keeps the old rule.
- App table columns take `format` (`email`, `url`, `phone`, `long_text`, `color`, `rating`, `percent`, `currency`), `options` for a single or multi-select, `min` and `max`, `comment` and `identity`. Tables take `id: "bigint"`, composite `unique` keys, `indexes` and a `comment`. `sql sync` renders each as a named constraint, index or comment, and the pgTAP file checks them; `email`, `url`, `rating`, `percent`, options and bounds add a check. No key takes free SQL.

  A Create design that uses one of these keys gets `version: 1` in its JSON (`designFormat`, `DESIGN_FORMAT` from `better-supabase/preset`); older designs encode to the same code as before, and a code newer than the installed release fails with a message to update. Create's column type picker is grouped (Text, Number, Date and time, Choice, Relation, Other) with Single select, Email, Rating, Autonumber, Person and Currency, which splits into `<name>_minor` and `<name>_currency`.
- A reference between two app tables scoped to the same tenant (a table that references itself included) now checks the tenant. `sql sync` adds a unique `(tenant, id)` index on the target and a composite foreign key `(tenant, column)`, so a row can no longer point at a row of another organization or workspace; `on delete set null` clears only the referencing column. The pgTAP file checks each composite key.

  Migration: run `better-supabase sql sync` and generate a migration. If an existing row already references a row of another tenant, the new foreign key fails to apply; fix or delete those rows first.

  Create can link two tables many to many from a table's menu ("Add many-to-many with…"), which adds a junction table with two required references, `on delete cascade` and a unique pair. A Tree primitive adds a table with a `parent_id` that references itself. When you select a table, its edges show the delete action, and a reference that is unique ends in a one-to-one marker.
- App tables take `rollups` and `lookups` (ADR 0044). A rollup counts, sums or takes the minimum, maximum or average of a column of another app table that references this one, optionally only for the rows whose select column equals one option. A lookup reads a column of the row a reference points at. Both are structured keys, so a design never carries SQL, and `resolveConfig` lists the valid tables and columns when a key is wrong.

  `sql sync` writes `public.<table>_view` with `security_invoker = true`, so each rollup counts only the rows the caller can read. Only `authenticated` and `service_role` can read the view. The pgTAP file checks that the view runs as the invoker and shows a stranger no rows. `gen` reads it as a read-only model (`db.projectsView.findMany()`).

  A design with rollups or lookups writes design format 3. `diffAppTables` reports a change to them as the safe `table.computed`. Create shows rollups and lookups as computed rows on the table and edits them in a Computed section of the table inspector.

  `better-supabase/config` exports `APP_ROLLUP_AGGREGATES` and the `AppRollupConfig`, `AppRollupAggregate` and `AppLookupConfig` types.
- An app table can be a subject: `appTables.<name>.subject` takes a `type`, a `title` and `url`, `search`, `permissions`, `rank` and `softDelete`, and is merged into `subjects`. Comments, attachments, tags, versions, watchers, activity and search then reach its rows. `rank: true` adds a `rank` column with an index and `softDelete` adds `deleted_at`. A design that uses `subject` writes design format 2.

  Migration: none for existing configs. A type in `subject` that is already in `subjects` fails `resolveConfig`; remove one of the two.

  Create gets a Features tab on the table inspector: turning on comments, attachments, tags, versions or search adds the block and makes the table a subject, and the table shows a badge for each feature.
- `appTables` declares the tables your app owns. `better-supabase sql sync` writes them into `900_better_supabase_app_tables.sql` with the scope column (per tenant, per user or open to signed-in readers), row level security, grants, indexes and the `updated-at` trigger, plus a pgTAP test that checks each table's policies and that a stranger can't read or insert outside their scope. Columns take a closed list of types, defaults from an allowlist and references to another app table, `auth.users` or a block's table, which follows the block's renames.

  `better-supabase/preset` encodes a Create design (app tables, extra columns on block tables and canvas positions) into a code that rides on the `/create` URL as `&design=`: `encodeDesign`, `decodeDesign`, `designOf`, `designConfig`, `designConfigSource`, `designInstallCommand` and `designApplyCommand`. The preset catalog lists the table each block's `extraColumns` extends. `init --preset <url>` writes the design's `appTables` and `extraColumns` into the new config, and `apply --preset <url>` merges them into the config you have.
- `approval_eligible` also asks the provider's `isApprover` for the kind `<type>.<action>`, and never for the requester. `createApprovals` takes `approverOf`, `wakeOnApproval` from `better-supabase/workflow-sdk` resumes a run waiting on a request id when the request is approved, rejected, expired or cancelled, and a workflow-builder approval node can file an approvals request and wait on its id.
- With the `approvals` SQL module installed, AI-chat tool calls that wait for approval (`ai_tool_approval`, action `run`) and support access requests for a tenant (`support_request`, action `start`) also file an approval request. Deciding either side decides the other: `decide_approval` approves or denies the tool call and approves or closes the support request, and `decide_ai_tool_approval` or `approve_support_request` settles the approval request. The owning module still decides who may approve, these requests can't be delegated, and `approvals.read` alone doesn't list them, so tool inputs and support reasons stay with their approvers.
- AsyncAPI channels now come from the same `defineTopic` definitions that `sql sync` turns into Realtime policies. Pass `topicsFrom(module)` (from `better-supabase/asyncapi`) to `defineApi({ topics })`. Each channel gets its broadcast events, its presence schema and an `x-better-supabase-realtime-status` of `beta` for private topics (Realtime Authorization is still in public beta) or `ga` for public ones. A topic gets a send operation only when it sets `clientSend: true`; before, every topic got one. `defineTopic` takes the new `presenceSchema`, `clientSend` and `status` options, and a topic exposes `events`, `clientSend`, `permission` and `status`.

  `better-supabase spec` loads the `realtime.policies.from` modules with the loader `sql sync` uses. It warns `asyncapi-topic-undocumented` for each topic the AsyncAPI document leaves out, and `--watch` renders again when a topic module changes.

  The new `dataApi` option documents the Supabase Data API next to your routes: `/rest/v1/<table>` operations and `/rest/v1/rpc/<function>` calls, with database column names, the API key and JWT schemes, the PostgREST error body and their own `servers` from `supabaseUrl`. Operations take an optional `servers` list, which the OpenAPI formats render. New diagnostics: `data-api-server-missing`, `data-api-table-unknown` and `data-api-function-unknown`.
- The audit block reverts row changes: `audit.revert(entryIds)` (SQL `revert_audit_entries`) writes the old values back as the caller, newest entry first, all or nothing, and refuses with `ROW_CHANGED_SINCE` when a row changed after the entry. Members need the new `audit.revert` permission, which admins have by default. The result carries an undo token that reverts the revert. The saved-views read policy now evaluates `auth.uid()` once per query when the teams module is installed.
- Fixes from an audit of the blocks, the jobs worker, the commerce adapters and the CLI.

  Breaking: `fromStripeAmount` returns a `Result<Money>`. It refuses an amount that isn't a safe integer, or an ISK or UGX amount with a fraction of the minor unit, with `invalid_input` and the hint `STRIPE_AMOUNT_PRECISION`, where it used to truncate. The Stripe payments and tax adapters return that error instead of a wrong amount.

  The notifications and settings blocks split their own-row `for all` policies into read, insert, update and delete policies, so `actor_may_write()` guards writes only and a read-only support session can still read. Run `better-supabase sql sync` and generate a migration.

  - The data-lifecycle purge checks the legal hold and the due date before it deletes storage objects or calls a webhook.
  - A jobs worker aborts a job's signal when its lease can't be extended, and reports `JOB_LEASE_LOST` through `onError` instead of completing a job another worker may hold. A failed `complete` is reported too.
  - The idempotency middleware answers `409` with `IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_LOST` when the lock expired before the response was saved, and the request fingerprint includes the query string.
  - The webhook inbox passes its handler an `AbortSignal` that aborts when the lease can't be renewed, and then leaves the message to the next worker.
  - The outbox treats a handler that returns a failed `Result` as a failure, so the event is retried.
  - `deleteAccount` succeeds on a retry for a user that Auth already deleted, and still removes the listed storage objects.
  - Invoice deposits are computed in `numeric`, so line totals round half away from zero past 2^53.
  - The search block adds a partial index for rows waiting for an embedding.
  - `env --out`, `env.output` and `env --from` refuse a path outside the project root.
  - A `42501` error says "permission denied" to the client, and keeps the object name and PostgREST's grant recipe in `details`.
  - `queueBackend({ client, schema })` calls the wrappers `blocks.jobs.api` writes into that schema, for the full feature set over the Data API. `client` alone still uses the `pgmq_public` RPCs.
  - `supabaseCollection` from `better-supabase/tanstack-db/supabase` works with `casing: 'camel'` and with tables outside `public`: it renames filters, bodies, rows and Realtime payloads, and runs requests and Realtime listeners in the table's schema.
  - A subject's `deletedAt` column becomes the table's `softDelete` flag in `gen` (it wins over `plugins.softDelete.column`), and declared metrics skip those rows unless the metric sets `includeDeleted: true`.
- `createServer(betterSupabase, { authorization })` takes `AuthorizationHooks` (`defineAuthorizationHooks` from `better-supabase/server`): optional `automation`, `support`, `delivery`, `identities`, `chat`, `approverOf` and `tickets` hooks, so one authorization provider decides publishing, support sessions, fan-out, identity links, chat actions, approvals and run tickets. `testAuthorizationHooks` from `better-supabase/testing` checks that they fail closed.

  The `authorization` config gains `functions.isApprover` (`{kind}`, `{subject}`, `{tenant}`), `functions.permitted` for checks on one instance, and `operations`, one table of the permission each workflow node, MCP tool, AI tool and inbox command needs.

  Breaking: with `authorization` set, `blocks.access.model` defaults to `provider`, so `can()`, `tenant_ids_with()` and every module check answer from the provider. Set `model: "roles"` to keep the built-in roles; doctor BS414 then reports policies and functions that call the access contract. `functions.canApprove` is deprecated in favour of `isApprover` and still read when `isApprover` is unset; doctor BS415 warns while a provider sets it, and `better-supabase codemod 0.9` lists each use.
- Add client controllers and hooks for the `billing`, `usage`, `entitlements` and `settings` blocks, each with `/react`, `/vue`, `/svelte` and `/solid` entries. `usePlanPicker()` (`better-supabase/blocks/billing/react`) lists your plans with the current one and starts Checkout through a server action you pass, so Stripe is only called on the server. `useUsageMeter()` (`better-supabase/blocks/usage/react`) reads this period's usage per meter with its fraction of the quota. `useFeatureGate()` (`better-supabase/blocks/entitlements/react`) tells whether the active organization's plan includes a feature from the session's features claim, and `hasEntitlement()` now also takes the client's `useAuth()` snapshot. `useSettings()` (`better-supabase/blocks/settings/react`) edits one scope of a `defineSettings()` definition with `fields` generated from each key's Standard JSON Schema, and `setField()` for generated forms. The plan picker, usage meter, feature gate and settings layout recipes show them in the Next.js and Expo examples.
- Blocks sit in 16 categories instead of 10, so the catalog, `blocks list --category` and `/blocks` group them the way an app is built. `BlockCategory` takes the new ids:

  | Old category                 | New category                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
  | ---------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | `foundation`                 | `identity` (access, actor, identities, mfa, sessions, sso, subjects), `tenancy` (tenant, organizations, teams, profiles, settings, reserved-slugs) and `database` (updated-at)                                                                                                                |
  | `collaboration`              | `collaboration`, and `messaging` (notifications, push, inbox)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
  | `work`                       | `work`, `people` (employees, hiring), `identity` (access-requests) and `database` (rank)                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
  | `scheduling`                 | `scheduling`, and `people` (time-tracking)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
  | `operations`                 | `growth` (onboarding, waitlist, announcements, flags), `admin` (audit, data-lifecycle, metrics, support-sessions) and `automation` (workflows, workflow-builder, workflow-sdk-world)                                                                                                          |
  | `platform`                   | `developer` (api-keys, credentials, service principals, signing keys, shared signals, the webhook blocks, rate-limit, idempotency), `automation` (jobs, outbox, streams), `identity` (grants) and `database` (ensure-rls, pgtap, jsonb-schemas, number-sequences, read-sets, realtime-tables) |
  | `ai`                         | `ai`, and `automation` (connectors)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
  | `crm`, `commerce`, `content` | unchanged                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     |

  `better-supabase/preset` adds 14 named presets, so `/create` and `init` offer 22 in five groups (`PRESET_GROUPS`, `NamedPreset.group`): `consumer` (per-user data with `scope: "user"`), `helpdesk`, `marketplace`, `people`, `drive`, `docs`, `invoicing`, `forms`, `ai-chat`, `ai-agents`, `developer-api`, `automation`, `enterprise` and `admin`. Every preset after the starters builds on `saas`. The existing presets keep their blocks, and `NamedPreset.conventions` holds the conventions a preset sets over the defaults.

  The UI recipes in the skill's `references/ui-recipes.md` grow from 15 to 32, with 46 more listed as planned, each naming what it waits for. A recipe now has a `kind` (`screen` or `component`), can span several blocks, and lists only the platforms it has code for. Each block manifest's `recipes` names the recipes that use the block.
