- More blocks meet the block contract. `createAuditLog` is now `createAudit`. The clients of agents, ai-cache, ai-chat, ai-files, ai-providers, ai-tasks, announcements, api-keys, attachments, audit, billing, bookings, bulk, calendar, comments, connectors and custom-fields take `hooks` (and `logger`) and run them around every method, and audit's purge calls and `activitySink` go through `blockCall`, so error mappers apply.

  Access-requests, agents, ai-chat, ai-files, ai-tasks, announcements, api-keys, approvals, attachments, bookings, calendar, catalog and collections take `options.extraColumns` on their main table and a `fields` Standard Schema on the client. The module adds the columns, its writes take them (columns a write leaves out keep their value or default) and its reads return them under `fields`; the client validates them on writes, parses them on reads and merges them into the rows it returns. Without `extraColumns` the SQL is unchanged.
- `comments`, `connectors` and `rate-limit` follow the block contract.

  `createRateLimit` takes one options object: `createRateLimit({ sql: postgres.admin })` or `createRateLimit({ transport: rpcTransport(adminClient) })` replaces `createRateLimit(source, options)`, with `schema`, `mappers`, `hooks`, `logger` and `temporal` next to it. `createBlocks` builds the `rate-limit` block from its shared `transport`.

  `comments` and `connectors` take `options.extraColumns` (on `comments` and `connector_servers`) and a Standard Schema `fields` option on `createComments` and `createConnectors`. `create` and `edit` of comments, and `servers.create` and `servers.update` of connectors, validate the `fields` they get and write them; every comment and server read returns the columns parsed under `fields`, and `comments.copy` copies them. `create_comment` and `edit_comment` take a `fields jsonb` argument only when extra columns are configured; `save_connector_server` takes them in its existing `fields` object.

  Every SQL module that takes a `fields jsonb` argument for its extra columns now names it `fields`: `request_access`, `reserve_ai_file`, `store_ai_file`, `create_api_key`, `request_approval`, `create_attachment`, `save_product`, `create_collection` and `rename_collection` took `extra_fields` before. Each module drops the signature it doesn't create, so turning `extraColumns` on or off never leaves two overloads a call matches, and the module contracts list the argument when it exists.
- Blocks from `notifications` to `settings` follow the block contract.

  `onboarding`, `outbox` and `settings` have a `create<Name>(options)` creator, and their manifests build it. `createOnboarding({ transport })` replaces `checklist.connect(...)`: its methods take the checklist first (`onboarding.complete(gettingStarted, "invite", organizationId)`), and `createOnboardingController` takes `client` and `checklist` instead of `scope`. `createSettings({ settings, transport })` replaces `settings.connect(...)`, with flat methods (`getUser`, `setUser`, `resetUser`, `getOrganization`, `setOrganization`, `resetOrganization`, `getPlatform`, `setPlatform`, `resetPlatform`); `defineSettings` returns only the `schemas`. `createOutbox({ transport, source })` replaces `createOutbox(source, options)`: pass `sqlTransport(postgres.admin)` for a server connection, and `service` for the consumer calls when `transport` acts as a user.

  `hooks` (and `logger`) now work on `createNumberSequences`, `createPush`, `createRank`, `createRateLimit`, `createRelations`, `createReminders`, `createSavedViews`, `createServicePrincipals`, `createOnboarding`, `createSettings` and `createOutbox`; job handlers call their methods through the hooks. `createNotifications` sends its delivery calls through `blockCall`, so `deliver()` rejects with the mapped `DbError`.

  `options.extraColumns` and a typed `fields` option cover the main table of `invitations` (as `invitationFields` on `createOrganizations`), `notifications`, `orders`, `pipelines`, `promotions`, `push`, `relations`, `reminders`, `saved-views` and `service-principals`, and `fields` now types the columns `parties` and `quotes` already took. Writes take `fields` and validate them before the call; reads return them in `fields`, built with `jsonb_build_object`, and parse them with the schema. Without `extraColumns` the SQL is unchanged.
- The 14 blocks with a headless controller (ai-chat, announcements, bookings, calendar, forms, inbox, notifications, onboarding, reactions, saved-views, search, undo, workflows and workflow-builder) get `better-supabase/blocks/<name>/vue`, `/svelte` and `/solid` entries. Each exports the React hook names (`useInbox`, `useUndoStack` and so on) over the same controllers: Vue returns a shallow ref of the snapshot, Svelte a readable store and Solid an accessor from `from`, with the controller's actions next to it. They take the controller's options; `auth` and `ports` come from `betterSupabase(bs)`, `setBetterSupabase(bs)` or `<BetterSupabaseProvider>` unless you pass them. The work starts on mount, never during server rendering, and the controller is disposed with the component.

  `testControllerBinding` from `better-supabase/testing` checks that a framework binding renders a controller's snapshot, follows its changes, passes its actions through and stops it on unmount. The React, Vue, Svelte and Solid bindings run it.
- Every block exports a manifest built with `defineBlock` (`commentsBlock` from `better-supabase/blocks/comments`): its SQL modules, option keys, its own events, the ports it provides and consumes, its creator, category and UI recipes. `createBlocks(options, blocks)` takes manifests and wires them on their ports (`notifier`, `files`, `attachments`, `workflows`, `sink`) instead of on names. `block.with(options)` sets a block's own options, and `block.using(create)` builds it with your own call to its creator. `defineBlocks(blocks, ...appEvents)` types the events of every block and the app's.

  Breaking:

  - `createBlocks` no longer takes factories or the `aiTaskNotification`, `reminderNotification`, `metricEvents` and `metricTime` options. Use `aiTasksBlock.with({ notification })`, `remindersBlock.with({ notification })` and `metricsBlock.with({ sink: { on, time } })`.
  - The closed `BlockEventMap` is gone. Each block exports its own event map (`CommentsEvents` and `commentsEvents`), `onBlockEvent(betterSupabase, events, pattern, handler)` takes the events to type by, `blockEmitter`, `emitBlockEvent`, `BlockEvent`, `BlockEventSchemas` and `SubjectEventType` take an event map, and `defineEvents` declares app events.
  - `createDrive` takes the attachments block as `attachments` instead of building one (`attachmentsSchema`, `requireScan` and `downloadTtl` move to that block), and the workflow builder takes the workflows block as `workflows` for schedule triggers.
  - `createBuilder` is renamed `createWorkflowBuilder` and `createPushDevices` is renamed `createPush`.
- Blocks are configured under a top-level `blocks` key in `better-supabase.config.ts`, the foundation blocks (`tenant`, `access`, `updated-at`, `ensure-rls`, ...) included. `sql.modules` is removed; `sql` keeps the SQL-wide settings (`workflow`, `dir`, `prefix`, `testsDir`, `schema`, `api`). The resolved config carries `blocks` and `blockNames`, `sql add` edits `blocks`, and `schemas/config-v1.json` describes the new key. The config types are renamed to match: `BlocksConfig`, `BlockConfig`, `AccessBlockConfig` and `BlockMode`. Code that reads `config.sql.modules` from a loaded or resolved config must read `config.blocks` instead.
- More blocks meet the block contract. `createFlagAdmin` is now `createFlags`. `createJobs({ queues, sql })` replaces `createJobs(source, queues, options)`, taking its store as `backend`, `sql`, `client` or `transport`, and the jobs block is built by `createBlocks`. `createIdempotency({ sql })` and `createIncomingWebhooks({ sql })` take their connection as an option, and the entitlements block gets `createEntitlements` with `has` and `value`.

  Deals, drive, employees, engagements, flags, forms, hiring, inventory and invoices take `options.extraColumns` on their main table and a Standard Schema as `fields`, which types and parses the columns on reads and validates them on writes. `create_form`, `update_form` and `save_location` take a `fields` argument. Data-lifecycle, entitlements, flags, idempotency, identities, imports, inbox, incoming webhooks, jobs, knowledge and memory accept `hooks`; inbox, knowledge and memory also take hooks per method group. Calls over a transport go through `blockCall`, so `mappers` apply to them.
- SQL module comments and error messages name the `blocks` config key instead of the old `modules` key, for example `blocks["support-sessions"].options.allowWrites` in the `SUPPORT_WRITES_DISABLED` error.
- Add the bookings block (`better-supabase/blocks/bookings` and `better-supabase/blocks/bookings/react`): bookable resources with weekly hours, exceptions and a capacity, services with a duration and buffers, open slots from the `available_slots` SQL function, holds that a `bookings.expire-holds` job cancels, an exclusion constraint that refuses double bookings (the module installs `btree_gist`), public booking through a share link with a rate limit and an access token, confirmed bookings copied into the calendar block, customer reminders, and a `useAvailableSlots` hook typed for headless time pickers such as ReUI's.
- The bulk module adds a `trash.trash` action when the trash module is installed, so a list's bulk bar can move many rows to the trash in one call or a background run, each row through the trash block's own checks.
- Add bulk actions (`better-supabase/blocks/bulk` and the `bulk` SQL module). `bulk.run` applies `tags.set`, `stateMachine.transition`, `relations.link` or an app's own action to up to 500 rows in one call with a result per row, and `bulk.start` puts larger sets on the jobs queue `bulk`, where each step runs as the user who started the run and broadcasts its progress on a Realtime topic.
- Adds the calendar block (`better-supabase/blocks/calendar` and `better-supabase/blocks/calendar/react`, SQL module `calendar`). It stores calendars, events with RFC 5545 recurrence, overrides and attendees. `occurrences()` expands series with the reminders block's `expandRule`, `editOccurrence` and `editFollowing` override one occurrence or split a series, `importIcs` and `exportIcs` read and write iCalendar files, and a share link to a calendar serves an iCal feed. `useCalendar` returns events in the shape an event calendar renders.

  `expandRule` takes a `window` so long-running series expand only the occurrences in a range, and `revoke_share_link` no longer fails with an ambiguous `id` column.
- Close the gaps CentraKit hit while adopting better-supabase:

  - Support sessions work with an app-owned request context, and the proxy prunes stale cookie chunks and takes an auth-outage hook.
  - `createJobs`, `createIdempotency` and `withLease` accept a block transport (`rpcTransport`), so job stats, dead jobs, retries, idempotency keys and leases work through the `blocks.<name>.api` wrappers without a direct Postgres connection.
  - Jobs, idempotency, rate limits, outgoing webhooks and usage get TypeScript `purge` methods and `purgeJob` handlers for the `purge_*` functions; `purgeAuditLog` and the webhook inbox purge accept a transport.
  - Behaviour change: `cancel_organization_deletion` asks the access contract for the `cancelDelete` permission (`organization.delete` by default) through the new `member_can_while_deleting()`, instead of hard-coding the `owner` role. With the default roles nothing changes; with an authorization provider, any member the provider grants the key can cancel. An access module in `custom` mode keeps the owner check.
  - Attachments take `subjects.<type>.permissions` (`read`, `upload`, `manage`), and attachments and comments take `subjects.<type>.scope`: a scope the authorization provider declares, so a grant on the subject record counts next to one on the tenant. Comments' `readableBy` also takes `{ sql, enforce: true }`, which applies it to the read policy.
  - The `realtime-tables` module adds `better_supabase.broadcast_topic()`, a row trigger that fills a topic's `{column}` placeholders from the row and sends the change. `topic.trigger(table, options)` returns the `create trigger` statement, and `realtime.triggers` makes `sql sync` write them next to the topic policies.
  - Incoming webhooks hand verified deliveries to an `IncomingDestination` (`apiVersion: 1`, checked by `testIncomingDestination`): `inboxDestination` stores them in the webhook inbox as before, and `jobsDestination` enqueues them on a jobs queue once per endpoint and message id, with the endpoint's tenant and metadata. `jobs.enqueueOnce` (`enqueue_job_once` in SQL) is the queue side: a key stays taken after its job completes.
  - `webhooks-in` and `webhook-inbox` gain `mode: "adopt"` over an app's own endpoint and message tables, with renamed columns and optional ones mapped to `null`. Their functions stay in `better_supabase`. In managed mode a column mapping is an error.
  - `credentials` gains `mode: "adopt"` over an app's own secrets table (`provider`, `name` and optional `tenant`, `scope`, `vaultId`, `secret`, `updatedAt` columns), with `secretStorage: "vault"` (a Vault id per row, deleted with its row) or `"column"`. `credential_get`, `credential_set` and `credential_delete` keep their signatures, so `vaultCredentials()` reads the table unchanged; a `vault` ref names another provider's row as `<provider>:<name>`. Doctor reports a table or column an adopt-mode module maps that the live database lacks (BS330).
- `supabaseClaimFixtures` adds `agentRunReadOnly`, an agent level with `read_only: true`. The new `supabaseInvalidClaimFixtures` (with the `SupabaseInvalidClaimFixture` type) lists the claims better-supabase refuses, each expecting `{ kind: "invalid", reason: "actor", code: "ACTOR_INVALID" }`: a 0.5.0 support token (`session_id` without `kind`), a support or agent `read_only` that is not a boolean, an agent with a `session_id`, an empty tool name or `tools` that is not a list, an unknown `kind`, and a nested level without `sub`. They are plain data, so other libraries can run the same cases.
- The CLI has an agent mode. When a coding agent runs it (Cursor, Claude Code, Codex, Gemini CLI, GitHub Copilot and the others `@vercel/detect-agent` knows), it prints the JSON result envelope by default, never prompts, and answers what it can't decide alone with exit code 3 and `status: "action_required"`. `--agent auto|yes|no` and `$BETTER_SUPABASE_AGENT` override the detection, and `--no-json` keeps text output.

  A missing value returns `input_required` with `missing`, `choices` and a `next` command that fills them in. Destructive work returns `consent_required` with a `token`: `--force` over files the CLI didn't write, and `seed --apply` against a database that isn't on this machine. Rerun with `--confirm <token>` once the user agrees; `--yes` doesn't replace it.

  `init` at a workspace root without `--package` now exits 3 with `input_required` instead of 2, and `skills install --agent` is now `skills install --for`, so the global `--agent` keeps one meaning.
- The CLI's command groups (`sql`, `blocks`, `preset`, `skills`, `spec`, `scaffold` and `codemod`) are real subcommands, each with its own help, and a flag has one meaning everywhere. `openapi emit` moves to `spec emit openapi` and `spec check` to `spec emit --check`. These flags are renamed:

  | 0.8                              | 0.9                            |
  | -------------------------------- | ------------------------------ |
  | `init --with`                    | `init --integration`           |
  | `add --framework`                | `add --integration`            |
  | `adopt --from`                   | `adopt --tables`               |
  | `gen --tasks`                    | `gen --only`                   |
  | `gen --out` (a directory)        | `gen --out-dir`                |
  | `introspect --format`            | `introspect --kind`            |
  | `doctor --strict`                | `doctor --fail-on warning`     |
  | `env --print`                    | `env --out -`                  |
  | `env --from`                     | `env --status-file`            |
  | `skills install --from`          | `skills install --source`      |
  | `skills install --agent`         | `skills install --for`         |
  | `sql add --force` (hook overlap) | `sql add --allow-hook-overlap` |

  An old command or flag exits with code 2 and a `command_moved` error whose `suggestion` is the new call. `better-supabase codemod 0.9` rewrites these calls in `package.json` scripts and GitHub workflows and lists them in other shell files. The package adds a `bsb` bin next to `better-supabase`.
- Add `better-supabase completion bash|zsh|fish`. It prints a script that
  completes commands, subcommands, options and their values (block names,
  presets, `gen --only` tasks, doctor rule codes) for both `better-supabase` and
  `bsb`. Add `eval "$(better-supabase completion zsh)"` to `~/.zshrc`, or the
  line for your shell from the [shell completion](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/cli/completion)
  page.
- `better-supabase/cli` exports `createCli({ commands })` in place of `registerCommand`. It returns a CLI with your commands next to the built-in ones, so two CLIs in one process share no state. `defineCliCommand` now marks each command with `apiVersion: 1`, and `createCli` refuses a command without it or one that takes a built-in or reserved name (`login`, `logout`, `whoami`, `link`, `projects`, `deploy`, `cloud`).

  The 0.2 command form, `(context) => CommandResult`, and the `Command`, `CommandContext` and `ParsedArgs` types are removed. Wrap such a command in `defineCliCommand`:

  ```ts
  // Before
  registerCommand("schemas", ({ args }) => ({
    code: 0,
    output: args.rest.join(" "),
  }));

  // After
  const cli = createCli({
    commands: {
      schemas: defineCliCommand({
        meta: { name: "schemas" },
        args: {},
        run: async (args) => ({ code: 0, output: args._.join(" ") }),
      }),
    },
  });
  await cli.run(process.argv.slice(2), { cwd: process.cwd(), env: process.env });
  ```

  `doctor --metadata` now reports a conflicting flag with the same message as `gen --metadata`, and `doctor` lists `--snapshot`, `--db-url-stdin` and `--project-ref` first in its help.
- The CLI reports three more specific problem codes, all exit 2: `unknown_flag` for an option the command does not take, `invalid_value` for an option or environment value it does not accept (an enum, `--agent`, `--cwd`), and `flag_renamed` for a renamed flag, which was `command_moved` before. A script that matched `usage` for these cases should match the new codes. `ExitCode` now includes `65` and `143`. On SIGINT or SIGTERM a run gets 2 seconds to clean up before the process exits, and a second signal exits at once.
- The CLI reports every failure with an error code and a matching exit code. The errors page lists each code, and `--json` puts it in `code`.

  - Exit code 3 means you have to act first, 130 means a cancelled prompt or Ctrl-C, and 143 means SIGTERM. A cancelled prompt no longer counts as "no".
  - A path flag outside the project root (`outside_root`) exits 2, like other usage errors, instead of 1.
  - `--cwd` has to name an existing directory.
  - `--db-url-stdin` and `gen --metadata -` fail at once when stdin is a terminal, instead of waiting for input.
  - `$CI=false` and `$CI=0` no longer turn prompts off.
  - The CLI never downloads the Supabase CLI through `npx --yes`. It runs `$SUPABASE_BIN`, then `node_modules/.bin/supabase`, then `supabase` on the `PATH`, and fails with `supabase_cli_missing` otherwise. The child process gets the run's environment and stops on Ctrl-C.
  - Install commands quote version ranges and directories that the shell would split or expand.
  - `tsconfig.json` and `package.json` are read as JSONC, so comments inside strings and trailing commas parse correctly.
  - The config file is discovered once per run, and the local stack's database URL is looked up once.
  - The package no longer ships `dist/cli/bin.d.ts`.
- Every CLI command that writes files now goes through one file plan. Each file gets one line: `Created`, `Updated`, `Unchanged`, `Skipped` (with the reason) or `Removed`, and `Would create` or `Would update` under `--dry-run`. These lines replace `Wrote`, `Kept` and `Would write`, so update any script that matched the old words. Under `--json`, `data.files` lists every file with its outcome; `seed`, `env` and `openapi emit` no longer return `data.wrote`, and `gen` no longer returns `data.removed`.

  Every writing command takes `--dry-run`, which prints a diff and writes nothing, and `--check`, which exits 1 on drift. `gen`, `introspect`, `scaffold`, `seed`, `env`, `keys`, `skills`, `spec`, `codemod`, `apply`, `adopt` and `openapi emit` gain the flags they were missing. A command writes its files together, so a failed run leaves none half written. It refuses paths outside the project root and never prints the contents of `.env.local` or the signing keys in a diff.

  A generated file without the `Generated by better-supabase` header was written by hand. `gen` used to overwrite it; now every command skips it and exits 1 with the new `file_conflict` error code. Pass `--force` to overwrite it.
- The CLI keeps stdout for the command's output. Prompts, spinners, warnings and notices go to stderr, and each stream is colored only when it is a terminal. Outside a terminal, progress prints one plain line instead of a spinner.

  - `--no-color` turns colors off on both streams, like `NO_COLOR`.
  - `--debug` prints the stack trace of an error, each child command the CLI runs with its exit code, and how long the run and each step took, all on stderr.
  - `gen --watch --json` and `spec emit --watch --json` print one result envelope per run on its own line (NDJSON) instead of text.
  - `spec emit --watch` now waits for a config that fails to load, the way `gen --watch` does, instead of running with the old one.
  - Commands registered with `defineCliCommand` get `log` (`info`, `warn`, `error`, `debug`, `progress`), `paint` and `emit` in their context. `CliIo` takes `stderrColor` and `spinner`, and `Prompter` no longer has a `spinner` method.
- Breaking for scripts that read `--json`: every command now prints one result envelope that follows `schemas/cli-result-v1.json`. It has `status` (`success`, `error` or `action_required`), the command's result under `data`, Problem Details under `error`, and the commands to run next under `next`. Read `.data.stale` where you read `.stale` before, and `.error.code` where you read `.code`. A failed `--check` keeps its `data`, so the stale files and the error come in one document.

  Every command returns data now, including `init`, `add`, `env`, `keys`, `skills`, `openapi`, `scaffold`, `seed` and `sql`. `--check` drift reports the `check_failed` code, doctor and spec findings report `findings`, and a usage error keeps the `usage` code and prints the command's usage in a terminal. In a terminal, `init`, `add`, `sql add` and `sql upgrade` list the commands to run next under `Next:`.
- The CLI checks its arguments before a command runs. An unknown option, a value outside an option's choices, or an argument a command doesn't take is now a usage error (exit code 2) with a suggestion, such as `Unknown option --chek for better-supabase gen. Did you mean --check?`. Under `--json` the error is a Problem Details document with the `usage` code.

  Help is grouped by task (set up, generate, local stack, inspect, maintain), and each command's help lists its arguments, options, examples, side effects and docs page. `better-supabase --help --json` prints every command, option and side effect as one document that matches `schemas/cli-commands-v1.json`, and `better-supabase <command> --help --json` prints one command.
- `better-supabase codemod 0.9` moves `sql.modules` to the top-level `blocks` key in `better-supabase.config.ts` (and `.mts`, `.js`, `.mjs`, `.json`), keeping the comments around it and dropping `sql` when nothing else is left in it, and renames `ModulesConfig`, `ModuleConfig`, `AccessModuleConfig` and `ModuleMode` to `BlocksConfig`, `BlockConfig`, `AccessBlockConfig` and `BlockMode`. A config that sets both keys, or keeps `sql.modules` in an environment block, is listed for review. A config that still sets `sql.modules` fails to load with `config_invalid` and names the codemod.
- Add the `access-requests` block (`better-supabase/blocks/access-requests` and the `access-requests` SQL module): members ask for a role, for a set time if they like, and the request goes through the approvals module. The final approval grants the role, `end()` and `expire_access_grants()` put the previous role back, and the module records `access.requested`, `access.request_approved` and `access.ended`. With the `provider` access model the adapter grants the role from the events. Approvals gains an internal `open_approval` that files a request under an explicit policy.
- Add the `approvals` block (`better-supabase/blocks/approvals` and the `approvals` SQL module): approval requests with steps per subject type and action, approvers by permission, role, team or user, a required count, delegation, self-approval off by default, expiry and reminders. The final approval runs a state-machine transition or enqueues a job, approvers and requesters are notified through the notifications module, and the module records `approval.*` events. State machines gain `requiresApproval`, which reserves transitions for an approved request (`STATE_APPROVAL_REQUIRED`).
- Add the `documents` block (`better-supabase/blocks/documents` and the `documents` SQL module) and the `better-supabase/yjs` provider. A document is a Yjs update log and a compacted snapshot attached to any subject, with a private `documents:<id>` Realtime topic for edits and presence under the subject's read and write rules. `createDocumentProvider` connects a `Y.Doc`: it loads the stored state, stores and broadcasts each edit, and catches up after a reconnect. `compactDocument` merges the log for the `documents.compact` job, and with the versions module a document's state can be saved as a version. `yjs` (`>=13.6 <14`) is a new optional peer.
- Add the `reactions` block (`better-supabase/blocks/reactions`, `better-supabase/blocks/reactions/react` and the `reactions` SQL module): emoji reactions on any registry subject, comments and inbox messages, checked against each subject's own read policies, with counts, `reacted` and the first actors per emoji, custom emoji per organization, a `reactions:{organizationId}` realtime topic and `useReactions()`. The module records `reaction.added` and `reaction.removed`. With it installed, the inbox's `react_to_message` stores reactions in the table and keeps the message's `reactions` column as a copy; installing it copies existing message reactions.
- Add the `trash` block (`better-supabase/blocks/trash` and the `trash` SQL module): soft delete through each subject's `deletedAt` column, children trashed in the same batch, restore with unique column checks, a list per organization, `purge()`, `purge_expired_trash()` after `retentionDays`, and `is_trashed()` for policies. The module records `subject.trashed`, `subject.restored` and `subject.purged`.
- Add the `versions` block (`better-supabase/blocks/versions` and the `versions` SQL module): numbered snapshots of any subject with a content hash, labels, debounced auto snapshots, versions frozen when a status column changes, retention, restore through columns or an apply function, and `diff()` as an RFC 6902 JSON Patch. `createPatch()` and `applyPatch()` are exported for any JSON value. The module records `version.created`, `version.restored` and `version.deleted`.
- The `collections` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/collections` add favorites and lists on any record registered in `config.subjects`. Favorites are a member's own stars, ranked for a sidebar (`star`, `unstar`, `starred`, `reorderStar`). Lists are named, static collections of one record type owned by a user, a team or the organization (`create`, `rename`, `archive`, `add`, `remove`, `reorder`, keyset-paged `entries` and `listsOf`); an entry is visible only when the caller can read its record, and deleting a record with `cascade: true` deletes its entries and stars. A saved view can keep to a list through `state.collectionId` and `collections.where(id)`, and the bulk module gains the `collections.add` action.
- The comments module (version 4) filters activity per record when the `subjects` module is installed: the read policy on `activity_entries` checks `can_subject(subject_type, subject_id, 'read')` for every registered subject type, and `list_activity` returns an empty timeline for a subject the caller can't read. Members no longer see entries on records they can't read, or on deleted records. Entries without a subject, or on a type the registry doesn't list, keep the tenant check alone.

  With the audit module installed with `options.readPolicy: true`, a subject's timeline also returns the audit log entries for its row, gated by the audit read policy. `ActivityEntry` has a new `source` field (`ActivitySource`, `"event"` or `"audit"`), so code that builds `ActivityEntry` values by hand needs to set it.

  With the notifications module installed, the author of a comment and the users it mentions follow the subject as `participating` watchers in `notification_subscriptions`; an existing row, such as `ignore`, is kept. `options.follow: false` turns this off. Run `sql sync` to rewrite the installed module.
- Add client controllers and hooks for the `comments` and `organizations` blocks. `createCommentsController` and `useComments()` (`better-supabase/blocks/comments/react`, `/vue`, `/svelte` and `/solid`) load one subject's thread and keep it current after `add`, `edit` and `remove`, which return a `Result`. `createOrganizationsController` and `useOrganizations()` (`better-supabase/blocks/organizations/react`, `/vue`, `/svelte` and `/solid`) list the caller's organizations, read the active one from the tenant claim, and switch with `switchTo()`, which refreshes the session when `switch_organization` asks for it. Both hooks run in React DOM and React Native, and the comment thread and organization switcher recipes show them in the Next.js and Expo examples.
- Add the `catalog` block and SQL module (`better-supabase/blocks/catalog`): products with a draft, active and archived status, options and variants with per-organization SKUs, prices in bigint minor units per currency and price list with validity windows, a category tree, manual and rule collections, and an opt-in public storefront. `catalogSubjects()` registers products and variants as subjects. Grant `catalog.read` and `catalog.manage` to the roles that use it.
- Add `better-supabase/blocks/commerce`. It holds money as a `bigint` count of the currency's minor units, using ISO 4217 List One (pinned as `SPEC_PINS.iso4217`), with arithmetic, half-to-even rates, allocation, parsing, formatting and JSON. It also defines the versioned `PaymentProvider` and `TaxProvider` interfaces. `stripePayments` implements the first on Stripe PaymentIntents and webhooks. `stripeTax` and `fixedRates` implement the second. `better-supabase/testing` adds their conformance kits, `testPaymentProvider` and `testTaxProvider`.
- Add the inventory block (`better-supabase/blocks/inventory`) and its `inventory` SQL module: locations, stock levels per catalog variant and location kept by an append-only movement ledger, reservations that expire (lazily and through a service-only `expire_reservations` job), transfers between locations and purchase orders with partial receiving and backorders. It emits `stock.*`, `reservation.*`, `transfer.*` and `purchase_order.*` events, and the catalog refuses to delete variants with stock history.
- Add the `orders` block and SQL module (`better-supabase/blocks/orders`). Carts belong to the signed-in user, anonymous users included, and `mergeCarts` moves them to the account the shopper signs in to. `checkout` prices the cart from the catalog, quotes tax through a `TaxProvider`, places the order with a number from `number-sequences`, reserves inventory and creates the payment through a `PaymentProvider` with an idempotency key. Paid orders turn their reservations into sale movements and get an invoice number. Fulfillments, returns and refunds (with a proportional tax reversal) follow, and orders emit `order.*`, `fulfillment.*` and `return.*` subject events. Grant `orders.read` and `orders.manage` to the roles that run the shop.

  `sqlTransport` now sends an array of objects as JSON, so `jsonb` arguments that take a list work over it.
- Add the `promotions` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/promotions`. Promotions take a percentage or a fixed amount off, or ship for free, for the whole cart or for products, categories (with their children) or manual collections. They apply automatically or by code, with single codes or generated batches, a minimum subtotal, a start and end, usage and per-customer limits, stacking rules and a priority. `orders` prices them into `priceCart`, lists the applied promotions, reports a code that doesn't apply as a cart problem, and redeems them at checkout with a row lock; cancelling an order releases its redemptions. Applied promotions in `PricedCart` and `Order` now carry their `kind`, and cart problems carry the `promotionId` and `reason`. `merge_carts` moves the promotion codes of an anonymous cart to the account's cart.
- **Breaking:** share-links, signatures, sla, tags, targets, teams, templates, time-tracking, trash, versions, waitlist, webhook-inbox, work-items and workflow-builder take `options.extraColumns` on their main table and a Standard Schema `fields` option on the creator; reads return the columns parsed under `fields`. share-links, sso, state-machine, tags, teams, undo, usage, waitlist, webhooks, workflow-builder and workflows take `hooks` (grouped by namespace for workflows and workflow-builder). `createWebhookInbox` takes `{ transport, ... }` instead of a SQL client: it no longer takes the client as its first argument, so `createWebhookInbox(postgres.admin, { source })` becomes `createWebhookInbox({ transport: sqlTransport(postgres.admin), source })`, and `receive_webhook`, `claim_webhooks` and `list_webhooks` return jsonb (webhook-inbox module version 7). `shareLinks.list` returns typed links. The money and payment helpers move from `better-supabase/blocks/commerce` to `better-supabase/commerce`.
- `createBlocks` returns `sinks`: a `searchSink` over the search block, so trashed, purged and restored subjects update the index, and a `metricsSink` over the metrics block for the events in the metrics block's `sink` option. Pass them to `outbox.relayRoute({ consumers: { ...app.sinks } })`.
- Create, the config and the CLI now offer the same conventions under the same names.

  `init` and `apply` take `--casing`, `--scope`, `--tenant-name`, `--access`, `--id-type` and `--api`. With a preset, a flag changes that field and the CLI writes the new preset code, so `preset info` and Create still read it. Without a preset, `init` writes the matching config keys, and it asks for each convention in Create's order and with Create's labels; `--yes` and CI use the defaults. `casing` defaults to `snake` everywhere, as it does on Create. With `--access provider`, `init` writes the same `authorization` comment Create's config does.

  `apply` takes `--package`, and both commands read `package=` from a Create URL. A URL with `authz=` prints a note that the authorization library isn't part of the preset.

  `plugins.tenant.column` and the tenant column of a subject without `tenant` now default to `<sql.naming.tenant>_id`, such as `workspace_id`, instead of always `organization_id`. Set the column to keep the old name.

  The config check now rejects what the types reject: a `json` entry without `import` or `type`, a malformed bucket (`path`, `policy`, `lifecycle`) and an unknown `sql.workflow`. `schemas/config-v1.json` is generated from the same schema, so the editor checks match the CLI, and it drops `triggerPrefix` and `disabled.tenantKey`, which the config never read.
- The custom-fields client gains `listDefinitions(organizationId, entity)`, which returns an entity's field definitions in rank order with their rank keys, so a settings page can show and reorder them. It calls the new SQL function `list_field_definitions(tenant, entity)`, which needs `custom_fields.read`. Run `better-supabase sql sync` to install it.
- The `deals` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/deals` add deals on pipelines. Installing deals binds the `deal` type in the pipelines module with a Qualified to Closed won or Closed lost ladder. A deal has an amount in minor units, a company and contacts from `config.subjects`, a team, line items, a motion, a priority, a forecast call, flags and a next step. `update(changes)` applies grid edits one by one and returns the refused ones with their hint. The table refuses edits to a locked deal, keeps the open date, counts close-date slips and stamps `closedAt` with `deal.closed`. `win`, `lose` and `reopen` move a deal through its pipeline's stages, `forecast` returns won, commit, best case, pipeline, not called and later with attainment and gap, and `health` flags rotting, slipped, missing and overdue next steps.

  Line items share one shape across blocks: `set_<record>_lines` replaces a record's lines and a trigger keeps its list, discount, subtotal, tax and total.
- Delegated tokens are narrowed inside the access functions. A token with a `client_id`, or an `act` chain that is not a support or impersonation session, passes `member_can`, `member_can_while_deleting`, `platform_can` and `tenant_ids_with` only for the permission keys its scopes map to, through the new `better_supabase.cap_allows(permission)`. Map scopes in `blocks.access.scopes` or `authorization.delegation.scopes` (a scope without an entry grants the key with its own name, `["*"]` grants every key). `forContext(context, { permissions })` sets a `bs_permission_cap` claim that caps the work the same way, and a provider can map scopes at runtime, per client or from RFC 9396 `authorization_details`, with the `delegation.permissions` hook.

  Breaking: an OAuth client or MCP server whose scopes map to no permission key is now refused where it used to pass with the user's roles. The 0.8 to 0.9 guide lists the mapping to add.
- `session.delegation.scopes` and `delegationOf(claims).scopes` leave out the OpenID Connect identity scopes, exported as `IDENTITY_SCOPES` from `better-supabase/server` (`openid`, `email`, `profile`, `phone`, `address`, `offline_access`). They grant no permission. A token whose `scope` names only identity scopes still has a delegation, with `scopes: []`, so an agent on it stays limited to its scopes. Code that looked for `openid` in `delegation.scopes` should read `claims.scope` instead.
- Delivery fails closed on records under an authorization provider. Without `delivery.subscribers` or `delivery.audience`, webhooks, notifications and reminders deliver an event about a registered subject only to users and service principals who may read it, through the new `can_subject_for(user, type, id, action)` in the `subjects` module and `subjectReaders(transport)`. Webhooks check each delivery against its stored subject, and a share link never counts.

  Breaking: realtime `fanOut` under a server with authorization hooks and no subscriber policy sends an event with a `subject` to no one and returns `forbidden` with the hint `REALTIME_SUBSCRIBER_POLICY_REQUIRED`; pass `policy: subjectReaders(transport)`.
- `doctor` reports BS223 (info) for functions in an exposed schema that return a scalar `int8` or `numeric`, or an array of them. PostgREST sends those as JSON numbers, so values past 2^53 lose precision before `$rpc` sees them; return them as `text` instead.
- Doctor warns with BS416 when the `support-sessions` block runs under the provider access model and `authorization.functions.isApprover` never handles `{kind} = 'support-session'`, in the template or in a function it calls. Such a template answers false, so no admin can approve a support request. BS413, which warns when support sessions run under a provider without `actorMayWrite`, now looks for the `support-sessions` block; it checked a block name that doesn't exist and never fired.
- New block `better-supabase/blocks/domains` (`better-supabase add domains`): the hostnames an organization serves your app on. A custom domain is verified by a DNS TXT record that `createDomainsAdmin().verify()` looks up over DNS-over-HTTPS, and with `blocks.domains.options.platformDomain` set, one label below your domain is a subdomain verified when added (checked against `reserved-slugs` when that module is installed). Each organization has at most one primary hostname, only one organization can hold a verified hostname, and `resolve(host)` returns the organization behind a request's `Host` header for the proxy. The `access` module adds `domains.read` (admins and members) and `domains.manage` (admins); re-run `better-supabase sql sync` to pick up the new default permissions.
- Adds the drive block (`sql add drive`, `better-supabase/blocks/drive`): folders and files on attachments, with grants to users, teams or roles that apply to everything under a folder. The same check runs in the drive functions, the attachment policies and restrictive `storage.objects` policies. Moves and renames follow a name-conflict policy. With versions, trash and share-links installed, the drive also keeps revisions, a bin, starred and recent items and folder share links. `driveTree` shapes nodes for a tree view, and `driveSubject()` lets other blocks attach to nodes. Adopt mode runs the drive on an existing tree and grant table. A SQL module can now set `before` to render ahead of another module without renumbering the generated files, and `drive_share` takes a team id from the teams table, so an adopted uuid team column accepts it.
- The `employees` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/employees` add the people an organization employs, with or without an account. An employee has a manager, team, job title, employment type, start and end date, and a status of preboarding, active, on leave or offboarded; the database refuses a manager who reports to the employee. `update` applies batched cell edits, `orgChart` and `reportsOf` read the tree (managers read their own reports), and `offboard` sets the end date and moves the reports up. `fromApplication` makes one employee from a hired application of the hiring block. Private details and a contract history with salaries in minor units sit behind `employees.view_sensitive`, declared to `data-lifecycle` for exports and anonymize rules. `employeesSubjects()` registers employees in `config.subjects`.
- The `engagements` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/engagements` log calls, emails, meetings, notes and tasks on one or more records registered in `config.subjects`, with an owner, a due time, an outcome and an external id that makes a repeat log an update. `of(subject)` reads a record's engagements, `next(subject)` its next planned step with an overdue flag, and `open(organizationId)` a member's open tasks. An engagement is visible to callers who can read one of its records, and assigning it to someone else notifies them.
- The `entitlements` module reads the Stripe customer from the `billing` or `organizations` module through their configured names: a renamed `billing_customers` table, its `customer` and `tenant` columns, or an `organizations` table in another schema or with another id column now resolve correctly instead of falling back to `stripe_customer_id`, `organization_id` and `better_supabase.organizations`. `entitlements` lists both modules in `integrates`.
- `evaluateFilterQuery(query, record, fields, clock)` from `better-supabase/list` tests a `FilterQuery` tree on one record in memory, with the same operators, case rules, relative dates and null handling as `filterWhere` compiles to SQL. It returns `matched` and the result of every rule and group by node id (`null` when a comparison met a missing value). A field's `column` may be dotted to read a nested value, and `FilterRecordField` describes fields that aren't table columns.
- `actorOf(claims)` and `delegationOf(claims)` are exported from `better-supabase/server`, with the `ActorOutcome` type, so another library can read an `act` and `scope` claim the way `toSession` does without building an `AuthState`.
- `blocks.data-lifecycle.options.omitPattern` leaves columns out of data exports by name. `true` matches `token`, `secret`, `_hash`, `password`, `api_key` and `license_key`, and a string is a Postgres regular expression; `data_lifecycle_tables()` then appends the matching columns of each listed and `autoTables` table to its `omit` when an export runs. It is off by default, so existing exports don't change. Doctor's new BS341 warns about exported columns that match the built-in pattern and that no `omit` or `omitPattern` leaves out.
- `expose` privileges can grant every column but a few: `select(*, -token_hash)`. `sql sync` turns the list into the columns the table's `create table` declares, in order, and fails when no schema file declares the table or the exclusions leave nothing. `better-supabase/sql` exports `resolveExposeColumns` for apps that render the grants module themselves, and the module refuses an unresolved `*`. Doctor's new warning BS339 reports an excluded column the table no longer has.
- Custom fields gain six types: `email`, `url`, `multi_option`, `user`, `rating` and `currency`. `CUSTOM_FIELD_TYPES` and `CustomFieldType` are exported from `better-supabase/blocks/custom-fields`, and `saveDefinition` takes a `CustomFieldType`. A form field that names a custom field reads the matching forms type: `multi_option` becomes `multi`, `rating` becomes `integer` from 1 to 5, `currency` becomes `integer` (minor units) and `user` becomes `text`.

  App table formats and custom field types now come from one list. `FIELD_FORMATS` from `better-supabase/config` gives each format its column types, its check and the input a form renders, and `scaffold screens` reads the input from it.

  Migration: run `better-supabase sql sync` and generate a migration. The custom-fields module replaces the inline `field_definitions_type_check` constraint with a named `bs_field_definitions_type` check that lists the new types.
- Add `better-supabase/flags-sdk`, a [Flags SDK](https://flags-sdk.dev) adapter over the flags block. `betterSupabaseAdapter()` evaluates `flag()` declarations from the flags table with the same rules as `flag_enabled()`, `createIdentify()` returns the user, the tenant and custom contexts from verified claims (the same entities `vercelAdapter` targets), and `getProviderData()` feeds Flags Explorer. See [Flags SDK](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/flags-sdk).

  The flags module moves to version 3. Run `better-supabase sql upgrade` to add:

  - Custom contexts, such as a brand: declare them in `blocks.flags.options.contexts`, target them with a rule's `contexts` or an override with `context` and `contextKey`, and pass their values through `flagContext({ contexts })` or `contextClaims`. `flag_evaluation()` takes a fourth `contexts` argument and reads a missing value from the tenant.
  - `description` and `archived` on flags. An archived flag serves its default variant; `save` and `FlagInput` accept both.
  - `createFlagsProvider().definitions()`, which returns the cached definitions.

  `better-supabase doctor` warns about flags past their `expires_at` extra column that aren't archived (BS336), and `expiredFlagsQuery()` in `better-supabase/sql` returns the query it runs.
- Add the `forms` block (`better-supabase/blocks/forms`). A form has numbered versions of steps and fields with `showIf` conditions, and each version stores a JSON Schema 2020-12 that `pg_jsonschema` checks on every submission. Submissions can be saved as drafts, linked to a record, or turned into one by a handler per form key. With `share-links`, a `respond` link opens the form to anyone who holds it, rate limited per caller with `rate-limit`, and `before_public_form_submission` receives a bot-check token. `useQuestionnaire` from `better-supabase/blocks/forms/react` drives a multi-step form from a version, and `validateAnswers` checks answers in the browser with the same schema.
- The frameworks the adapters support are now optional peer dependencies: `react-router` (7.9 to 8), `@sveltejs/kit` (2 and 3), `h3` (1 and 2), `nuxt` (3 and 4), `astro` (7), `@solidjs/start` (2), `@tanstack/react-start` (1), `elysia` (1), `express` (5), `fastify` (5) and `koa` (3). Nothing new is installed, but your package manager now warns when a framework is outside the majors the conformance tests cover. The ranges are on the [Peers](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/getting-started/peers) page.

  The conformance tests found these fixes:

  - `better-supabase/h3` and `better-supabase/solid-start` read the Cloudflare bindings from `event.req.runtime.cloudflare.env`, where Nitro 3 puts them. Before, `getEnv` found no bindings on Cloudflare with H3 2 or SolidStart 2.
  - The SvelteKit `handle` type-checks as SvelteKit's `Handle` (SvelteKit 2 and 3), so it works inside `sequence()`.
  - The SvelteKit adapter calls `error(status, message)` instead of the `error(status, { message })` form SvelteKit 3 deprecated. A custom `kit.error` now receives the message as a string.
  - The TanStack Start middleware accepts a `next()` that returns its result without a promise.
  - The Astro image service accepts any `quality` string. A string other than `low`, `mid`, `high` or `max` uses the default quality.
  - The Nuxt module type-checks as a module for Nuxt 3 as well as Nuxt 4.
- Block hooks now wrap headless controllers, so they run in React DOM and React Native alike. Each block with a `/react` entry exports `create<Name>Controller` from its main entry (`createNotificationsController`, `createCommandSearchController` and the rest), with `subscribe`, `getSnapshot`, `actions` and `dispose`; the hooks are `useSyncExternalStore` wrappers over them.

  Controllers reach the platform only through ports on the client, `bs.ports`: realtime topics (`topicPort` from `better-supabase/realtime`), storage, the keyboard, file picking and upload, push tokens and deep links. `createClient` from `better-supabase/client` fills the web ports (`webStorage`, `webKeyboard`, `webFilePicker`, `webDeepLinks`, `storageUpload`, and `webPushToken` on request), and `nativePorts()` from `better-supabase/client/native` builds the React Native ones from the Expo modules you pass to `createNativeClient(betterSupabase, supabase, { ports })`.

  Breaking changes: `useCommandSearch` listens for Cmd+K through the client's keyboard port instead of `window`, so React Native has no shortcut. `useInboxWidget` keeps the conversation id in the storage port instead of `localStorage`, and reads it after the first render. `groupHits` and its types moved from `blocks/search/react/groups.ts` to `blocks/search/groups.ts` (still exported from `better-supabase/blocks/search/react`).
- The `hiring` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/hiring` add an applicant tracking system on pipelines. Installing hiring binds the `application` type in the pipelines module with one pipeline per job (Applied, Screening, Interview, Offer, then Hired, Rejected or Withdrawn). Jobs open and close, a candidate is any record in `config.subjects` (a person by default) and applies once per job, and interviewers score the application at its current stage. `hire`, `reject` and `withdraw` end an application, and `application.hired` carries the candidate for the app to turn into an employee.
- `httpSink` aborts a request after `timeoutMs` (10 seconds by default) instead of waiting on an endpoint that never answers. `doctor` gives up on the splinter download and the Management API advisors after 30 seconds.
- `createIdempotency().handle` now renews the key's lock at half its length while the handler runs, so a handler slower than `lock` keeps its key instead of losing it to a retry. The new `extend(key, holder, scope)` does the same for callers of the lower-level protocol. Rerun `better-supabase sql sync` and generate a migration to add `better_supabase.extend_idempotent`.
- Add the `identities` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/identities`: `identityResolver` maps an email address or chat user to an app user or contact, linked only through a one-time code from `beginLink` that the service redeems with `redeemLink`, or a confirmed `auth.users` address. `better-supabase/chat-sdk` adds `botJobs`, which enqueues a linked author's job with a ticket and an unlinked author's with none, `chatActions`, which decides buttons through `chat.onAction` and refuses unlinked authors, and `approvalLink` and `approvalPrompt`, which link to the app's approval page without approving.
- The inbox no longer owns `inbox_teams` and `inbox_team_members`. It requires the `teams` block and assigns a conversation to a `teams` row. Installing both copies the old rows and drops the old tables. `create_inbox_team` and `set_inbox_team_member` call the teams functions. Team ids stay `uuid` while the inbox is installed.
- Jobs and workflow runs that an OAuth or MCP client queues keep the client's limits: the envelope records the token's `client_id`, `scope`, `authorization_details` and full RFC 8693 `act` chain, and `bs.forContext` runs with them instead of the user's full rights. The new SQL helper `better_supabase.token_scope_allows(scope)` in the `actor` module checks a delegated token's scope in policies.

  `enqueue` fails with `forbidden` for a context with an invalid `act` chain or malformed delegation claims, and `forContext` refuses such a stored context. `ServerOptions.claimsFor` can no longer set `act`, `client_id`, `scope` or `authorization_details`. A job's `delay` must be a whole number of seconds, else `enqueue` fails with `validation`.

  Jobs and workflows take an opaque `ticket`: the `ticket` enqueue option, `startFor(ctx, workflow, args, { ticket })`, `workflowContext(ctx, { ticket })` and `startOnEvent({ ticket })`. Runs carry it as the `bs.ticket` attribute, and `forContext` resolves it through `ForContextOptions.ticket` or the provider's `tickets.subject` hook, failing with `forbidden` when nothing resolves it. `startOnEvent`'s `args` now gets the event's context as a second argument.

  `better-supabase/workflow-sdk` exports `approvalWaiter`: `wait(token, { expiresAt })` pauses a run until `wake(token, payload)` or the RFC 3339 deadline.
- New block `better-supabase/blocks/leave` (`better-supabase add leave`): leave types in days or hours, requests that go through the `approvals` module, and a balance ledger per member and year. A request books the working days in `blocks.leave.options.workingDays` (Monday to Friday by default) or a half day, can't overlap another pending or approved request, and can't exceed the available balance unless `allowNegative` is on. The final approval books it as taken leave, and cancelling approved leave gives the days back. `createLeave()` adds `accrue` (idempotent with a key, for a monthly cron), `adjust` and `closePeriod`, which carries each balance up to the type's `carryoverMax` into the next year and expires the rest. The `access` module adds `leave.request` (members), `leave.read`, `leave.approve` and `leave.manage` (admins); re-run `better-supabase sql sync` to pick up the new default permissions.
- Add `filters` to `defineListQuery`: an allow-list of typed fields that a ReUI Filters `FilterQuery` tree may name. `parse` checks each rule's operator and value against the field type, caps depth and rule count, drops unfinished rules, and takes a `timeZone` that relative dates resolve in. The tree compiles to repository `where` input, so it runs through PostgREST, the Postgres executor and SQLite. `filterFields(labels)` returns ReUI `FilterField` objects, the URL keeps the tree in one base64url `filter` parameter that `toSearchParams` and the nuqs parsers handle, and `openapi` and `jsonSchema` describe it.
- Add client controllers and hooks for members, invitations, the audit log, API keys and tags. `useMembers()` and `useInvitations()` (`better-supabase/blocks/organizations/react`) list an organization's members and open invitations; `setRole()` shows a role change at once, `remove()` removes a member, and `invite()` keeps the token for the accept link in `sent`. `useAuditLog()` (`better-supabase/blocks/audit/react`) pages through the audit trail with `setFilters()` by actor, action or text and `loadMore()`. `useApiKeys()` (`better-supabase/blocks/api-keys/react`) lists, creates and revokes keys and shows a new token once. `useTagInput()` (`better-supabase/blocks/tags/react`) adds, removes and creates a record's tags. Each has a `create<Name>Controller` in the block's main entry and `/vue`, `/svelte` and `/solid` bindings, and runs in React DOM and React Native. The tags module gains `list_tags` and `subject_tags`, which `tags.list()` and `tags.forSubject()` call. The members table, invite dialog, API keys table, audit log table and tag input recipes are now available in the Next.js and Expo examples.
- Adds the `metrics` block (`better-supabase/blocks/metrics`). `defineMetric` declares a count, sum or average over a table with dimensions, a time zone and a permission. Metrics roll up into `metric_points` per grain through a `metrics.rollup` job or `metricsSink`, or are read from the table directly. `series()` and `kpi()` compare with the previous range, and `toChartData` returns Recharts rows. Usage events can be a metric's source.
- Add the `better-supabase/blocks/mfa` and `better-supabase/blocks/sessions` entries, with React, Vue, Svelte and Solid
  bindings. `useMfa` enrols, verifies and removes TOTP factors, and `useStepUp` raises a session to `aal2` after an
  `INSUFFICIENT_AAL` refusal (`needsStepUp`). `useOAuthConsent` serves the consent page of the Auth OAuth server, and
  `useOAuthGrants` lists and revokes the clients a user approved.
- Breaking: every amount with a currency in the deals, quotes, invoices, pipelines and employees blocks is a `Money` value (`{ amount: bigint, currency }` from `better-supabase/commerce`) instead of a bare `bigint`. Build one with `money(amount, currency)`. Line inputs, totals, payments, aging buckets, forecasts, board totals and contract salaries all change, and the separate `currency` input of a contract is gone because `salary` carries it. Target goals and progress stay `bigint`.

  | Old                                                                  | New                                                                           |
  | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | `deals.create(org, { amount: 4_800_000n, currency: "EUR" })`         | `deals.create(org, { amount: money(4_800_000n, "EUR") })`                     |
  | `{ description, unitAmount: 9_500n }`                                | `{ description, unitAmount: money(9_500n, "EUR") }`                           |
  | `invoices.recordPayment(id, { amount: 137_940n })`                   | `invoices.recordPayment(id, { amount: money(137_940n, "EUR") })`              |
  | `deals.forecast(org, { currency: "EUR", target: 60_000_000n })`      | `deals.forecast(org, { currency: "EUR", target: money(60_000_000n, "EUR") })` |
  | `employees.addContract(id, { salary: 5_400_000n, currency: "EUR" })` | `employees.addContract(id, { salary: money(5_400_000n, "EUR") })`             |
  | `invoice.totals.total`, `invoice.balance`: `bigint`                  | `Money`; read `.amount` for the `bigint`                                      |
  | board `totals: [{ currency, amount: bigint, weighted: bigint }]`     | `totals: [{ currency, amount: Money, weighted: Money }]`                      |

  Breaking: an amount in another currency than its record returns an `invalid_request` error with hint `CURRENCY_MISMATCH` before any database call, and an unknown ISO 4217 code returns `CURRENCY_INVALID`. A deal, quote or invoice created without a `currency` takes it from its amounts. The SQL checks the same rule: the line-items setters refuse a line in another currency (`LINES_CURRENCY`), `update_deals` rejects an amount change in another currency (`DEAL_CURRENCY_MISMATCH`), and `record_invoice_payment` is now `(invoice, amount, currency, paid_at, method, reference)` and refuses a payment in another currency (`INVOICE_CURRENCY_MISMATCH`). The old five-argument function is dropped.

  Breaking: the block SQL functions return every `int8` as text, and the clients read amounts only from an integer string or a `bigint`. Amounts past 2^53 now round-trip exactly, and a missing or malformed amount is an error instead of `0n`. Code that calls these functions through `$rpc` or `queryRaw` gets strings where it got numbers. Rerun `better-supabase sql sync` and generate a migration to install the new functions.
- The notifications module tracks what an email provider reports after a send. `notification_delivery_events` stores each `delivered`, `opened`, `clicked`, `bounced`, `complained` or `delayed` event once per provider event id, and deliveries gain `provider_status`, `provider_status_at` and `template_key`. `notifications.recordDeliveryEvent(input)` calls the service-only `record_delivery_event`, which moves the status forward, suppresses the address on a hard bounce or a complaint, and emits `notification.<kind>` with the notification's subject. `notifications.subjectDeliveries({ subject })` lists the deliveries of every notification about one record. A `templateKey` returned from the email channel's `compose` is stored on the delivery.

  `resendWebhook` now records every Resend delivery status before it writes suppressions, and `resendDeliveryEvent(event, id)` maps one event without the handler. `notification.delivered` and `notification.failed` now carry `subjectType`, `subjectId`, `organizationId` and `deliveryId`, and the five new events are subject events.

  `complete_notification_delivery` takes a seventh argument, `template_key`, and the six-argument signature is dropped. Run `better-supabase sql sync` to install module version 6; `resendWebhook` answers 500 against a database without `record_delivery_event`. An adopted module tracks delivery events only when `tables.deliveryEvents` names your table.
- `send` accepts `permission` (with a tenant) and reaches the users who hold that key. A provider supplies them through `authorization.functions.holdersOf`. The roles and catalog models derive holders from memberships. A provider model without `holdersOf` raises `NOTIFICATION_PERMISSION_AUDIENCE_UNAVAILABLE` instead of notifying every member.
- The `notifications` module takes `options.subjects` like the other subject-bound modules. A subject type with `cascade: true` gets an `after delete` trigger on its table that deletes the subject's notifications, with their recipients and deliveries, and the subscriptions to it. Without `options.subjects` the module reads `config.subjects`, so a registered type with `cascade: true` now cleans up its notifications too. The keys may contain hyphens (`time-off-request`), because `subject_type` in `notification_events` takes any text.
- `createNotifications` takes `email: { transport, from, compose?, unsubscribe? }`, an email channel over a `DeliveryTransport` (`apiVersion: 1`) that sends with the delivery id as the idempotency key, sets `Auto-Submitted: auto-generated` on all mail and `List-Unsubscribe` with one-click `List-Unsubscribe-Post` on notification mail only. `better-supabase/blocks/notifications` exports `resendTransport` (fetch-based, `Idempotency-Key` header), `resendWebhook` (verifies the Svix signature and records bounces and complaints), `mailHeaders` and `resendSuppressions`, and `better-supabase/testing` gets `testDeliveryTransport`.

  The notifications module (version 5) adds `notification_suppressions` with RLS and `suppress_notification_address`, `unsuppress_notification_address` and `list_notification_suppressions`; the block gets `suppress`, `unsuppress` and `suppressions`, and `deliver` skips a suppressed address. `createNotifications({ audience })` checks recipients before sending and falls back to `server.authorization.delivery.audience`. `defineTopic` takes `permission` (an instance permission from a topic segment, checked with the provider's `permitted` template or the access contract) and `subscribers`, and topics get `fanOut`.

  Breaking: run `better-supabase sql sync` and create a migration for the module upgrade; an adopted module honours suppressions only when `tables.suppressions` is mapped. `deliver()` counts suppressed and audience-denied deliveries in `skipped`. A hand-written `Topic` object needs `fanOut`.
- `gen` reads `@notNull` and `@notNull(a, b)` from a function's comment, so a function can say its result is never null next to its SQL instead of in `functions.<name>.notNull`. A config entry still wins. Snapshots record the comments that carry the tag (`extras.functionComments`), and doctor's new info rule BS338 lists the `notNull` entries a tag could replace or already matches.
- `accessTokenResolver({ jwks, issuer, audience })` from `better-supabase/server` verifies RFC 9068 access tokens that another authorization server issued, next to `apiKeyResolver`. A verified token resolves to a user with `source: "oauth"`, and its `sub`, `client_id` and `scope` reach `ctx.sql` and `forContext` as claims, so the request runs as the user under row level security and a `scope` limits it like any OAuth client. The resolver fails closed on a wrong issuer or audience, an expired token, a missing required claim, a `service_role` role or a sender-constrained token, and can check a revocation list keyed by `jti` and grant. JWS verification also accepts RS256 and PS256 keys.

  The new `oauth-server` block (`better-supabase/blocks/oauth-server`) stores what an authorization server needs: an RFC 7591 client registry with hashed secrets and registration tokens, authorization codes bound to an S256 PKCE challenge, RFC 8628 device codes, grants per user and client, and the revocation list. A replayed authorization code revokes its grant. The block issues no tokens; sign them with `createSigningKeys`. Install it with `better-supabase add oauth-server`. The pins are `SPEC_PINS.rfc9068`, `rfc7591`, `rfc7636`, `rfc8628` and `rfc9700`.
- A block's SQL and TypeScript halves now read one config. `better-supabase gen` writes `blocks.generated.ts` next to `generated.ts`: each installed block's schema, API schema and the options both halves share (`attachments.requireScan`, `bulk.limit`, the realtime topics of notifications, announcements, inbox, reactions and ai-chat, `time-tracking.timeZone`, `waitlist.codeField`, `webhooks-out.allowHttp`), with the module defaults filled in. `defineBlocks(blockConfig, blocks, ...appEvents)` takes it as its first argument and passes each block its schema (the API schema when there is one) and those options, so the app no longer repeats them. `createBlocks` takes it as `config`, and its shared `schema` option is removed. `generatedOptions(entry)` gives a creator you call yourself the same values, and `fillTopic(template, values)` fills in a topic template such as `notifications:{userId}`.

  `blocks.<name>.options` is typed from each block manifest's options schema: an unknown block, an unknown option or a value of the wrong kind is a type error, and the SQL renderer refuses it too. Manifests describe their options with `blockOptions({ key: option.string(), ... })` in place of `optionKeys([...])`, which is removed, and `schemas/config-v1.json` is generated from the same schemas. `BlocksConfig` is the typed input; the resolved config and the SQL renderer use `AnyBlocksConfig`. Preset wiring (`init --preset`) and the `blocks` registry item build on `defineBlocks(blockConfig, ...)`, and `add` edits a `defineBlocks` call as well as a `createBlocks` one.
- Every action a module checks is an operation named `<module>.<action>`, and `better_supabase.operation_permission(op)` returns its permission key in SQL, after `blocks.<module>.permissions` renames and with the provider's `operations` over them. MCP tools take an optional `operation`, agents take `toolOperations`, and workflow-builder step library entries take `operation`, each naming the entry to check in place of `mcp.tool.<name>`, `ai.tool.<name>` or `workflow.run`.
- `@openfeature/server-sdk`, `@supabase-labs/middleware-openfeature`, `@vercel/connect`, `@ai-sdk/anthropic`, `@powersync/common` and `flags` are now optional peer dependencies. better-supabase still imports none of them, so nothing new is installed, but your package manager now warns when one of them is outside the major versions the conformance tests cover. The ranges are on the [Peers](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/getting-started/peers) page.
- `otel()` records a `BATCH` span for the round trip when an executor runs the reads of one `db.$many` call together, as the direct Postgres executor does. The span carries `db.operation.batch.size` and is marked failed when any read fails. Plugins that wrap `executor.batch` now see these batches too.
- The `parties` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/parties` keep the companies and people an organization works with: lifecycle stages, owners, who works where with a role and one primary contact, dedupe by domain and email, `mergeCompanies` and `mergePeople`, and keyset-paged `listCompanies` and `listPeople`. `partiesSubjects()` registers both tables in `config.subjects` so comments, tags, files and pipelines attach to them, and the owner watches the record when notifications is installed.
- With the inbox module installed, the parties module links inbox contacts to people. `personForInboxContact(contactId)` returns the linked person, links the person with the contact's email when there is none, and with `create: true` saves an unknown contact as a new lead. `linkInboxContact` and `unlinkInboxContact` change the link by hand.
- The `pipelines` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/pipelines` put any record in `config.subjects` on pipelines an organization edits. Bind a type in `blocks.pipelines.options.subjects` with its categories, each mapped to `open`, `success` or `failure`, and the module keeps several pipelines per type (or one per scope, such as a job), checks every stage change, stamps the time a record entered its stage, writes a stage history and records `pipeline.stage_changed` with the record as its subject. A binding can set a status column from the stage category through the state machine, and the owner watches the record when notifications is installed. `createPipelines` manages pipelines, stages and transitions, and reads the board (counts and per-currency totals weighted by probability), stats (entries, time in stage, win rate) and health (days in stage, rotting).
- Lists and resources page by cursor by default. `defineListQuery` and the default list of `defineResource` and `defineApi` read the same number of rows however deep the page is, and rows inserted between requests don't shift it. Set `pagination: "offset"` to keep page numbers and totals; the 0.9 codemod adds it to calls that set no pagination in files that read page numbers, and lists the other calls for review. A table or view without a primary key stays on offset.

  | 0.8 (offset default)                       | 0.9 (cursor default)                  |
  | ------------------------------------------ | ------------------------------------- |
  | `?page=3&size=50`                          | `?after=<nextCursor>&size=50`         |
  | `page.page.number`, `.total`, `.pages`     | `page.hasMore`, `page.nextCursor`     |
  | `ListPage<R, F, C>` is an `OffsetPage`     | `ListPage<R, F, C>` is a `CursorPage` |
  | `list.args(query)` returns `page`, `count` | `list.args(query)` returns `after`    |
  | OpenAPI and MCP list `page`                | OpenAPI and MCP list `after`          |

  The World's `world.analytics` lists return `(created_at, id)` keyset cursors instead of offsets. Pass back the cursor the previous page returned; an offset number from 0.8 fails with "not an analytics cursor".

  Time tracking rates are `Money` (ADR 0037). An entry's `rate` is an hourly amount in minor units with its currency, and the timesheet totals return `amounts`, one `Money` per currency, instead of `amount`. The client returns `CURRENCY_INVALID` before any database call for an unknown code, and `time_entry.*` events carry `rate` as text with `rateCurrency`.

  | 0.8                              | 0.9                                                 |
  | -------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------- |
  | `time.start(org, { rate: 120 })` | `time.start(org, { rate: money(12_000n, "EUR") })`  |
  | `entry.rate` is `120`            | `entry.rate` is `money(12_000n, "EUR")`             |
  | `sheet.totals.amount` is `480`   | `sheet.totals.amounts` is `[money(48_000n, "EUR")]` |

  The module upgrade turns `rate` into a `bigint` with `round(rate * 100)`, adds `rate_currency`, and drops the `numeric` signatures of `start_timer` and `log_time`. Set `rate_currency` on existing rows after the upgrade; the 0.8 to 0.9 guide has the statements.

  Tagging a record needs `tags.apply` or `tags.manage`. In 0.8 any signed-in user could tag or untag a record. The `role_permissions` defaults give `tags.apply` to members; apps with their own roles grant it to the roles that tag.

  | 0.8                                          | 0.9                                                        |
  | -------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------- |
  | `tag()` and `untag()` check the subject only | they also need `tags.apply` or `tags.manage` on the tenant |

  Audit, webhooks and notifications write a batch in one call. `audit.recordMany(events)`, `webhooks.publishMany(events)` and `notifications.sendMany(items)` take up to 1000 items each, run the single-item checks per item, and return one `Result` per item in order, so a bad item never drops the others. The audit, webhook and notification sinks use them, one call per batch. `auditSink` takes the batch function:

  | 0.8                                      | 0.9                                                              |
  | ---------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | `auditSink(record, options)`             | `auditSink(recordMany, options)`                                 |
  | `record: (event) => AsyncResult<string>` | `recordMany: (events) => AsyncResult<readonly Result<string>[]>` |

  The outbox, usage, notifications and webhooks modules take `partitioned: true`, as the audit module does. It partitions a new events, `usage_history`, `notification_delivery_events` or deliveries table by month on its time column, with a primary key of `(id, created_at)`, and retention drops whole months. It applies to new tables only; the default stays unpartitioned (ADR 0038).

  Direct Postgres (`better-supabase/postgres`) honors `count: "planned"` and `count: "estimated"` the way PostgREST does: `planned` reads the row estimate from `explain`, and `estimated` counts exactly up to `estimatedCountThreshold` (default 1000) and uses the estimate above it. Counts are cast to `bigint`.

  | 0.8                                          | 0.9                                                            |
  | -------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | `planned` and `estimated` ran an exact count | `planned` reads `explain`; `estimated` switches at 1000 rows   |
  | `idleInTransactionTimeout` unset             | defaults to 15000 ms; `0` turns it off                         |
  | an idle connection error crashed the process | it arrives as an `error` event, or the logger without handlers |

  `createPostgres` takes `events` and `logger` for those errors. The World's pool has limits now: `maxConnections` (10), `connectionTimeout` (30 s), `idleTimeout` (10 s), `statementTimeout` (30 s) and `idleInTransactionTimeout` (15 s), also read from `WORKFLOW_POSTGRES_MAX_CONNECTIONS`, `WORKFLOW_POSTGRES_CONNECTION_TIMEOUT_MS`, `WORKFLOW_POSTGRES_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `WORKFLOW_POSTGRES_STATEMENT_TIMEOUT_MS` and `WORKFLOW_POSTGRES_IDLE_IN_TRANSACTION_TIMEOUT_MS`.

  Realtime trigger functions include the schema, so tables with the same name in two schemas no longer replace each other's function. The trigger keeps its name. Drop the old functions in the migration; grants on them don't carry over.

  | 0.8                              | 0.9                                       |
  | -------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- |
  | `bs_broadcast_<topic>_<table>()` | `bs_broadcast_<topic>_<schema>_<table>()` |

  The key payload of `broadcast_topic()` reads only the primary key and the placeholder columns and looks the key up once per statement.

  Doctor has five new rules:

  | Code  | Severity | Finds                                                                                           |
  | ----- | -------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
  | BS117 | warning  | a policy without a `to` role, so it applies to `public`                                         |
  | BS224 | warning  | `anon` or `authenticated` without `statement_timeout` or `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`  |
  | BS225 | info     | `pg_stat_statements` not installed                                                              |
  | BS226 | warning  | postgres.js without `prepare: false`, or Prisma without `pgbouncer=true`, next to a `:6543` URL |
  | BS227 | info     | `pagination: "offset"` in a list or resource config, or `count: "exact"` on a list              |

  BS216 accepts a `where col is not null` partial index as the foreign key's index. BS335 now also
  checks the outbox, usage history, webhook deliveries and notification delivery events tables, not
  only the audit log.

  `calendar_attendees.role` is checked against the RFC 5545 roles, `notification_events.priority` against the configured priorities, and `reminders.offset_minutes` is limited to a year either way. Each check is added `not valid` and then validated, so existing bad rows leave it unvalidated with a warning instead of failing the migration. `comments.list` takes `cursorId` with `cursor`, so comments with the same timestamp no longer repeat or skip between pages. The audit list ignores `offset` when it gets a cursor.

  Line totals on quotes, invoices and deals are recounted once per parent per statement instead of once per line.
