Trigger functions and `broadcast_changes()` are no longer executable by `anon` or `authenticated`, and the storefront helpers are revoked from `public` and granted to the roles that call them. `bs_reserved_slugs_read` names its roles, and `scope.platform()` checks the platform permission once per statement. The reminders, calendar, bookings, imports, bulk, share-links, tags, memory and workflow-builder modules index the columns their policies and lookups filter on. Search writes embeddings in one statement, the pgmq backend archives a batch in one call, and `metrics_rollup` locks per tenant.
- `createPostgres()` session options take `statementTimeout`, which overrides the role's timeout for `asUser`, `executorFor` and `transaction`. `transaction()` without claims now applies `settings`, `readOnly` and `statementTimeout` too, and an `admin` query with no timeout to set runs without `begin` and `commit`.
- **Breaking:** the CLI uses `@supabase/postgrest-typegen` 0.6.0, and the optional peer now accepts `>=0.6.0 <0.7` instead of `>=0.4.0 <0.5`. Upgrade it with `pnpm add -D @supabase/postgrest-typegen@0.6.0`. `database.types.ts` again matches `supabase gen types` from Supabase CLI 2.121, which writes the same `ComputedFields` keys. View columns are typed with the nullability postgrest-typegen 0.6 reads from the view definition, so run `better-supabase gen` to regenerate.

  The `GeneratorMetadata` type gains the optional `rpcFunctions` collection that postgrest-typegen 0.6 derives from `functions`, and the `RpcFunction` types it uses are exported next to it. Snapshots and `gen --metadata` leave it out, and the generator resolves it again from the ordered, stable functions of the generated schemas only.
- `watch` and `useWatch` from `better-supabase/powersync` take `refresh: { everyMs, onReconnect }`, so a read that runs on the executor's server `fallback` reruns without waiting for a local change: on an interval (skipped while PowerSync reports it is offline) and when PowerSync connects again. `PowerSyncDatabaseLike` gains the optional `currentStatus` and `registerListener` members that `refresh` reads.
- The CLI starts and changes projects from presets. `init --preset <name|code|url>` writes the config with `preset`, the module files of every block it installs and a `lib/blocks.ts` that wires their clients, and prints the registry glue for the framework. `apply --preset` sets `preset` in an existing config (or, with `--only blocks`, lists its blocks; with `--only conventions`, takes the conventions alone), shows the diff and writes the modules. `preset decode` and `preset info` print what a code holds and how the config differs from its preset, and `blocks list`, `blocks view` and `blocks search` read the block catalog.

  `add <block...>` now adds blocks: it lists them in `blocks`, writes their modules and what they require, wires their clients into `lib/blocks.ts` and names their registry glue. The framework glue moves to `add --framework <integration,...>`; `add` without arguments still sets up the integrations the config lists. In a terminal, `init` first asks for a preset. `better-supabase/preset` adds `presetApplyCommand`, `presetGlue` and `glueCommand`, and `presetInstallCommand` now returns `init --preset <code>`.
- `better-supabase/preset` describes an app as a preset: a block selection plus its conventions (framework, platforms, casing, id type, scope, tenant name, access model, API schema and integrations). `encodePreset` and `decodePreset` turn one into a short code, and `resolvePreset` returns every block to install with the reason it is there, the soft integrations between them and the conflicts. It reads a generated catalog of the blocks, so it runs in the browser. The named presets are `minimal`, `saas`, `crm`, `projects`, `commerce`, `scheduling`, `ai` and `everything`.

  `defineConfig({ preset, blocks })` takes a named preset, a code or a `/create?preset=` URL and expands it into `blocks` and its conventions first; the keys you set win. The docs Recipes section is now Presets, at `/docs/presets`.
- The `promotions` module calls `before_promotion_activate(promotion uuid)` when you define it, after every write that leaves a promotion active: `set_promotion_active(id, true)` and each `save_promotion` of an active promotion. Raising refuses the change, which holds a promotion for approval. The commerce recipe uses it to hold discounts above 30 percent, and now also indexes products for search and saves order grid views.
- The `quotes` and `invoices` SQL modules and their blocks add the sales documents after a deal. A quote has lines and totals the database keeps and a revision chain. It is sent as a link the customer opens without an account and answers with accept, reject or changes requested, and it expires after its valid date. `quotes.fromDeal` makes one draft per deal. An invoice gets a gapless number when it is issued and collects payments (a payment reference is recorded once, and a refund larger than the amount paid fails with `INVOICE_REFUND_TOO_LARGE`). Credit notes reduce its balance, and it can be voided, written off and aged per currency. `invoices.fromQuote` makes deposit invoices and one final invoice that subtracts them. With the relations module installed, both link to their origin with `created_from`.
- Add fractional rank keys. `better-supabase/blocks/rank` exports `rankBetween`, `rankKeys`, `isRankKey` and `createRank`, and the new `rank` SQL module adds `rank_between`, `move()` and a `rank.rebalance` job for subjects with a `rank` entry in `config.subjects`. `move()` runs as the caller and can change the row's group, so a state-machine trigger on that column checks the change.

  Tags and custom fields now order definitions by a `rank` key instead of an integer `position`. Both modules move to version 2: the upgrade backfills keys in the old order and drops `position`. `tags.reorder` and `customFields.reorderDefinition` take the neighbours' keys. Adopt configs that mapped `position` map `rank` instead. An adopted table without a rank column maps it to `null` (`columns: { tags: { rank: null } }`, and `definitions` and `options` for custom fields); `sql sync` names the column until you do.

  A state-machine config without `initial` now names `initial` in its error instead of `column`.
- Add `rateLimit()` middleware to the Hono and oRPC adapters, built on `createRateLimit`. Responses carry the
  `RateLimit` and `RateLimit-Policy` fields of draft-ietf-httpapi-ratelimit-headers-11, and a refused request gets a
  429 with `Retry-After`. `better-supabase/server` exports `rateLimitHeader()` and `rateLimitPolicyHeader()`.
- A read-only support or impersonation session can no longer write. Every security definer function granted to `authenticated` or `anon` that writes checks `better_supabase.actor_may_write()`, or `authorization.functions.actorMayWrite` when a provider sets it. The same check is added to generated write policies. Writes that succeeded during those sessions in 0.8.0 now fail with `ACTOR_READ_ONLY` (`42501`). `read_only: false`, an agent actor and a signed-in user without `act` still write. Reads are unchanged. `start_support_session`, `end_support_session` and `expire_support_sessions` stay callable so the session can be left.
- Topic triggers stop sending whole rows. This changes behaviour: the default message is now `{ operation, schema, table, id, values }`, the primary key and the topic values, so subscribers refetch the row through the Data API and their column grants apply. Before, every subscriber of the topic received every column.

  - `topic.trigger(table, options)` is the one trigger API. Pass `betterSupabase` for typed table keys and parent-row lookups, `payload` for a custom object or `"row"` to keep whole rows on purpose, and `topics` to send the same change to more topics.
  - `triggerSql(betterSupabase, table, options)` is deprecated and calls `trigger(table, { ...options, betterSupabase })`. Its default payload changes too.
  - `rowKeys` reads the new message on the client. `rowChange` reads the `payload: "row"` shape.
  - `realtime.triggers` entries take `payload: "keys" | "row"`, and the config loader now accepts `realtime.triggers` at all.
  - `better_supabase.broadcast_topic()` sends the key payload, takes a fourth `'row'` argument, accepts `{name=column}` placeholders, and refuses to run as a `before` trigger. A topic value with characters topics don't allow raises a warning and skips that send instead of failing the write; generated trigger functions do the same per topic.
  - Doctor BS337 warns about trigger functions that broadcast whole rows without the `payload: "row"` marker, such as 0.8 `triggerSql` functions. Snapshots record trigger arguments, and the source and comment of broadcasting trigger functions.

  Run `better-supabase sql sync` and create a migration to update `broadcast_topic()`. See [Realtime triggers](https://bettersupabase.com/docs/migration/0.8-to-0.9#realtime-triggers).
- Glue for your app now installs with the shadcn CLI from the `@better-supabase` registry at `https://bettersupabase.com/r/{name}.json`: block wiring (`blocks`, `postgres`, `outbox`, `onboarding-checklist`), Next.js server actions for notifications, the `use-current-organization` and `push-native` hooks, route handlers for the outbox relay (Next.js and Hono), Stripe webhooks and the jobs drain, the Expo config, and the `saas-next` and `saas-expo` bundles. The registry holds no UI components, config or SQL.

  The UI comes from recipes instead: the notification bell, notification list, command search and onboarding checklist show the shadcn components to add and the web and React Native code side by side, with the same hook call. They are on bettersupabase.com/recipes, in the docs at `/docs/registry/ui-recipes` and in the `better-supabase` skill (`references/ui-recipes.md`). `SPEC_PINS.shadcnRegistry` pins the shadcn registry schema the items follow.
- The `relations` module declares a fourth default kind, `created_from` (inverse `converted_to`, one origin per record, no cycles), for records that turn into other records. `converted_into(tenant, origin_type, origin_id, into_type)` and the client's `convertedInto` return the record an origin was already converted into, so a conversion runs once, and `lineage(organizationId, subject)` returns a record's origins and conversions. `sql sync` rewrites the relations module.
- Add the `relations` block (`better-supabase/blocks/relations`). It links two subjects with a declared kind (`blocks`, `parent` and `relates` by default), stores inverse names from one end, refuses cycles for acyclic kinds under an advisory lock, replaces the target of one-target kinds, and reads ancestors, descendants and rollups. `link` and `unlink` return an undo token, and `toGanttTasks` and `toTreeItems` shape links for a Gantt chart or a tree.
- Retention policies and legal holds in `data-lifecycle`: `createRetention` sets how long each kind of record is kept (`audit`, `outbox`, `webhook_deliveries`, `webhooks`, `inbound_events`, `job_archive`, `usage`, `notifications`, `workflow_runs`), for every tenant, a plan's tenants or one tenant, and places and releases legal holds that every module purge and `purge_organization` (`ORGANIZATION_LEGAL_HOLD`) respect, including chats and trash. Holds emit `legal_hold.placed` and `legal_hold.released`. Without `data-lifecycle`, each purge keeps its `older_than`.

  The audit module (version 8) gains `options.chain`, which hash-chains each tenant's entries, and `createAuditChain` in `better-supabase/blocks/audit` verifies the chain and records signed checkpoints of its heads with any `signJwt` signer. Without a `to`, verification runs up to the latest checkpoint, so deleting the newest entries reports a gap. `options.partitioned` creates a new managed log partitioned by month, `better_supabase.maintain_partitions()` adds the months ahead, and `purge_audit_log` drops expired months when no hold or `audit_retention` hook applies. `better-supabase doctor` reports a log that asks for partitions but isn't partitioned (BS335).
- New block `better-supabase/blocks/reviews` (`better-supabase add reviews`): ratings and reviews of catalog products and other subjects. Any signed-in user can review an active product on a public storefront, a product in a catalog they read, or a registered subject in an organization they belong to, once per subject; a new review replaces their earlier one. `verified` is true when the `orders` module shows a paid order for the product, and `blocks.reviews.options.requirePurchase` makes that a requirement. Reviews wait for a moderator under `moderation: "pre"` (the default) or publish at once under `"post"`. `createReviews().summary()` returns each product's count, average and histogram, which anonymous visitors can read for a public storefront. The `access` module adds `reviews.manage` (admins); re-run `better-supabase sql sync` to pick up the new default permissions.
- Under the provider access model, `member_organization_ids()`, `has_organization_role()` and `organization_member_role()` read membership from the provider's `memberIds` and `memberIdsFor` templates, so a user the provider no longer lists stops counting as a member. Doctor rule BS334 (info) reports app policies and functions that still call them and points at `tenant_ids_with` or `member_can`.
- `db.$rpc` now casts result columns that have a codec to `text`, as repository reads do, over both PostgREST and the Postgres executor. An `int8` above 2^53 in a `returns table` or `returns setof` result now reaches its `bigint` codec intact, and a `numeric` keeps its digits. `{ raw: true }` calls send no column list. Executors receive the list as `RpcContext.select`; an executor that ignores it returns the full row, as before.
- New block `better-supabase/blocks/rules` (`better-supabase add rules`): saved, versioned condition trees over the fields you register per subject type. `defineRuleFields()` declares each field's type, column type and operators, and `createRulesAdmin().syncFields()` stores them for the database. A rule has a trigger, a `FilterQuery` condition, ordered actions, a rank and `stopProcessing`. `matching()` evaluates every enabled rule for a subject in Postgres (`better_supabase.rule_matches`), and `test()` evaluates one rule locally with `evaluateFilterQuery`; both give the same answer for every operator. `createRuleDispatcher()` runs the matched actions through your handlers, and `approvalsAction()` opens an approval request from a rule. Every save keeps a version, every evaluation can be logged, and `rule.saved`, `rule.enabled`, `rule.disabled`, `rule.removed` and `rule.matched` go to the outbox. The `access` module adds `rules.read` (members), `rules.edit`, `rules.publish` and `rules.admin` (admins); re-run `better-supabase sql sync` to pick up the new default permissions.
- The rules block has an editor controller. `useRuleBuilder` from `better-supabase/blocks/rules/react` (and `/vue`, `/svelte`, `/solid`) loads a subject type's fields and a rule, edits its condition tree, reports the problems `checkRuleConditions` finds and whether anything changed, and saves the rule with its version. Nodes named in `locked` can't be changed, moved or removed. `createRuleBuilderController` is the same state without a framework, and the tree helpers it uses (`findNode`, `insertNode`, `moveNode`, `removeNode`, `setRuleField`, `setRuleOperator` and others) are exported from `better-supabase/blocks/rules`.
- The Rule builder UI recipe renders a rule editor with shadcn components: field, operator and value rows, nested And and Or groups, drag handles and a locked first condition. The `use-rule-editor` registry item installs `useRuleEditor`, which reads the organization from the session and the rules schema from `blocks.generated.ts`, and the preset catalog lists it.
- Add eight blocks, each with managed, adopt and custom modes: `number-sequences` (gapless numbers assigned in the document's transaction), `teams`, `tags`, `state-machine`, `custom-fields`, `share-links`, `reminders` (RFC 5545 expansion) and `imports` (RFC 4180 CSV). Subject link and history tables can be adopted per subject.
- `diffAppTables` and `diffDesigns` from `better-supabase/preset` list what changes between two sets of app tables. Added tables and optional columns are safe. A dropped or renamed table or column, a type change (of a column or of the table's id) and a column made required without a default are breaking, and each comes with its expand-and-contract steps.

  `apply --preset` with a design now lists the breaking changes to tables the config already has, writes nothing and exits 1. Pass `--allow-breaking` to write them anyway; `--dry-run` shows them without it.

  Doctor gains BS228 (warning): a column of an app table whose type or nullability in the database differs from `appTables`.

  Create shows the changes since the page loaded its link on a Changes tab in Get code.

  Migration: a script that runs `apply --preset` with a design that retypes, drops or renames app table columns now needs `--allow-breaking`.
- `blocks.tenant.options.sameTenant` takes `{ auto: true, entries? }`. `sql sync` then derives an entry for every single-column foreign key between two tables with the tenant column, from `references`, `foreign key` constraints and `alter table ... add constraint` in the schema files. A listed entry for the same column wins, and a derived entry renders the same trigger as the listed one. The list form is unchanged. Doctor's new BS340 lists tenant foreign keys without a `bs_same_tenant_<column>` trigger. `better-supabase/sql` exports `sameTenantLayout`, `sameTenantColumn` and `sameTenantTrigger`.
- Saved views keep the whole view state and per-user preferences. `useSavedViews` takes `getState` and `onApplyState`, so `saveCurrent` stores the columns, grouping, density and layout with the URL's query, `apply` restores them, and `active` and `isDirty` compare them; the `save` callback now also receives `state`, the value `savedViews.save()` takes. The `saved-views` SQL module adds a `saved_view_preferences` table and `set_view_preference`, so a member can make a shared view their own default, hide it or pin it without the right to change it, and `list_views` returns the caller's `preference` with each view. `defaultView` opens the caller's preferred view first and skips hidden views. `duplicate_view` and `savedViews.duplicate()` copy a view the caller sees into a new view, with the same checks as saving one. The client adds `setPreference()` and `duplicate()`, and the hook adds `duplicate` and `setPreference`. Run `better-supabase sql sync` to install module version 2.
- Add the `saved-views` block. The SQL module stores named list views per user, team or organization, with one default per slot and rank order; sharing a view with a team or the organization needs `views.share`, and team views use the `teams` module. `better-supabase/blocks/saved-views` exports `createSavedViews`, which checks a view's query against its `defineListQuery` definition before saving, plus `savedViewStateSchema`, `defaultView` and helpers that convert between nuqs URL state and a stored query. `better-supabase/blocks/saved-views/react` exports `useSavedViews`, which opens the default view and saves the URL's query. The default roles gain `views.read` for members and `views.*` for admins.
- `better-supabase scaffold screens <table>` writes Next.js pages for an app table: a list, a create form with an input per column (from its type, `format`, `options`, `min` and `max`, and a select of rows for a reference to another app table) and a detail page. The pages read and write through the generated repository with `bs`, so RLS checks every request, and each file is written once and then kept as yours. `--base-path` sets the route, `/<table>` by default. The command refuses a table outside `appTables`, a table with `scope: "none"` and a required column the form has no input for.

  The UI recipes gain an App tables group: `record-grid`, `record-form`, `record-page`, `record-board`, `record-calendar`, `record-timeline` and `record-gantt`, built on projects and tasks in the Next.js example. Create lists the `scaffold screens` command for each app table in Get code, and its AI instructions link the record recipes once the design has an app table.

  Migration: none.
- `scimHandler` takes a `store`: a versioned `ScimStore` with `list`, `get`, `save` and `delete` for users and groups, which may apply the filter and paging itself. `sqlScimStore(transport)` is the default, and `transport` still works. The store gets the raw resource, extension schemas included. `blocks.sso.options.syncLog` adds a `scim_sync_log` table; pass `sqlScimSyncLog` as the handler's `log` to record each write.
- Blocks run per tenant, per user or in one shared space. `sql.scope` sets the scope of every block and `blocks.<name>.scope` the scope of one. Outside `tenant` scope a block needs neither the `tenant` module nor the access contract, its scope column is nullable (the user's id by default in `user` scope), and its functions set the scope id themselves. `tags` and `comments` run in every scope, the blocks without tenant data (such as `notifications`) run anywhere, and blocks that need tenants declare `scopes: ["tenant"]`, so `sql add` and `resolvePreset` refuse them in another scope.

  `sql.naming.tenant` renames the tenant concept in the tables, columns and functions the blocks write: `workspace` gives `workspaces`, `workspace_id`, `create_workspace` and `member_workspace_ids`. `blocks.<name>.functions` renames single functions. A tenant name that would collide with a block's own names is refused. `gen` writes the names into each block's `naming` in `blocks.generated.ts`, and `defineBlocks` wraps the block's transport with the new `blockNames()` middleware, so the block clients call the renamed functions. The preset model's `tenantName` field takes `workspace` and `team`, and presets write `sql.scope` and `sql.naming`.

  The SQL modules check access only through `ctx.scope` (`can`, `ids`, `check`, `canUser`, `platform`, `members`), and `canIn` and `tenantIn` are removed from the module helpers. `tags.update_tag` no longer fails with an ambiguous `name` column.
- Adds the `search` block (`better-supabase/blocks/search`). Triggers generated from each `search` entry in `config.subjects` keep a `search_documents` index, and `search(q)` runs as the caller with websearch, prefix and trigram matching, filtered by `can_subject`, and ranked hybrid when the index keeps embeddings. Recents and favorites come from the share-links user state, trashed rows drop out, `searchSink` handles trash events, and `useCommandSearch` (`better-supabase/blocks/search/react`) drives a Cmd+K dialog.
- Add the `service-principals` block (`better-supabase/blocks/service-principals` and the `service-principals` SQL module): organization members that are not people, each an `auth.users` row that cannot sign in and holds its role through a membership. `create`, `setRole`, `disable`, `defaultFor` and `list` manage them; a member who asks for the change needs `service_principals.manage` and may only assign a role they could assign. A workflow-builder version the organization owns runs as its default principal, capped to the permissions the version stored, and a service webhook endpoint gets an event only when the default principal may read it.
- Users can now manage their own sessions and reminders. The `sessions` module adds `list_my_sessions()` and `end_my_session(id)`, which list the caller's unexpired sessions (with the current one marked) and end one of them. The `reminders` module adds `list_my_reminders` and `delete_reminder`, and the reminders client adds `list(organizationId, subjectType, subjectId)` and `remove(id)`. Run `better-supabase sql sync` and generate a migration to install the new functions.
- `create_share_link` takes a `version_id` that pins the link to a version of its subject and must belong to it, and `shareLinks.create(..., { versionId })` passes it. `shareLinks.resolve(token)` is typed and returns `versionId`. The `versions` module adds `get_shared_version`, granted to anon, and the client gains `versions.getShared(token)`, which reads a pinned version through its link.
- Share links expire. `shareLinks.create(..., { expiresAt })` takes a `Temporal.Instant` (`SHARE_EXPIRY_INVALID` when it is not in the future), and `resolve` returns the link's `organizationId` and `expiresAt`. Every module that reads through a link (calendar feeds, booking pages, public forms, drive links, shared AI chats) now checks expiry the same way; shared AI chats used to ignore it. `create_share_link` takes `expires_at` as a sixth argument and drops the five-argument signature.

  `revoke_share_link` lets only the link's creator or a member with `share_links.create` revoke a link, and returns `SHARE_NOT_FOUND` to anyone else. `list_share_links` returns the caller's own links and those in organizations where they hold `share_links.read`. `record_view` refuses a caller who is not a member of the organization (`SHARE_FORBIDDEN`), and no longer fails with an ambiguous `subject_type` column.

  `authorization.functions.isApprover` takes an optional `{requester}` placeholder: the uuid of the user who asked (the approval's `requested_by`, the support admin, the chat's owner), so a provider that resolves out-of-office covers can refuse the requester's own delegator. Under the `provider` model, `delegate_approval` checks the delegate's membership through the provider's `memberIdsFor`.
- New `shared-signals` block (`better-supabase sql add shared-signals`, `better-supabase/blocks/shared-signals`): an OpenID Shared Signals Framework 1.0 transmitter and receiver, with every CAEP 1.0 and RISC 1.0 event type. `createSsfTransmitter` serves the well-known metadata, stream management, status, verification and RFC 8936 poll routes, and its `authorize` option names the receiver behind each request. `emit` mints one Security Event Token (RFC 8417) per stream that delivers the event, signed by any `signJwt` signer such as a `signing-keys` signer. With `webhooks-out` installed, a push stream (RFC 8935) gets an endpoint with the new `set` format, so SETs go through the outbox with its retries and dead letters. Poll streams queue SETs until they are acknowledged, and a paused stream holds its SETs until it is enabled again. `createSsfReceiver` verifies SETs against the transmitter's JWKS, drops replays through the service-only `ssf_received` table, runs a handler per event type, and serves the push endpoint or polls. Stream changes are recorded as `ssf_stream.*` events. The pins are `SPEC_PINS.rfc8417`, `rfc8935`, `rfc8936`, `ssf`, `caep` and `risc`.
- Add the `signatures` SQL module and `createSignatures` in `better-supabase/blocks/forms`: simple electronic signatures on a frozen version of a record, signed by click, typed name or drawn image, in parallel or in order. Each signature stores a hash over the version's content, so `get` reports whether the signed content or a signature changed and whether the record has a newer version. With the approvals module, a signer's signature can approve the step of a linked approval request.
- New `signing-keys` block (`better-supabase sql add signing-keys`, `better-supabase/blocks/signing-keys`): Ed25519 and ES256 keys per owner, with the private key in Supabase Vault and the public JWK in a service-only `signing_keys` table. `create` and `rotate` keep one active key per owner and retire the previous one. A retired key stays in `jwks(owner)` until the last `exp` that `signJwt` noted plus `jwksGrace` (`5 minutes`). `jwksResponse` serves the set as `application/jwk-set+json`, and `purge()` deletes keys no token can still need. `signer(owner)` signs raw bytes or compact JWTs in TypeScript with WebCrypto, and `verifyCompact` checks them against a JWK Set. Ed25519 keys sign as `EdDSA` by default, or as `Ed25519` (RFC 9864, pinned as `SPEC_PINS.rfc9864`).

  Outgoing webhooks can sign with Ed25519 (Standard Webhooks `v1a`). `webhooks-out` 4 adds `signing` (`v1` or `v1a`), `format` and `credential_ref` columns to `webhook_endpoints`. A `v1a` endpoint is signed by the new `asymmetricSigner` option, which takes `standardWebhooksAsymmetric({ signer })`. It needs no secret and is dead-lettered when no signer is set. `verifyWebhook` accepts a `whpk_` public key, which `webhookPublicKey(jwk)` builds, in place of a `whsec_` secret.
- The `sla` and `targets` SQL modules and their blocks add service levels and goals. A service-level policy per record type and priority sets first response, next response and resolution minutes, optionally in business hours with a time zone. Binding a record type's status and priority columns starts, pauses, resumes and resolves its clocks, `record(subject, "responded")` meets a response clock, and `check()` records `sla.warned` and `sla.breached` and notifies the record's watchers. A target is a goal per period for a member, a team or the organization. Its progress is computed for won deal amounts and counts, completed work items and paid invoices, or read from a value the app keeps.
- `functionRoute(name, fn, options?)` from `better-supabase/spec` documents a `defineFunction` Edge Function as `POST /functions/v1/<name>`, with its input as the request body and its output as the 200 response. `defineFunction` now keeps its `input`, `output` and a new optional `summary` on the handler it returns, and `TypedFunction` requires them, so `functionRoute` accepts only `defineFunction` handlers.
- `better-supabase sql add` lists the new modules in `blocks` of the config file, as `name: {}` in an object or a name in a list, and edits `better-supabase.config.json` too. When `blocks` isn't a literal it can follow, it leaves the file alone and prints the entries to add. `--dry-run` says what it would add.
- `sql.api` and `sql.schema` set the Data API schema and the module schema for every module that leaves its own unset, so a config no longer repeats `api: "api"` per module. A module's `api: false` keeps it out of the API schema when `sql.api` is set.
- The SQL modules support three workflows through the new `sql.workflow` config key: `pg-delta` (Declarative Schemas 2.0, the default), `migra` (declarative schemas 1.0 with `supabase db diff`) and `migrations`. When the key is unset, the CLI reads `supabase/config.toml` and keeps a project that already uses migra on migra. Under `migrations`, `sql add`, `sql sync` and `sql upgrade` write the modules into one `<stamp>_better_supabase_modules.sql` migration instead of `supabase/schemas`, `gen` writes the read sets into a migration, and doctor compares against the newest module migration. `better-supabase init --workflow <name>` picks the workflow, and `sql add` and `sql sync` turn pg-delta on in `config.toml` under the pg-delta workflow. Doctor BS316 stays silent when the config chooses `migra` or `migrations`, and the BS404 grant hint names a new migration under `migrations`.
- A `state-machine` machine can keep a lifecycle row per record with `lifecycle: true`: `pause(subjectType, id, { until, reason })`, `resume()` and `setFlag(subjectType, id, flag, enabled)` on `createStateMachine()`, a pinned `version` and a list of allowed `flags`. While a pause holds, the trigger refuses transitions with `STATE_PAUSED`; a pause with an end clears on the next transition after it. The module records `status.paused`, `status.resumed` and `status.flagged` next to `status.changed`.

  The block reads as the caller: `state()` returns the status with its `step` and `progress` through the machine's new `order`, and the lifecycle; `history()` pages status changes newest first; `stages()` returns the stages timeline. `status_history`, `status_stages` and the new `subject_lifecycles` table have select policies that show a row when the caller can see its record. A machine without `table` takes its table, id type and tenant from `config.subjects`, and `idType` compares ids in their own type.
- Breaking: `better_supabase.transition()` in the `state-machine` module now runs as the caller instead of as a security definer function. Before, any signed-in user could change the status of any record of a machine, in any tenant. Now the subject table's row level security decides: a caller who can't update the record gets `STATE_SUBJECT_NOT_FOUND` (`P0002`, a `not_found` error from `createStateMachine().transition()`) instead of `true`. Give members an update policy on each machine's table, or call `transition()` with the service role where a server action moved records on their behalf. Approval-gated transitions and `bulk` transitions keep working; a bulk row the caller can't update now fails. The stages timeline only clears and numbers stages within the record's own tenant and machine. Run `better-supabase sql sync` to install module version 2.
- New `better-supabase/stripe` entry with `stripeFromKey({ secretKey, apiVersion })`. It returns a `stripe` source for `createBilling`, `reportUsageToStripe`, `stripePayments` and `stripeTax` that creates the client once, on first use, from a literal `import("stripe")`, so Next.js, Vite and worker bundlers resolve the package and file tracing ships it. `stripe` stays an optional peer.

  Bundled apps that passed `stripe: { secretKey }` got an empty module, because the loader's `import(specifier)` was too dynamic for the bundler, and every Stripe call threw that the stripe package was missing even when it was installed. The optional peer loader now carries `webpackIgnore` and `@vite-ignore` comments, so bundlers leave a native import that resolves in Node, Bun, Deno and apps that keep the package external, and the error names the fix: pass `stripe: stripeFromKey({ secretKey })` from `better-supabase/stripe`, or a Stripe client. `{ secretKey }` keeps working as the plain Node shortcut.
- Add `config.subjects`, an app-wide subject registry. Modules that take `subjects` (tags, comments, attachments, reminders and the others) use the registered types when their own option is unset, accept a list of registered names, and merge an object entry onto the registered one. The new `subjects` SQL module writes `subject_title`, `subject_url` and `can_subject`, which run as the caller. `gen` emits `subjectTypes`, `SubjectType` and `SubjectRef`, and doctor reports a registered table or column the database lacks (BS331) and a registered table without RLS (BS332).
- **Breaking:** `@supabase/supabase-js` 2.118.0 is the new minimum, and the `@supabase/postgrest-js` dependency moves to `^2.118.0`. It is the first release that reads the `ComputedFields` key and the no-argument function overloads in the types postgrest-typegen 0.6 writes.
- Repository calls on `ctx.postgres` and `ctx.postgresAdmin` now return `@supabase/server`'s pool errors (`POSTGRES_POOL_BUSY` and `POSTGRES_CONNECT_PAUSED`) as a `network` error with status 503 instead of `unexpected` with 500. `network` errors carry `retryAfter` in seconds when the pool reports how long it is paused, and the problem response sends it as `Retry-After`. A pool failure at the start of a `$many` transaction now returns an error for each operation instead of rejecting.
- Support sessions now deny by default. `supportSessions` refuses every start unless `authorize` or the `support.authorize` authorization hook allows it, and `authorize: "store"` leaves the decision to the store. A start also needs a second admin by default (`policy.approval: "distinct"` and the `support-sessions` module option `approval: "distinct"`): the admin calls `support.request`, a different admin calls `support.approve`, and the SQL module refuses self-approval in `approve_support_request` and in a check constraint on the new `support_requests` table. Set both options to `"none"` to keep the old behaviour.

  `approverOf` and the SQL `isApprover` template decide who may approve, with kind `support-session` for support requests and kind `ai-tool` (subject the tool's name) for `decide_ai_tool_approval`, where `isApprover` always refuses the approval's owner. The ai-chat module still reads `canApprove` when `isApprover` is unset.
- `ctx.storage` writes in a writable support session. `upload`, `update`, `remove`, `copy`, `move` and `purgeCache` first run the row change Storage would make as the target, inside a savepoint that is rolled back, so the policies on `storage.objects` decide with `owner_id` set to the target. A refused dry run resolves to Storage's `403` error (`AccessDenied`) with the policy's message. Then the service client writes, the admin Postgres connection makes the target the owner of the stored object, and its `user_metadata` records `supportSessionId` and `supportAdminId`. `remove` deletes only the paths the target may delete. Signed uploads still resolve to `SUPPORT_STORAGE_WRITES`, and a read-only session still refuses every write with `SUPPORT_READ_ONLY`.

  `supportStorage` takes `adminSql` and `session` for writes. `attachments.put` no longer creates a signed upload URL before it uploads, so it works in a writable support session.
- The tags module's `untag` qualifies its columns, so its delete no longer fails with "column reference `tag_id` is ambiguous" against the function's own `tag_id` parameter, on the polymorphic `taggings` table and on adopted link tables.
- Add the `templates` SQL module and the `better-supabase/blocks/templates` block: typed, versioned templates that create records. `createTemplates({ types })` takes a Standard Schema for each type's settings and body and an `apply` that creates the record. `apply(templateId, { into })` uses the newest published version, records which version made which record (`template.applied`), and starts the workflow-builder workflows attached to the template on the new record. System starters are kept by key with `sync(starters)`, and `fork(source, organizationId)` copies one into an organization with `forkedFrom`, `forkedFromVersion` and a `behind` flag. Each organization, type and language has at most one default.

  `renderMergeFields(text, values, { escape })` and `defineMergeFields(subjectType, fields)` render `{{ field }}` placeholders with no dependencies, and `composeTemplateEmail` renders an email template for the notifications email channel. The `templates.apply` workflow step (`TEMPLATES_APPLY_STEP`, `templatesApplyStep`) creates recurring records from a scheduled workflow.

  Workflow-builder versions can store an input JSON Schema: `versions.save(definition, graph, { inputSchema })` sets `input_schema`, which `WorkflowVersion.inputSchema` returns and a fork copies. `save_workflow_draft` takes a third `input_schema` argument. Templates check the inputs of the workflows they attach against it.
- Time tracking and orders ship metric definitions. Time entries get a generated `seconds` column and carry `startedAt` in their events; `timeTrackingMetrics()` sums tracked seconds per start day, and `timeEntryMetricTime` buckets entry events by that day. `orderMetrics({ currency })` defines orders placed, revenue and average order value, with `ORDER_METRIC_EVENTS` for the sink. `metricsSink` takes a `time` option and `createBlocks` a `metricTime` option for events whose rows sit at another time.
- Add the `time-tracking` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/time-tracking`: timers and logged time on any record, with one running timer per member and no overlapping entries enforced by an exclusion constraint. A member submits a week as a timesheet that goes through the approvals module; the week is locked while the request is pending and once it is approved, and `reopen` unlocks it. `toWeeklyGrid` turns a week's entries into rows per record and day for a weekly data grid.
- New `better-supabase/blocks/undo` applies the undo tokens that `relations.link`, `relations.unlink` and `audit.revert` return, checking that every call is an accepted inverse, and `better-supabase/blocks/undo/react` adds `useUndoStack`, a stack of tokens for a toast's Undo button or a Cmd+Z handler.
- Subject events share one shape, and watchers can follow them.

  - Every subject event (`comment.*`, `subject.tagged`, `subject.untagged`, `status.changed`, `field_value.set`, `share_link.created`, `subject.viewed`, `reminder.sent`) carries `organizationId`, `subjectType` and `subjectId`, typed as `SubjectEventData` from `better-supabase/events`.
  - `watcherHandler(notifications, { types })` from `better-supabase/blocks/notifications` turns outbox rows (`outbox.consume`) into notifications for the subject's watchers, keyed by the event id, and leaves out the actor the row records. `watcherSink` does the same for CloudEvents, leaving out an actor the event data names.
  - `status.changed` now carries the subject's tenant, the previous status in `from` and the `reason`. The table trigger records it, so a direct update or `rank.move()` into a new status emits it too, and an update that keeps the status emits nothing.
  - `transition()` clears its reason when it returns, so a later direct update in the same transaction no longer writes that reason to the history row.
  - `reminder.sent` now carries `subjectType`, `subjectId` and `userId`.
  - `mark_reminder_sent` no longer fails with an ambiguous `occurrence_at` reference, and a second delivery of the same occurrence returns `false` and records no second event.
  - `reminders.deliver(payload)` and `reminders.job` deliver one occurrence. With `reminderNotification`, `createBlocks` wires them to the `notifications` block (`REMINDER_DELIVERY_QUEUE` names the queue).
- Outgoing webhooks write each endpoint's `format` with a versioned `WebhookEncoder`. `cloudEvents()` stays the default; `otlpLogs()` sends OTLP/HTTP JSON logs to a collector and `splunkHec()` sends Splunk HTTP Event Collector events. An endpoint's `credential_ref` adds the token from the new `credentials` option, and an encoder's `classify` reads responses such as an OTLP partial success or an HEC error code inside a 200.
- Outgoing webhooks pick the body per endpoint. The new `standard` format (`standardEnvelope()`) writes the 0.8 body, `{ type, timestamp, data }` or an array of them for a batch endpoint, signed per Standard Webhooks like `cloudevents`. `sql upgrade` from 0.8 gives existing endpoints `format = 'standard'`, so their receivers keep working, and new endpoints get `blocks.webhooks-out.options.defaultFormat` (default `cloudevents`), which also covers an adopted table that doesn't map `format` or holds `null`. Set an endpoint's `format` to `cloudevents` once its receiver reads CloudEvents.
- Breaking: outgoing webhooks send a CloudEvents 1.0 event in structured mode (`content-type: application/cloudevents+json`) instead of `{ type, timestamp, data }`, and `transform` now returns the event's `data`. The event's `id` is the delivery id; `source`, `subject`, `time`, `dataschema` and `traceparent` come from the new `source`, `appUrl`, `typePrefix`, `dataschema` and `traceparent` options and from `publish({ subject })`. An endpoint with `batch = true` gets a CloudEvents batch (`application/cloudevents-batch+json`) per delivery run.

  Breaking: `publish_webhook_event` is now `(event_type, payload, tenant, event_id, subject, read_permission)`, and the old four-argument function is dropped. It queues an event only for endpoints whose owner may read it: a user owner needs `read_permission` (else the module's view permission) through the authorization provider or the access contract, and a service endpoint (`created_by` null) needs it in its new `permissions` column. User endpoints get nothing when neither the provider nor the access contract is installed. `createWebhooks` and `createOutbox` take `subscribers` (a `SubscriberPolicy`) and fall back to `authorization.delivery.subscribers`; deliveries it leaves out are canceled, and outbox events it leaves out are counted as `refused`. Adopted webhook tables keep working: the new `permissions`, `batch` and `subject` columns are used only when mapped. Rerun `better-supabase sql sync` to install version 3 of the `webhooks-out` module.

  Breaking: Problem Details for `invalid_request`, `unauthorized`, `forbidden`, `not_found`, `conflict`, `rate_limited` and `unexpected` at their default status use `"type": "about:blank"` and the HTTP status phrase as the title. Other kinds keep `https://bettersupabase.com/problems/<kind>`. `better-supabase codemod 0.9` does not rewrite these strings, so update clients that compare on `type` or `title`.

  | Old title           | New title               |
  | ------------------- | ----------------------- |
  | `Invalid request`   | `Bad Request`           |
  | `Not found`         | `Not Found`             |
  | `Too many requests` | `Too Many Requests`     |
  | `Unexpected error`  | `Internal Server Error` |

  `verifyWebhook` and `verifyStripeWebhook` take a `seen(id, until)` hook and refuse a replayed message id with a `conflict` error (`WEBHOOK_REPLAYED`); a throwing hook gives `WEBHOOK_DEDUPE_FAILED`. A malformed `whsec_` secret now fails with `WEBHOOK_INVALID_SECRET` instead of throwing.
- The `work-items` SQL module and `better-supabase/blocks/work-items` add issues and tasks on pipelines. Installing work-items binds the `work_item` type in the pipelines module with one pipeline per team, Linear's categories (triage, backlog, unstarted, started, completed, canceled) and the stage's category as the item's `state`. Items have an identifier, a parent the table keeps free of cycles, several assignees (capped by `maxAssignees`), a priority from 0 to 4, an estimate, progress, start, end and due dates, a lock that keeps the dates, and optional projects and iterations. `update(changes)` applies grid edits and Gantt drags one by one, closing stamps `closedAt` and a close reason, `assign` notifies new assignees and makes each watch the item, and `tree` rolls progress up for a Gantt group.
- A workflow-builder `condition` node can test a filter tree: `config: { filter, fields }`, where `filter` is a `FilterQuery` (the format of list filters and the rules block) and each field's `id` is the path it reads under `input`, `results` or `subject`. The single-path form (`{ path, op, value }`) keeps working. Event triggers take the same shape in `config.when`, over `type`, `tenant` and `payload...`: `triggers.onEvent` skips a trigger whose `when` doesn't match, and `triggers.save` refuses one it can't use with `invalid_input`. New exports: `filterConditionOf`, `matchesFilter`, `triggerMatches` and the types `WorkflowFilterCondition`, `WorkflowFilterField`, `WorkflowNodeCondition` and `TriggerEvent`. `conditionOf` now returns a `WorkflowNodeCondition`.
- The workflow builder adds record-bound runs, test runs, system workflows and restricted definitions. A definition lists `subjectTypes`, and `run({ subject })` starts it on a record through `workflow_subject_start_target`, which checks the type and `can_subject`; event triggers attach the event's record and conditions read a `subject` root. `test({ definition, stubs })` runs the draft with stubbed steps, skipped sleeps and stubbed approvals, filling missing stubs from each library step's `outputSchema` example. A graph may have several trigger nodes: a trigger picks one with `config.node`, and the run starts there. `scope: "system"` definitions belong to the app; organizations see them, `definitions.fork` copies one into the tenant (lists show `upstreamVersion` and `behind`), and `definitions.optOut` stops its runs there (new `workflow_opt_outs` table). `restricted: true` limits running and editing to `workflow.admin` and `definitions.grants` (new `workflow_definition_grants` table). Static executors spread `graphRunFields(meta)` and set `executorRunsTests` on `graphStarter`, which refuses test runs otherwise.

  Breaking: `save_workflow_definition`, `workflow_definitions_list`, `workflow_start_target` and `workflow_run_principal` take new arguments, `versions.runAs(version, tenant?)` takes the tenant for system definitions, and `validateGraph` no longer reports a second trigger node. Run `better-supabase sql sync` to install module version 2.
- Published workflow-builder versions now run with their publisher's rights. `publish_workflow_version` records `published_by`, `owner_kind` and the `permissions` the version's steps need, and refuses a publisher who lacks one (`WORKFLOW_PUBLISHER_FORBIDDEN`). With `createWorkflowBuilder({ authorization })`, `automation.mayPublish` also decides each `versions.publish(version, { claims })`.

  Breaking: each step must call `builder.versions.runAs(call.version)`, which fails with `WORKFLOW_PUBLISHER_REVOKED` once the publisher loses a permission or their membership, or when `automation.runAs` returns `null`. Versions published before this release have no recorded publisher and fail until they are published again. An organization-owned version, published by the service role, runs only as the service principal `runAs` returns. `GraphStepCall.version` and `GraphRunMeta.versionId` carry the version id to each step.

  Workflow nodes, MCP tools and inbox commands read the provider's operations table first: `workflow.node.<step>`, `mcp.tool.<name>` (through the new `createMcp({ operations })`) and `inbox.command.<name>`. Doctor reports `admin()` in a file that runs workflow-builder steps (BS333).
- `startOnEvent` and the schedule starts of `workflowStarter` take `runAs`: a publisher and the permission keys the run may use, or a function of the event or start call that returns them. The run starts as the publisher, capped to those keys, and the event's actor or the schedule's creator is recorded as `bs.trigger` for audit only. `WorkflowStartCall` gains `source`, and `WORKFLOW_PUBLISHER_FORBIDDEN` errors carry the missing keys as `permissions`, in the `DbError` and in Problem Details.

  Breaking: a schedule start without `runAs` throws, so a schedule no longer runs with the rights of the member who created it.
- Workflow runs can belong to a record. `workflow_runs` gains `subject_type`, `subject_id`, `principal_kind`, `principal_id`, `mode` (`live` or `test`) and `trigger_node`. `runs.list({ subject, mode })` filters by them, `admission.request({ subject })` queues a start for a record, outbox events carry `subjectType` and `subjectId`, and `startFor(ctx, workflow, args, { subject })` stores the record as the `bs.subject` attribute (`subjectAttribute` builds it). Test runs emit no outbox events and are left out of run lists by default.

  Breaking: `record_workflow_run`, `workflow_runs_list` and `request_workflow_start` take the new arguments, and `sql sync` drops the old signatures. Runs that `startOnEvent` and `workflowStarter` start with `runAs` now record the user who triggered them as `bs.actor` (and `actor_id`), not the publisher; the publisher is in `principal_kind` and `principal_id`, and `runAs` takes `principalKind`. `startFor` drops `bs.principal_kind` and `bs.principal` from caller attributes. Run `better-supabase sql sync` to install version 2 of the `workflows` and `workflow-sdk-world` modules.
- `better-supabase add <block>` is the documented way to install a block: it writes the module files like `sql add`, then wires the block's client into `lib/blocks.ts` and prints the glue command. `add` now takes `--tests-dir`, so it accepts every option `sql add` does. `sql add` stays as the low-level command that writes only the SQL files and the config entry. The header of generated module files, the doctor hints for grants, MFA and realtime triggers, and the Data API grant error now name `better-supabase add`. Re-run `better-supabase sql sync` to refresh the header in existing module files; it is a SQL comment, so the schema doesn't change.
- CLI fixes. `doctor --claims` without `--explain` exits with code 2 instead of being ignored. A failed database connection suggests `supabase start` only when a local host refused or dropped the connection, not for a remote host or a rejected password. `openapi emit --check`, `seed --check`, `skills install --check`, `scaffold api --check` and `spec check` print the diff of each stale file. `codemod --help` lists the 0.9 codemod.

  `bs.resource` in `better-supabase/hono` matches paths with the same rules as `resourceRouter`: a malformed id returns a 400 `invalid_input` problem, and an unknown path under the resource returns a 404 problem instead of Hono's plain-text 404.
- `clientIp(request)` reads the last `x-forwarded-for` hop, the one your own edge appended, instead of the first. A client controls the hops to the left, so it could send any IP as `Sb-Forwarded-For` to Auth on a refresh and dodge Auth's per-IP limit or spend another IP's. Behind more than one proxy, pass your own `clientIp` reader.
- `better-supabase config` no longer prints `source.accessToken`. Every string under a key that names a credential (`token`, `secret`, `password`, `apiKey`, `privateKey`) prints as `<redacted>`, and the password in any URL is replaced, in text and in `--json`.
- `better-supabase env` writes `.env.local` (or the `--out` file) with mode `0600`, and tightens an existing file's mode even when its contents didn't change. The file holds the secret key, the database URL and the JWT secret, and it used to be created readable by every user on the machine.
- Every CLI request to the Management API and GitHub now goes through one HTTP
  client. It sends a `better-supabase/<version>` user agent, times out after 30
  seconds, and logs each request under `--debug`. A request that gets no response
  fails with the new `network_error` code. A command that reads a hosted project
  without `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN` now fails with `auth_required` (exit code 3)
  instead of `missing_value`.
- `better-supabase introspect --snapshot <file>` now converts a saved snapshot without connecting to a database, and rejects `--snapshot` next to `--db-url-stdin` or `--project-ref` with a usage error.
- A missing positional (`preset decode`, `blocks view`, `sql add`, `sql print`) now fails with `input_required` and exit code 3, with the positional in `missing` and its values in `choices`, instead of a usage error. A bare group, or no command under `--json`, returns a `usage` envelope that lists the subcommands in `choices["<command>"]`, and `--agent` is checked before the root help prints.
- The CLI starts faster. The bin turns on Node's module compile cache and answers a bare `better-supabase --version` without loading the CLI.
- `better-supabase codemod` runs when the config file fails to load, so `codemod 0.9` can migrate a config that still sets `sql.modules`. It walks the directory that holds the discovered config file, or `--cwd` when there is none. Commands built with `defineCliCommand` opt in to the same behaviour with `runsWithInvalidConfig: true`.
- `better-supabase codemod 0.9` puts the moved `blocks` key in sorted position when the config's top-level keys are already sorted, so a config checked by a `sort-keys` lint rule stays valid. A config with unsorted keys still gets `blocks` right after `sql`.
- The comments block docs say that `options.notify: false` leaves following on, that `options.follow: false` turns it off, and that an adopted subscriptions table must accept the commented subject types while following is on.
- `encodeDesign` now throws a `DesignTooLongError` (still a `TypeError`, with the code's `length`) when a valid design is too long for a URL, so a caller can tell it apart from an invalid design. `better-supabase/preset` also exports `appReservedColumns(tenant)`, the columns every app table can get from its scope, and `isAppDefault(type, value)`, the default check `appTables` runs, so an editor can refuse a column name or a default before encoding.

  Create uses them: a key to `auth.users` gets `owner_id` instead of the reserved `user_id` (which made the design fail to encode with a misleading "too large for a link" message), the Default field says which defaults a type takes, and changing a column's type drops a default the new type can't take. A table can now reference itself (`parent_id`, `on delete set null`) and reference the same table twice (`owner_id`, `assignee_id`, or `<table>_2_id`). Create also starts without blocks, from "Draw your own tables". `/create/prompt.md` takes `&design=<code>`, and the AI instructions link to it with the design.
- Doctor BS330 no longer reports an adopted module's opt-in tables that `blocks.<name>.tables` does not map: the notifications `deliveryEvents` and `suppressions`, the audit `restricted` table and the invitations `platformInvitations`. The generated SQL uses those tables only when they are mapped, so the check now matches it and still reads a mapped table's columns. `ModuleTableSpec` gains `adoptOnlyWhenMapped` to mark such a table.
- Doctor's BS213 check no longer counts a subject's `deletedAt` column from `config.subjects` as a column that grants access. `can_subject` runs as the caller and only hides soft-deleted rows, so a user who may write that column could already read the row. A column the authorization provider lists in `decidingColumns` still counts.
- Doctor BS213 no longer flags a single-column primary key, or a tenant column that every write policy pins to `better_supabase.current_tenant_id()` without an `or`, because neither lets a caller grant itself access. A column the authorization provider lists in `decidingColumns` is still flagged.
- Doctor's BS321 check no longer reads `create extension` inside a string literal as a statement. The jobs module's error message that tells you to create `pg_cron` was reported as an extension no migration creates.
- Doctor BS208 no longer warns about temporary files written by better-supabase's own catalog reads. `introspect`, `gen` and `doctor` now start each query they send over a database connection with the comment `/* better-supabase */`, and BS208 leaves those statements out, so running `introspect` before `doctor --strict` on a large schema no longer fails the check.
- Doctor BS208 no longer warns when the only statements that spilled to temporary files ran as Supabase's service roles, such as PostgREST's schema cache query on a large schema. With `pg_stat_statements` installed, the top statements it lists are the app's own.
- `doctor` reports fewer false warnings on block schemas. BS206 no longer counts a policy that already calls a security definer helper as `(select helper())` with no arguments, such as `session_active()`. BS213 counts a tenant column pinned by `better_supabase.can('tenant', column, ...)` or `column in (select better_supabase.tenant_ids_with(...))` like one pinned to `current_tenant_id()`, and leaves out a block's soft-delete column that a write can set but never clear, such as `comments.deleted_at`. Blocks declare those columns with the new `SqlModule.hidingColumns`, and `better-supabase/sql` exports `moduleHidingColumns`.

  The generated app-tables pgTAP test now expects the `bs_session_active` policy on the tables that `blocks.sessions.options.policies` covers.
- `doctor` no longer reports BS204 for a view's tenant column, since a query on a view uses the indexes of its tables. BS213 now counts a tenant column that every write policy holds to `column in (select better_supabase.member_organization_ids())` as pinned, the same as `tenant_ids_with(...)`, so app tables that are subjects no longer warn.
- The drive module drops `drive_insert_node` before it recreates it, so moving drive from a managed table to an adopted one (or renaming the table) no longer fails with "cannot change return type of existing function".
- The Flags SDK adapter serves a `null` variant when the declaration's `defaultValue` is `null` or left out, and treats a default variant that is missing from the variants as a missing flag instead of `null`. `evaluateFlag` returns `found`, so callers can tell the two apart. A flag row with an unknown `type` always serves its fallback: the OpenFeature provider resolves it with `PARSE_ERROR` (or `GENERAL` when the `errorCodes` map has none), and `getProviderData` shows it as a hint in Flags Explorer.
- `better-supabase gen` writes the read-sets module again when `sql.dir` points outside the project, as in a monorepo whose apps share one Supabase stack. It failed with "is outside the project root" while `gen --check` passed.
- Fix `finish_import` in the `imports` SQL module: its `id` parameter clashed with the runs table's `id` column, so finishing a run raised "column reference is ambiguous".
- Three SQL modules pass `supabase db lint` again when more blocks are installed. `save_view` and `set_user_state` no longer fail with an ambiguous column reference, and the forms module reads an adopted custom field options table without a constant query that plpgsql_check rejects when the table is missing. The saved-views indexes are now named after their tables, so renaming `views` or `preferences` no longer collides with another install's index names. Run `better-supabase sql sync` and generate a migration.
- The HMAC and HS256 helpers type their imported keys from `crypto.subtle` instead of the global `CryptoKey`, so the published types check under `@types/node` 24 without the DOM lib.
- `bs.middleware()` from `better-supabase/orpc` now type-checks in `.use()` on a single contract procedure with a typed output, so a contract-first router can guard one procedure with `permission`, `roles` or `requireTenant`. The oRPC docs show the pattern and explain that the `authorizer` passed to `createOrpc` decides every permission.
- The optional `@supabase/mcp-server-supabase` peer now accepts 0.14 as well as 0.13 (`>=0.13.0 <0.15`). `supabaseMcp()` and `supabaseMcpHandler()` are tested against 0.14.0.
- The direct Postgres executor runs a counted read's rows and count queries in one transaction, so both see the same snapshot. On `createPostgres()` clients this also halves the round trips of `findMany({ count: "exact" })`.
- The direct Postgres executor looks a function up in `pg_proc` once per executor instead of on every call without generated metadata (block calls included), and looks it up again after an error that says the function changed. Arguments for `OUT` and `TABLE` parameters are dropped from the call, as PostgREST drops them.
- The teams module's `update_team` function qualifies its column references, so plpgsql_check no longer reports `name` as ambiguous and the update no longer fails at runtime.
- `move()` in the `rank` module now writes the group column as its own type, so a board grouped by a `uuid` stage id or an enum column moves rows instead of failing with `column ... is of type uuid but expression is of type text`. `sql sync` rewrites the rank module.
- The block manifests and the `ui-recipes` skill reference list 46 new UI recipes, from the CRM deal board and the quote builder to the drive explorer, the form builder and the webhook endpoints table. Each one points to working code in the Next.js example.
- `sql sync` no longer stops with `blocks.share-links.api: found no definition of share_ai_chat(uuid, text) to wrap` when `share-links` and `ai-chat` are installed together with `sql.api` set. The four ai-chat sharing functions in the share-links module now put `returns` on its own line, so the Data API wrappers are written for them. Re-run `better-supabase sql sync` and generate a migration; the function bodies don't change.
- The `better-supabase-api` skill now shows the files `better-supabase init` writes: the Expo native client is `src/lib/supabase/client.native.ts`, and each Edge Function creates `bs` in its own `server.ts`.
- `gen`, `doctor`, `seed` and `snapshot` now find a local stack that runs on the Supabase CLI's stack engine (`[experimental] stack = true`), which picks its ports per checkout. When no database URL is configured and `supabase/config.toml` sets no `[db] port`, they read `DB_URL` from `supabase status` before falling back to port 54322.
- Moving a time entry to another day now rolls up both days. `time_entry.updated` carries `previousStartedAt`, a metric's `time` function may return several times, and `metricsSink` rolls up one window per day they fall on.
- `toSession` refuses an invalid `act` claim on a hand-built `AuthState` the same way `resolveAuth` does: the session is `{ kind: "invalid", reason: "actor" }` with code `ACTOR_INVALID`, never the user without an actor. Before, a malformed agent level (a `session_id`, an empty tool name, `tools` that is not a list) read as the plain user there. The `ACTOR_INVALID` message now names `agent` among the accepted kinds.
- The `updated-at` block is now titled "Update timestamps" in the preset catalog, in place of "updated_at triggers", so it reads like the other block titles. The block's key stays `updated-at`, so configs and preset codes keep working.
- The outgoing webhooks block's default retry backoff uses equal jitter: each wait is at least half its step and at least a second. Full jitter could schedule a retry for the same instant as the failure, so a struggling receiver got hit again at once.
- The `workflow-sdk-world` SQL module follows `@workflow/world-postgres` 5.0.4: its DDL adds the `seq_in_band` column to `workflow.workflow_event_slots`, from the `0026_add_seq_in_band` migration.

## 0.8.0 (2026-10-12)

- New `better-supabase/arazzo` subpath: `createArazzo` and `arazzoFormat` render Arazzo 1.0 and 1.1 workflows from the API model, with source descriptions that point at the OpenAPI and AsyncAPI files. `defineWorkflow` types each step by the `operationId` it calls, and `paginationWorkflows`, `createThenGetWorkflows` and `authWorkflow` build common workflows from your resources' operations.
- New `better-supabase/asyncapi` subpath: `createAsyncApi` and `asyncapiFormat` render AsyncAPI 3.0 and 3.1 from the same API model as OpenAPI. The document describes Realtime table changes and broadcast topics on the websocket server, CloudEvents with a header trait (`CLOUD_EVENT_TRAIT`) and a `dataschema`, outgoing webhooks, and the events that resource actions emit.
- `audit_event` and `audit_event_trusted` take `support_session`, `impersonated_by` and `impersonation_reason`, stored ahead of the values in the caller's `act` claim. The service role may pass any values; another caller only itself as `impersonated_by`, from a token without an `act` claim. `sql sync` drops the previous signatures. Module actions pass them through `ModuleAudit.supportSession`, `impersonatedBy` and `impersonationReason`, so the `support-sessions` module's `support.started` and `support.ended` entries now carry the session id, the admin and the reason in the impersonation columns instead of only in metadata. `AuditEventInput` gains `supportSessionId`, `impersonatedBy` and `impersonationReason`, and `auditEventOf` fills them for `support.*` events from `sessionId`, `adminId` and `reason`.
- `createServer` takes an `authorizer`: a runtime decision point (`apiVersion: 1`) that receives each request in the AuthZEN information model (subject, action, resource and context) and returns `granted`, `denied` or `approval-required`. better-supabase builds the subject from the verified request only (`subject.properties.claims` carries a read-only copy of the verified access-token claims, so an adapter can read memberships and roles without a database read) and refuses anything but `granted`. `defineAuthorizer` and its types (`Authorizer`, `AnyAuthorizer`, `AuthorizationRequest`, `AuthorizationOutcome`, `AuthorizationSubject`, `AuthorizationResource`, `PermissionRef`) are exported from `better-supabase/server`, and `better-supabase/testing` adds `testAuthorizer`, `staticAuthorizer` and the `testDocumentFormat` conformance kit.

  Routes, actions, `bs.require` and the guards in every adapter take a `permission` option that the authorizer checks. `defineResource` adds `hooks` that run around the repository call and `actions` (`defineAction`) on an item or a collection, served next to the collection and item routes by Hono `resource()`, edge `resources()` and the new Next.js `bs.resources()`, which returns the handlers for a catch-all route. MCP tools take a `permission` too, and `tools/list` hides the tools the authorizer denies.
- The CLI reads its options from `better-supabase.config.ts`, so `better-supabase gen` is the one command a project runs.

  - `gen` runs every generator the config sets up, in order: the typed client, `sql sync`, `spec emit`, the seed client, `scaffold api` and, when listed, `env`. `gen.tasks` or `--tasks` picks the list, `gen --check` checks them all, and `gen --watch` reruns the steps whose inputs changed. `gen.watchInterval` sets the watch interval.
  - New config keys give each repeated flag a default: `env.output` and `env.prefix`, `doctor.only`, `doctor.format` and `doctor.output`, `specs.manifest`, `scaffold.api`, `skills.agents` and `keys.output`. A flag still wins for one run.
  - `$env.<name>` and the `$development`, `$production`, `$test` and `$ci` blocks override the config per environment. The CLI applies the one named by the new global `--env` flag, then `$BETTER_SUPABASE_ENV`, then `ci` when `$CI` is set. `better-supabase config` shows the active environment.
  - `init` records the integrations it set up under `integrations`, and `add` without an argument sets up the listed ones whose files are missing. `scaffold api` and `add` print the config entry for the flags you passed.
- A `Db` carries Storage as the same caller: `db.$storage` is the new `storage` option of `connect(client, context, { storage })`, or the client's `storage`, and it throws on a connection with neither. `bucket.connect(db.$storage)` and blocks given `db.$storage` then follow whatever the `Db` runs as. In a support session, `ctx.db.$storage` and `ctx.sql.$storage` are `ctx.storage`, which is now typed `StorageApi`, so the same code reads Storage as the caller in a normal request and as the target in a support session.
- `dbTransport(db, { schema })` from `better-supabase/blocks` (and every block entry) calls block functions through a `Db`'s executor, so a block follows whatever the `Db` runs over: the Data API for `ctx.db` in a normal request, and direct Postgres as the target user in a support session or for an API key. It rejects with the raw database error like `rpcTransport` and `sqlTransport`, so block error mappers see the same codes and hints, and a `DbError` that a transport rejects with now passes through block calls unchanged.
- Table and column comments now reach the generated meta as `description` and `deprecated`, and the JSON Schema output marks deprecated tables and columns. `jsonSchema()` takes a `target` (`draft-2020-12`, `draft-07` or `openapi-3.0`, typed as `JsonSchemaTarget`). MCP tools take a `jsonSchemaTarget`, and `tools/list` converts tool schemas to draft-07 for clients on protocol revisions before 2025-11-25. `zod()` takes `flavor: "mini"` (typed as `ZodFlavor`) to generate Zod Mini schemas, and CloudEvents take a `dataschema` option that receives a `DataschemaContext`.
- New `better-supabase/overlay` subpath for OpenAPI Overlay 1.0, 1.1 and 1.2 documents. `applyOverlay` and `applyOverlays` apply them to any JSON document, with RFC 9535 JSONPath targets, a diagnostic for each target that matches nothing, and the 1.2 reusable actions (`expandReusableActions`). `defineOverlay` types an overlay in code, and `overlayFromDiff` writes the overlay that turns one document into another. JSONPath comes from the optional `jsonpath-rfc9535` peer, loaded on first use, or from an engine you pass. The subpath imports no Node built-in, so it runs on every WinterTC runtime.
- A cleanup of shared code inside the package, with a few fixes.

  - Fix `doctor` BS303 comparing generated files with a different line-ending rule than `gen`, fix `sql upgrade` ignoring the provider defaults for modules, and flush pending events in `finally` in the React Router, SvelteKit, Hono, Edge and oRPC adapters when a handler throws.
  - Bound the HS256 key cache and the verified-token secret ids, and strip the event type prefix the same way in every block.
  - Move the Standard Webhooks code to `core/signatures` and the HMAC helpers to `core/hmac`, share one optional-peer loader and one bounded TTL cache, and add `methodNotAllowed()` to `core/problem`. None of these paths are public exports.
  - Split `config/config.ts` into `config/types/*`, `expose.ts`, `json.ts`, `resolve.ts` and `defaults.ts`. `resolveConfig` now resolves `gen.watchInterval` (default 2000), the SQL registry and the CLI read their fallbacks from `CONFIG_DEFAULTS`, and `GenTask`, `IntegrationName` and `SkillsAgent` derive from the new `GEN_TASKS`, `INTEGRATION_NAMES` and `SKILLS_AGENTS` tuples, which `better-supabase/config` now exports.
  - Split `sql/registry.ts` into `module-types.ts`, `registry.ts`, `module-context.ts`, `render.ts`, `paths.ts`, `markers.ts`, `upgrade.ts`, `introspect.ts` and one file per inline module under `sql/modules`. A module declares `extras` for SQL that depends on the whole layout, replacing the name switch in the renderer, and the tenant and provider entitlement checks share one template.
  - Share one cron route guard (`core/cron-route`), one time budget (`budget`), one lease heartbeat, one `blockEmitter` and one `toBlockError` across the jobs, outbox, webhooks, notifications, organizations and billing blocks.
  - Split the jobs, billing, notifications, data-lifecycle and webhooks blocks into type, helper and per-factory files; the public subpaths are unchanged.
  - Split `server/resource`, `server/server`, `core/repository`, `core/define`, `next/create`, `testing/conformance`, `ai-sdk/mcp`, `ai-sdk/messages` and `workflow-sdk/world` into type, helper and per-kit files, and share one `isRecord` from `core/guards`.

  - Share the AI SDK HTTP helpers, add `withFlush` to flush events after a response, and run the React hooks on the same bindings as Vue, Solid and Svelte (`ClaimsOf` and `ProfileOf` now live in the bindings).
  - Build the MCP table tool schemas from the same page schemas as the OpenAPI document, so list pages describe their page counts, and share `integer` and `whereClause` between the Postgres and SQLite compilers.

  - Split server client construction into `server/clients.ts` (with an injectable `createClient`), `db.$search` into `core/db-search.ts`, the topic SQL into `realtime/topic-sql.ts` and the page helpers into `core/paginate.ts`. The incoming webhook endpoint now shares `hitRateLimit` and `receiveWebhook` with the rate limiter and the inbox, and `gen` builds the spec step with `specOptionsFromConfig`.
- `powersyncExecutor` takes a `fallback` executor. Reads that SQLite can't run (an include, full-text search, a function source) and `db.$rpc` go to the fallback, usually `postgrestExecutor(supabase)`; writes never fall back. `checkSqlite(betterSupabase, list, { all: true })` returns every unsupported feature a list uses instead of the first. `db.$rpc` on an executor without `rpc` now returns a `DbError` of kind `unsupported` (HTTP 501) instead of `invalid_request`. The PowerSync docs add a guide to porting a PostgREST list.
- New `better-supabase spec emit`, `spec check` and `spec validate` commands. `spec emit` renders every output in the new `specs` config key (format, version, output file, overlays, JSON or YAML, and an optional reference page), applies the overlays and caches renders by content hash. `spec check` fails when a file on disk is out of date, `spec validate` checks each document against the official JSON Schema for its version, and every run writes a diagnostics manifest. `--fail-on`, `--ui`, `--watch` and `--manifest` cover CI, reference pages and local work. `openapi emit --check` now compares text the same way `spec check` does.

  `better-supabase scaffold api` writes the resource routes and an OpenAPI route for Hono or Next.js: `lib/<name>.generated.ts` on every run and `lib/<name>.ts` once, for your own changes.

  The `openapi` config key is deprecated. It still works and maps onto `specs`; move it to `specs.outputs` with `format: "openapi"`.
- New `better-supabase/spec` subpath: `defineApi` builds one API model from the schema, resources, routes and plugin `describe` hooks, and renders it in any registered document format and version, with transforms, overlays and lint diagnostics. `defineDocumentFormat` adds a format of your own (`apiVersion: 1`), `serializeDocument` and `specResponse` serve a rendered document with a strong ETag, and `specReference` serves a reference page for Scalar, Swagger UI, Redoc, Stoplight Elements or RapiDoc, from jsDelivr at pinned versions with SRI hashes or from your own assets, with a CSP nonce.

  `better-supabase/openapi` now renders OpenAPI 3.0, 3.1 and 3.2 from that model, and `3.3-preview` when you ask for it. `createOpenApi` keeps its signature and takes a `version` option. It now emits OpenAPI 3.1.2 by default (it emitted 3.1.1), so a snapshot of the `openapi` field changes. 3.0 output lowers schemas to the 3.0 Schema Object, and the 3.2 fields travel as `x-oai-*` extensions below 3.2. `openapiFormat` lints a document for duplicate operation ids, path parameters that don't match the template, unknown security schemes and `$ref`s that don't resolve. The tenant, soft delete and timestamps plugins describe their columns and headers in the document.
- Add the `standardSchema()` generator. It writes `<output>.standard.ts` with a Standard Schema per table for its Row, Insert and Update shapes, built on the new `tableSchema()` runtime helper, so apps can validate writes without installing zod or valibot. Each schema also implements Standard JSON Schema for the `draft-2020-12`, `draft-07` and `openapi-3.0` targets, and the file exports `validators` for the validation plugin. `gen --metadata --emit standard-schema` prints it. Generators now receive `runtimeImport` in their input.
- `bucket.connect()` and `buckets.connectStored()` take any `StorageApi`, not only a supabase-js client: `StorageClient` is now `StorageApi | { storage: StorageApi }`, where `StorageApi` is an object whose `from(bucket)` returns a `StorageBucketApi`, the file methods of `supabase.storage.from(bucket)` typed structurally with the supabase-js `{ data, error }` results. A supabase-js client and its `storage` still type-check unchanged. `bucket.apply()`, `defineAnalyticsBucket().connect()` and `defineVectorBucket().connect()` call the admin API, so they take the new `StorageAdminClient` (a supabase-js client) instead.
- Supabase Lite support. `createServer(betterSupabase, { backend: "lite" })` verifies Lite's HS256 tokens locally against `SUPABASE_JWT_SECRET` (a new `env` setting, at least 32 characters), skips the JWKS prefetch, and with `liteDriver` set to `sqlite-postgres` or `sqlite` returns an `unsupported` error for `$rpc` without sending a request. `liteStack()` in `better-supabase/testing` runs Lite in the test process on in-memory SQLite or PGlite, applies migrations, schemas and seed SQL, and signs `asUser` tokens with the Lite secret. `gen` and `doctor` read a Lite project's `[db] driver`: the `postgres` driver's `[db] url`, or the migrations and declarative schemas replayed into an in-memory PGlite (`source.lite: false` turns this off). Doctor adds BS328 (`$rpc` on a Lite SQLite driver) and BS329 (Realtime or Edge Functions on Lite), and skips BS403 on Lite projects. `init --lite` prints Lite's install and CLI steps. `@supabase/lite` is an optional peer (`>=0.11.0 <0.12`).
- Connect agents to Supabase's MCP server and serve it from your app. `supabaseMcp()` in
